The cycle loop*), every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. ## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#The two roles, and why the verifier is blind

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RO1 | A developer leaf writes the failing test, the implementation, or the refactor. | The three developer phases are named, so a leaf's job is unambiguous. |
| RO2 | A developer leaf is allowed to see everything: the issue, the repository and the prior phases. | Nothing is withheld from the role that has to make the change. |
| RO3 | A verifier leaf confirms one developer claim. | One claim per verification keeps a verdict readable. |
| RO4 | A verifier leaf is blind: it receives only the test, the issue's behaviour spec and the repository, never the developer's rationale. | A verdict formed from the developer's reasoning confirms nothing. |
| RO5 | The verifier establishes ground truth by running the test itself. | The verdict rests on an observation, not on a report of one. |
| RO6 | Blindness is enforced structurally: the verifier prompt is built by `build-verify-prompt.sh`, none of whose inputs can carry the developer's rationale, so the developer's reasoning physically cannot reach it (ADR-0003). The verifier-leaf bullet states this as a property of every input the builder takes and names no closed list of those inputs; it may give the `refactor` phase's base sha (a hex commit name, #151) as an example of an input that carries no prose. The same bullet says no input may be added to the builder that could carry that rationale. | A rule the plumbing enforces cannot be forgotten under pressure, a closed list goes stale the day a flag is added, leaving a reader nothing to check the new flag against, and the rule that governs the next flag must be stated where that flag would be added. |
| RO7 | The conductor keeps the two roles distinct. | The separation is maintained by whoever orchestrates it. |
| RO8 | The conductor does not write the test or the implementation itself. | A conductor that writes code has no blind verifier for it. |
| RO9 | The conductor does not pass a developer leaf's explanation into a verification. | The one channel that would defeat blindness is closed by hand as well as by construction. |
| CY139 | "The two roles, and why the verifier is blind" says that verification is on unless the run's pinned `VERI_SKIP_VERIFY` names the phase, that the `plan` and `surround` phases are never skipped, and cites ADR-0033; the sentence is no longer than the one it replaces. | A reader of the blindness rule must meet its exceptions there, and SKILL.md has no bytes to spare under its ceiling. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#The scripts (this skill's `scripts/` directory)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SS1 | The scripts section says `$S` is pinned: a run launched through `$S/run-conductor.sh` executes the skill snapshot, the commit recorded in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skill-commit` and exported to every leaf as `VERI_SKILL_COMMIT`, not the clone's current tip, so a PR landing on `main` while the run works does not change the scripts and templates under a running conductor; it points to *The skill snapshot* in the repository's `README.md`, *Launching the conductor*. (The scripts section, second paragraph, first sentence.) | A conductor that read the clone's tip would change procedure mid-run. |
| SS2 | The same paragraph says every launch `supervise.sh` makes also writes the commit it ran under to the event log, `.tdd/events.jsonl`, under the key `skill_commit`, so a run that took a newer skill with `VERI_NEW_SKILL=1`, or on the first launch after `release-issue.sh` released it, shows as the sha changing partway down that file. (The scripts section, second paragraph, last sentence.) | It is how a reader tells a run that changed skill from one that did not. |
| SS3 | The scripts section says the catalogue of every script (arguments, output, exit codes) is `<base directory>/SCRIPTS.md`, and that, before calling a script whose arguments, output or exit codes the step at hand does not spell out, the reader reads that script's entry with `$S/script-help.sh <name>`, which prints just that entry, rather than reading or grepping the whole file. (The scripts section, last paragraph.) | SKILL.md carries no per-script entry, so the catalogue is where a call is checked. |
| SS4 | The scripts section's first paragraph says all leaf machinery lives under `$S` and is tested by pytest files under `$S/tests/py/`; it names no `.test.sh` suite. (The scripts section, first paragraph, last sentence.) | It is the first line about tests an agent reads in the procedure, and the bash suites it named are gone. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Launching a leaf — detach, then wait in the foreground

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EV183 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| LL19 | A conductor never puts a question to the user interactively, since no one answers a headless run and a turn that ends on a question leaves a run that looks in progress. Where the procedure itself says to ask the user — *Detecting the test command*'s exit-3 ask among them — that instruction is discharged by bailing with cause `needs-user`, a process failure, writing the question in full into the bail comment so a resume or the human can answer it. The conductor also never declines the procedure's next instruction. The paragraph stating the absolute names *Detecting the test command* itself, so a reader of this file alone knows it is discharged by the bail; a general rule the reader must apply to it is not enough. Preflight check 1's dirty-tree stop is not such an ask: it is a preflight refusal (`PREFLIGHT-FAILURES.md`), never a bail. | The absolute and the instruction to ask are the same rule once "ask" means "stop and record the question". A dirty tree found before the claim is no question for this run: a bail labels the issue `bailed` and needs a hand unlabel (#4505). |
| LL35 | The conductor never launches a leaf in a background Bash call and then waits for its notification: it often runs as a print-mode session (`claude -p "/tdd <n>"`), one of the two unattended modes described in `README.md`'s *Launching the conductor* — cited by that file and section name, never as a list "below" or "above" this one — and in print mode that notification never arrives — the turn ends, the `claude -p` process exits, and the leaf is orphaned, its result never read, its ledger never ticked, its outcome never narrated (#70). Detach-then-wait behaves identically interactively and unattended, so it is the only shape the procedure uses. | A reader of this file alone can find the modes, instead of looking below for a list that is not there. |
| LL1 | Every leaf launch has the same three-part shape: clear the completion file, launch detached, wait in the foreground. | One shape, so no launch invents its own failure modes. |
| LL2 | A developer leaf (red, green, refactor, revise) is launched straight through the supervisor: `rm -f .tdd/<name>.exit .tdd/<name>.pid`, then `VERI_TEST_CMD='<test-cmd>' VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_STEP="$(cat .tdd/issue-<n>.md \| $S/ledger-steps.sh current)" $S/detach-leaf.sh $S/supervise.sh <name> <cmd> [args...] > .tdd/<name>.out 2> .tdd/<name>.err`, then `$S/wait-leaf.sh <name>`. | The launch line is written down, so it is not reassembled from memory. |
| LL3 | The `rm -f` comes first because the completion file is named for the phase, not the cycle: a `.tdd/develop-red.exit` left by the previous step's red leaf would end the next wait instantly with the previous cycle's code, and a stale `.tdd/<name>.pid` naming a supervisor long gone would end it with a 2 before the new supervisor wrote its own. | A stale file answers for a leaf that never ran. |
| LL4 | The launch is detached, through `detach-leaf.sh`, so the call returns at once and the leaf runs in a session of its own. | The leaf outlives the call that started it. |
| LL5 | `wait-leaf.sh` runs in the foreground, blocks until the leaf finishes, and exits with its code. | The conductor waits where the harness can see it waiting. |
| LL6 | On `wait-leaf.sh` exit 3 the leaf is still running and only the wait's budget was spent: the conductor calls `wait-leaf.sh` again, as many times as it takes. | A spent wait is not a finished leaf. |
| LL7 | Every foreground `wait-leaf.sh` call is made with no max-seconds argument and the Bash tool's maximum `timeout` of 600000 ms; the 600 s foreground cap is the limit, so a wait costs one call per 590 s and no fewer. | The cap is the tool's, so paying less per call buys nothing. |
| LL8 | Only a 0, 1 or 2 from `wait-leaf.sh` is an answer. | Any other code means the wait, not the leaf, ran out. |
| LL9 | The leaf's report is read from `.tdd/<name>.out`, the captured stdout, not from the launcher's stdout, which the detached call no longer hands over. | Detaching moves the output, so the reader must follow it. |
| LL40 | The conductor reads a leaf's report with `$S/extract-result.sh < .tdd/<name>.out`, which prints the report alone, and never with `tail`, `head` or `cat` on the file. | The file is the leaf's stream-json: `tail -30` of one returned 20.8 KB where the report is 1.9 KB (#3771's `develop-red.out`), and every later conductor request re-reads it. |
| LL10 | A leaf's `BASH_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` follow its effective timeout — the one `supervise.sh` enforces (`VERI_TIMEOUT`, else the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout`, else 900 s) — unless the caller set them, so a foreground suite is not cut off at the Bash tool's default. | The leaf's own tool limits match the limit it is judged by. |
| LL11 | A leaf is never launched with `nohup … &` in place of the detach: `nohup` ignores a hang-up but leaves the leaf in the process group of the Bash call that launched it, so a harness that signals that group when the call ends takes the leaf with it; such a leaf writes no completion file, the wait returns 3 forever and is read as "still running", and the run stalls with nothing to say why. | The stall this prevents is silent, which is why the reason is written down. |
| LL12 | Before every leaf launch, developer or verifier, the conductor runs `$S/stop-requested.sh`, ahead of the `rm -f`. | A stop is noticed before a leaf is spent. |
| LL13 | `stop-requested.sh` exit 3 means no stop is pending: the leaf is launched as normal. | The common case costs one check. |
| LL14 | On exit 0 the conductor branches on the printed mode: `at-ledger` launches the leaf anyway, because every leaf between two ledger commits is on the way to the next one. | Stopping mid-step would throw away the step. |
| LL15 | Mode `at-leaf` or `now` launches nothing and goes to *Stopping*: the conductor `Read`s and runs `<base directory>/STOPPING.md`. | An urgent stop is obeyed before more work starts. |
| LL16 | On exit 2 the conductor treats it as a stop in mode `at-ledger`, reading `STOPPING.md` likewise, narrating that the request was unreadable: a `.tdd/stop` exists, so somebody meant to stop this run, and only how urgently is in doubt — the mode that cannot be wrong is the one that loses nothing. | An unreadable stop is still a stop, resolved the way that costs least. |
| LL17 | Before reading any leaf's exit 2 as killed-stuck, the conductor runs `$S/stop-requested.sh`. A `now` stop kills the live leaf, so its wait comes back 2 exactly as a hung leaf's does. A pending `at-leaf` or `now` stop makes `supervise.sh` refuse the launch with exit 2. Either pending mode explains the 2, and the run goes to *Stopping* instead of bailing. Any other answer leaves the 2 to be read as the phase reads it. | A stop must not be recorded as a failure, and a launch the supervisor refused for a stop is a stop. |
| LL18 | Whenever the procedure says **bail**, with the cause the bailing line names, the conductor `Read`s and runs `<base directory>/BAIL.md`. | One bail procedure, reached the same way from everywhere. |
| LL20 | The launch carries `VERI_TEST_CMD`, the run's test command, through the environment and not the prompt, so the verifier stays blind; it is a prefix on the launch line rather than a one-off `export`, because every Bash call is a fresh shell and nothing set in preflight survives to a launch three steps later. | Blindness is kept, and no state is assumed between calls. |
| LL21 | The launch carries `VERI_ISSUE` and `VERI_STEP` for the same reason: the supervisor's `launch` and `exit` events and the verifier's `verdict` event are written by `veri/event.py` in-process, which reads the run context out of the environment and can get it nowhere else — `leaf-role.sh` derives actor, phase and attempt from the leaf's name alone — so a launch that omits them leaves every event of that leaf with an empty `issue` and `step`. | The run log is only as good as the context each launch passes. |
| LL22 | The step's title reaches a command line as data, never hand-typed into it where a script can read it instead: double quotes stop an apostrophe but not an expansion, so a `$`, a backtick or a `"` in the title is substituted, run or cut short inside `"<STEP>"`, silently, and the run carries on with a title nobody wrote (#437). | A retyped title can execute, and does so without a sound. |
| LL23 | Every leaf launch reads `VERI_STEP` out of the committed ledger inside its own command substitution — `next-unchecked.sh` before the step's green commit ticks it, `next-pending-refactor.sh` after, `next-pending-refactor.sh \|\| next-unchecked.sh` where the phase could be either — and a verifier's launch reads `--step` the same way. | The title comes from the ledger at the moment it is used. |
| LL24 | The conductor's own lines read the title the same way: every `event.sh` mark's `VERI_STEP` and `anchor=`, every `preserve-attempt.sh` call, and the matcher handed to `tick-ledger.sh`, `clear-refactor-pending.sh` and `tick-issue-criterion.sh` to find the checkbox they tick are, once the step is ticked and cleared, the text of the last `done` record `ledger-steps.sh` prints. | One source for the title, for leaves and conductor alike. |
| LL25 | `tick-issue-criterion.sh` is the exception: it ticks the issue body, whose criteria are the issue's own words, so its matcher is criterion k's first line read out of the body's `## Acceptance criteria` — still data, and never retyped. | The issue's words come from the issue. |
| LL26 | Where a command still spells the title it is in double quotes, `"<STEP>"`, because a single-quoted `'<STEP>'` closes on the title's apostrophe — that shell's stderr becomes `'that shell'` followed by junk — and the line dies in the shell it is typed into before anything is launched (#431); a fragment of the title fails the same way one argument over, while inside double quotes an apostrophe is an ordinary character. | The quoting rule is stated once, with the failure it prevents. |
| LL27 | The other placeholders on those lines stay single-quoted: they are paths, commands and ids, and none carries the planning leaf's prose. | Only the prose needs the looser quoting. |
| LL28 | A verifier (`verify.sh`, which also goes through the supervisor) takes the same shape with one wrinkle: the verify verb is reached through `run-verdict.sh`, the verdict launcher, not typed into a `bash -c "…"` command string, because that string broke on a title carrying an apostrophe (#426); the launcher takes the title as one element of its argv and hands it on as one element of `verify.sh`'s, so no second shell parses it. | One shell fewer is one parser fewer to break. |
| LL29 | The verifier launch is `rm -f .tdd/verdict-<phase>.exit .tdd/verdict-<phase>.pid`, then `VERI_TEST_CMD='<test-cmd>' VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_STEP="$(…ledger-steps.sh current)" $S/detach-leaf.sh $S/supervise.sh verdict-<phase> $S/run-verdict.sh --branch-base <base> --step "$(…ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done)" <phase> … > .tdd/verify-<phase>.reason 2> .tdd/verify-<phase>.err`, then `$S/wait-leaf.sh verdict-<phase>`. | The verifier's launch line is written down too. |
| LL30 | The verifier leaf is named `verdict-<phase>`, never `verify-<phase>`, which the verifier leaf's own supervisor already owns. | Two leaves must not claim one name. |
| LL31 | `run-verdict.sh` execs the verify verb, so the code the supervisor sees is `verify.sh`'s own and the completion file it records is the verdict. | The launcher adds no exit code of its own. |
| LL32 | `wait-leaf.sh verdict-<phase>` returns the verdict: 0 PASS, 1 REJECT, 2 malformed or killed-stuck. | The verdict arrives as an exit code, not as prose. |
| LL33 | The reason is read back from `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason`, the file the revise step takes as a path. | The reason is a file, so the next leaf can be handed it. |
| LL34 | `wait-leaf.sh verdict-<phase>` also exits 2 when its supervisor died without recording a status — nothing wrote the completion file, so `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` is empty or left over from an earlier attempt — and the conductor reads that as a killed-stuck verifier exactly as *Develop (red)* reads a killed developer leaf: it bails cleanly (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`), does not retry, and does not quote the reason file for a verdict nothing wrote. | A leftover reason must never be read as this attempt's verdict. |
| LL36 | The conductor's own long commands, the baseline and full-suite `make test` included, are never backgrounded with the turn ended: they go through `detach-command.sh start`, then `detach-command.sh wait` in the foreground. | A command outlives the turn only if something waits for it. |
| LL37 | The conductor never ends its turn while anything it started is still running — a subagent, a background command or a background task — because ending the turn ends the process and kills it; it waits inside the turn (`detach-command.sh wait` for a command, the subagent's own result for a subagent), the mutation-parity pairs table included, which it builds inline or waits for inside the turn. | Ending the turn is a kill, so nothing is left outside it. |
| LL38 | In *Launching a leaf*, after the stop check LL17 names and before any phase reads a developer leaf's exit 2: when `.tdd/<name>.err` carries the supervisor's status line `supervise[<name>]: killed-external`, the conductor **bails** with cause `killed-external` whatever the phase, and does not relaunch the leaf; any other exit 2 is left to be read as the phase reads it. | An external kill is the environment's failure, not a stuck leaf, and naming it so is what lets a resume treat it as one (#2427). |
| LL39 | The rule "before reading any leaf's exit 2 as killed-stuck, run `$S/stop-requested.sh`" also covers an exit 2 from `$S/detach-command.sh wait` on the conductor's own command (the baseline or full-suite run): a pending `now` makes that wait return 2 within one poll, so with `now` pending the run goes to *Stopping* (`Read` and run `<base directory>/STOPPING.md`) and does not bail or refuse preflight. | `detach-command.sh wait` returns 2 under `now` exactly as it does for a command that died recording nothing, and only the stop request tells the two apart. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Inputs

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| IN1 | There is one command, so what the user typed after it decides which of the two procedures runs. | One entry point, and the argument is the whole of the routing. |
| IN2 | No shape of argument is ever a question to put back to the user: a word that is not a number is never a malformed issue number, and an empty argument is never a missing one. | Every input resolves, so a launch never stalls asking what was meant. |
| IN3 | The conductor reads the first word of `$ARGUMENTS` and resolves it three ways, with no fourth case. | The routing is total, so no argument falls through unhandled. |
| IN4 | An integer is an issue number `<n>`, and the invocation runs the single-issue procedure: it runs *Procedure* on that issue, end to end. | The common case is the direct one. |
| IN5 | Any other word is a scope to drain: the conductor `Read`s `SCOPE-LOOP.md`, beside this file at `<base directory>/SCOPE-LOOP.md`, and runs *Draining a scope — the loop* from it, which selects the issues in that scope and runs the single-issue procedure over them one at a time. | A scope is drained by repeating the single-issue procedure, not by a second one. |
| IN6 | The loop lives in its own file because only the scope branch ever executes it, and an integer argument takes *Procedure* and never comes back, so keeping it in SKILL.md would bill every single-issue conductor for procedure it will not read (#202). | A conductor pays only for the procedure it runs. |
| IN7 | No argument at all is a bare invocation, which is the scope `plain`, so it takes the scope branch with `plain` as its scope. | The bare case is a scope, not a fourth branch. |
| IN8 | A session born as a **worker** `<name>` takes none of those three: it `Read`s `WORKER.md`, beside this file at `<base directory>/WORKER.md`, and runs *The worker loop* from it, which works the issues the work queue assigns to `<name>` through the single-issue procedure. | A worker is assigned its issues rather than choosing them. |
| IN9 | `WORKER.md` lives in its own file for the same reason `SCOPE-LOOP.md` does: only a worker ever reads it. | The same cost rule, applied consistently. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#0. Preflight — baseline checks (bail before any cycle)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EV184 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| PL70 | Step 0 tells the conductor to run `$S/preflight.sh <n> <slug>` first, without reading `PREFLIGHT.md`, and to run it again on exit 3; that exit 0 is a whole preflight on a fresh run and on a resume alike, the claim included on a fresh run, whose values are its last lines; and that on any other exit it `Read`s `<base directory>/PREFLIGHT.md` and runs it from the step the script's last line names. It does not say that exit 2 means a resume. | Every session of #3243, #3244, #3793 and #3785 read all of `PREFLIGHT.md` before calling the script that makes the read needless on a clean run; since PRD 027.2 the script runs the resume path too, so exit 2 no longer tells a resume. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#1. Read the issue, enter the issue's worktree, capture the spec (or resume)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PL1 | Step 1 does not run `$S/stop-requested.sh --clear` itself: its worktree item enters the worktree and records the settled timeout, and leaves the stale-request clear to preflight. | A second clear here would sit away from the lock write it belongs beside. |
| PL3 | The blind spec file `.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` is written by `$S/capture-spec.sh <n>`, whose entry in `SCRIPTS.md` enumerates what it writes; the step names no bare `gh issue view` for it. | The verifier is blind, and the on-disk spec the script builds, never the conductor's narration, is what it sees. |
| PL4 | On a fresh run, step 1.6 claims the issue during preflight, right after the worktree is entered and before the baseline and the full-suite lock wait, so before any plan or leaf: it refuses (exit 2) an issue already carrying another lane's `taken:*` label (or its own lane's when a ledger `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` exists) or lacking the opt-in label, stopping the run, while a re-claim with no ledger accepts the run's own lane's `taken:<lane>` label and exits 0; otherwise it removes the opt-in label (`VERI_LABEL`) so `solvable-issues.sh` stops listing the issue, and adds the label `taken:<lane>`, `<lane>` being the text after `VERI_LABEL`'s last `:` or `main` when it has none. That is the run's first claim and the draft PR its durable claim, and a bailed or parked issue keeps `taken:<lane>` (#1656, #2030). | The issue list shows which issues are taken and by which lane, and a bailed or parked issue no longer looks like one nobody opted in. |
| PL37 | The spec is written with `mkdir -p .tdd && $S/capture-spec.sh <n> > .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md`, run inside the worktree, which the conductor is by then in. | Every later step of the run happens inside that worktree, so its `.tdd/` is the one the leaves read. |
| PL38 | `$S/ensure-run-state-ignored.sh` writes the rule that ignores `.tdd/*` except the ledger into two files, the worktree's committed `.gitignore` and `$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)/info/exclude`; when it prints `added`, `git add .gitignore && git commit -m "chore: ignore veri run state (#<n>)"` is the branch's first commit, and `already present` means there is nothing to commit. | Without the rule the atomic `git add -A` in Green would commit every leaf log and prompt, and `info/exclude` keeps `.tdd/wt/<n>` out of the caller's `git status`. |
| PL39 | If `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` already exists in the worktree, the run is a resume: it skips step 2, posts a `### ▶️ Resuming` comment naming the next unchecked step by running `$S/comment.sh <n> resuming`, goes straight to the cycle loop, and `Read`s `<base directory>/RESUME.md` and follows it. `issue-worktree.sh` is idempotent, so a second invocation puts the run back in the same worktree with its committed ledger already on disk. | The plan is already written, and a re-run needs no special casing. |
| PL40 | On a resume the issue's missing opt-in label is expected, since the claim removed it at the first launch, so the run never stops or asks about it; a resume skips the claim. | Stopping on the missing label would stop every resume. |
| PL41 | The worktree's lock is live only while its pid answers and that process's start time matches the one recorded on the lock's second line. `run_conductor.py` deletes the lock when the run it launched ends; a crash or a `kill` that skips that leaves a lock no live process matches, which frees the worktree, never the issue, which its PR goes on holding. An `issue-worktree.sh` exit 2 is a preflight-class stop: the script's diagnostic is reported verbatim and the run does not start, with no branch of its own, no cycle and nothing to revert. | A stale lock or a reused pid must never read as a live run (#2537), and a run killed before cleanup must still free its worktree without a cleanup step to forget. |
| PL52 | A human re-queues a claimed issue by restoring the opt-in label and removing `taken:<lane>` (#2030). | `solvable-issues.sh` never lists an issue carrying any `taken:*` label, so both edits are needed to make it takeable again. |
| EV185 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| SC78 | Step 1.5 (Resume check) says a resume skips verify-plan only when the ledger's plan is unchanged since its last verify-plan PASS; when a `plan:` commit is newer than that PASS, the resume runs step 2c's verify-plan before the cycle loop. | A resume that always skips verify-plan lets a hand-rewritten plan (#3056) reach the first step leaf unverified. |
| PL72 | *1. Read the issue, enter the issue's worktree, capture the spec (or resume)* says a resumed run reads `$S/park-decision.sh <n>` before its first leaf and applies a decision that changes the plan as a `plan: rewrite #<n>` commit. | The decision lives on GitHub, whoever made it; a ledger change outside a plan commit is an off-route bail. |
| PL263 | At its start, step 1 tells the conductor to run `$S/run-start.sh <n>` first (it has no exit 3, so no call again), to take sub-steps 2–5's values (the recorded timeout, the spec path, the `.gitignore` outcome, `run=` and `verify_plan=`) from its last lines on exit 0, and on any other exit to continue by step 1 from the sub-step its last line names; every sentence of step 1 stays. | Four fixed calls become one request (PRD 027.13), and the prose remains the path for everything not clean (PRD 027 D4). |

| ID | Requirement | Why |

| PL5 | Step 2.3 runs `$S/push-branch.sh` and then opens the draft PR as the first act after the plan commit, before the Plan comment: `draft=true`, a body whose run-authored part (marked as the run's own, the part `$S/pr-body.sh` rewrites) starts `Closes #<n>`, and a title that starts `WIP:` (ADR-0024). | An open PR saying `Closes #<n>` is the claim other machines can see, a draft that reads as finished work is misread, and marking the run's part is what lets a later write keep a section an operator appended. |
| DP218 | Step 2.3 wraps the draft PR's creation (its `gh api … pulls -F draft=true` call) in `event.sh stage-start stage=draft-pr` before it and `event.sh stage-end stage=draft-pr status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` after it, whatever its exit. | A prose stage is marked at its site, or its run time and failures go unrecorded. |
| PL6 | Each plan step's line ends with ` ⟦criterion k⟧`, where k is the 1-based position of the acceptance criterion it serves, and a gap step, one no criterion covers, carries none. | `criterion-finished.sh` reads that notation to decide when the issue's criterion may be ticked. |
| PL7 | A criterion whose only observable is that the repository's test command passes is not planned as a Red/Green step: it is written as one line, `- [ ] <criterion> ⟦full-suite⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧`, recorded as covered by the full-suite run (#481). | It has no failing test to write, so a Red leaf could only answer `UNTESTABLE` and the run would bail. |
| PL8 | A criterion whose deliverable is the record of a manual check that only a person can make is not planned as a Red/Green step and is never handed to a developer leaf: it is written as one line, `- [ ] <criterion> ⟦human-owed⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧`. A go-red guard (a guard broken on purpose, its named case watched going red, the code restored) is not such a check, and never carries `⟦human-owed⟧` (#481). | An agent can break a guard and watch it go red; six PRs on 2026-11-10 waited hours for a human on a check the review had already made. |
| PL266 | A go-red guard step is not run through the Red leaf: it is one line naming its probe, ``- [ ] <criterion>, probe `<file>` `<old>` → `<new>` on `<case>` ⟦go-red⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧``, which `run-step.sh` runs through `go-red-step.sh`: `mutation-probe.sh` on the existing case the step names (break, watch it go red, restore), the step ticked against that mutation record, so such a step never reaches `UNTESTABLE`. (The go-red paragraph.) | A go-red is a procedure on an existing case, not a new behaviour a Red leaf can pin; routing it through Red bails the run (#4432). |
| PL9 | A criterion that is a property of the branch's own diff (something removed, something left unchanged, a change confined to named lines) is written as one line, `- [ ] <criterion> ⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧`, judged against the diff by a blind verifier, and its step adds no test; a criterion stating a rule future changes must keep is still a Red/Green step (#727). | A test could only check a diff property by reading another suite's source text or the history, which pins text, not behaviour. |
| PL10 | A criterion that changes a unit with a requirements list is planned as that unit's `⟦skill-edit⟧` step (PL35); a criterion that changes prompt or skill text no requirements list claims is planned as a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step, never as Red/Green, judged blind against the diff (#1713, #2032). | No test can say whether reworded prose still means what it should: a graded unit is judged by its list, text no list claims by the blind diff verifier. |
| PL12 | When a criterion changes what existing cases outside the step's own Red pin — a script's output (a usage string, a constant, output compared verbatim or by checksum), the input those cases feed being made unacceptable, what a fake those cases use must return, or the calls or their order a fake records — the plan holds a step that updates those older cases, placed ahead of the step that makes the change (#1862, #2032, #3583, #3739, #3758, #3771). | Green may not edit a test, so without that step the older cases turn red and no leaf allowed to edit them is ever launched; four of PRD 029's six bails were of the kinds beyond output and passed the rule as it was written. |
| PL13 | A defect in a test's own fixture, stub or harness whose script under test is correct is planned as `- [ ] <criterion>: repairs the fixture '<fixture or stub name>' in <fixture file>, reproduced by a case in <test file> ⟦fixture-repair⟧ ⟦criterion k⟧`, naming the fixture by a function, stub or helper and never a line range, and `<fixture file>` must be a test file by the rule of transform-touches-tests; the mark is used for nothing else, a defect in the script under test being an ordinary step (#1021). | The mark lets Green edit only the named fixture, so it must not cover a defect in production code. |

| PL15 | A replacement that drives unchanged code passes against it by design: when its Red reports `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES`, the conductor `Read`s `<base directory>/PIN.md` and the step takes the pin path there, verify-pin and then a green leaf that deletes exactly the named cases, and Refactor and the ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark follow that deletion green as they follow any Green (#1397). | A passing Red must still end in the deletion the step exists for. |

| PL17 | Each step's text carries every constraint the issue attaches to the cases that step covers: the case size it must keep (a medium case, with a real process), "no production change", and any end-to-end path through real processes that must stay covered. | The leaves work from the step text and not from the issue. |

| PL61 | Step 1's planning rules state that a step making code produce a new outcome (a new rejection, error or empty result) that an existing caller does not yet handle is ordered after the step that adds that caller's handling, so no existing case turns red when the producer starts producing it (#946, #2607). | Ordered the other way, every producer step turns an existing consumer case red and its Green must add handling no test of that step requires. |

The same paragraph exempts a deliberate pin step (whose Red reports "already passes") and a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step, whose new case passes on the before-state by design. | A step green on the before-state has no failing test to write, so verify-pin rejects it as `unpinnable-missing`, the run bails, and the resume bails the same way on the same plan (#3691). |

Scope & plan* marks the plan as stage `plan`: `event.sh stage-start stage=plan` before the plan is written, and one `event.sh stage-end stage=plan status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` per stage start: `plan-publish.sh` writes it at its plan commit, and the conductor writes it only when something else ends the plan (a plan commit the conductor makes itself, a park, a stop, a bail), its status 0 at the plan commit, 1 at a park, 3 at a stop, else the code of the call that ended it; nothing after the plan commit ends the stage again. | A prose stage is marked at its site, or its run time and failures go unrecorded. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#2b. Start comment

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PL43 | The start comment announces that veri has started, naming the branch and the worktree it is running in, the number of plan steps, and that each phase will be blind-verified. | The thread shows where the run lives and what to expect of it. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#2c. Verify the plan (blind)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PL31 | The plan is verified once, blind, after the ledger is committed and before the first step, its inputs being the issue spec and the proposed ledger and nothing else. A REJECT (exit 1) rewrites the ledger from the reason alone, posts the revised plan to the thread, and re-verifies, up to `REVISE_BUDGET` times; if the last re-verification is still REJECT the run bails as a process failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`). | The plan is the run's definition of done, and a plan caught wrong after step 3 has already been paid for. |
| PL32 | A malformed or killed-stuck plan verification (exit 2) bails as a system failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`); a plan nobody looked at is never a PASS. | No verdict is not a verdict, and the plumbing broke, not the methodology. |
| PL33 | The verify-plan exit-2 branch bails after the stop check in *Launching a leaf*, the section where `$S/stop-requested.sh` is asked before a leaf's exit 2 is read as killed-stuck. | A pending `now` stop must go to Stopping and not to a bail. |
| PL44 | The plan verifier is launched with `VERI_TEST_CMD='<test-cmd>' VERI_ISSUE=<n> $S/detach-leaf.sh $S/supervise.sh verdict-plan $S/run-verdict.sh --branch-base <base> plan .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md .tdd/issue-<n>.md`, followed in the foreground by `$S/wait-leaf.sh verdict-plan`, which is called again on exit 3. | The third positional is the ledger, where the other phases pass a test path, and the plan has no step under judgement yet. |
| PL45 | The verdict's reason is read back out of `.tdd/verify-plan.reason` and posted by `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-plan` in a `### 🗺️ verify-plan — PASS\|REJECT` comment, even on a PASS, since it names any step the verifier judged too big for one cycle; on PASS (exit 0) the run continues to the cycle loop. | That observation must reach the thread whichever way the verdict went. |
| PL46 | A REJECT rewrite is committed with `git add .tdd/issue-<n>.md .tdd/issue-<n>-added.md && git commit -m "plan: rewrite #<n> after verify-plan REJECT (#<n>)"`. | The rewritten ledger is the run's new starting point, committed like the first. |
| EV187 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| PL253 | `$S/refresh-spec.sh <n> .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` runs right before verify-plan launches. Exit 0 and exit 3 go on; exit 2 goes on with the capture as it stands. | Split from PL57. A criterion re-scoped on the issue during a run reaches the next verdict, and an unreadable issue never stops a run. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#The step's cost check

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY1 | `$S/step-cost.sh '<STEP>'` follows every leaf before the green commit: develop-red, each verify-red, each revise attempt of red or green, develop-green, each verify-green. None follows a leaf after it, because the refactor leaf already has its own numeric budget (ADR-0017). | Without the check a budget is never weighed; after the green commit the step is ticked, so a check there could change nothing. |
| CY2 | The check runs only once the leaf has exited and `supervise.sh` has harvested its `usage` event. | Reading earlier gives a figure short of the real spend. |
| CY3 | `step-cost.sh` exit 3 (no leaf of the step has reported usage yet) is not a crossing: the step goes on and the next leaf is checked as usual. | Nothing has been spent, so there is nothing to weigh. |
| CY4 | The check fires at most once per step: after the crossing branch has run, later leaves are not checked and nothing re-arms it. | A step that costs 3x its budget is asked about once, not at every leaf. |
| CY5 | A crossing is not a failure, because the budget weighs cost and not whether the work is right; this is stated once, and no exit code changes meaning (1 stays process failure, 2 system failure, 3 nothing to do). | Without it a crossing could be read as a bail. |
| CY6 | On a crossing, `event.sh step-over-budget spent=<s> budget=<b>` is written once, before the `over-budget` leaf launches, on every crossing, before the split, park or carry-on branch. | A mark written only after the answer would record nothing for a crossing whose leaf never came back. |
| CY7 | The `over-budget` prompt is built with `build-develop-prompt.sh over-budget`, the leaf is launched via `detach-leaf.sh $S/supervise.sh develop-over-budget` and waited on in the foreground, and its answer is read with `exhausted-outcome.sh < .tdd/develop-over-budget.out`, never by eye. | Which outcome the leaf holds is an exit code (0 split, 1 decision for the user, 3 neither), not a per-run judgement. |
| CY8 | Exit 0 (split): run `git reset --hard HEAD` and `git clean -f -- <test-path>`, then one `add-ledger-step.sh instead-of` call with no `--criterion` (the split keeps its anchor's criterion), and commit the plan before the run goes on. | Otherwise the uncommitted work becomes the first replacement step's start, and a resumed run reads only the committed ledger. |
| CY9 | The exit-0 branch writes `step-added` and `step-split at=over-budget`; the `at=` value tells this split apart from a leaf-volunteered one (no `at=`) and from the revise-exhausted one (`at=exhausted`). | The run log shows which of the three causes grew the plan. |
| CY10 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 on the split bails with cause `step-too-big`, exit 2 with cause `step-add-refused`; the split shares the growth cap with the other two plan-growth causes. | Every cause of plan growth draws on one cap. |
| CY11 | Exit 1 (a decision for the user): park the step, stopping the run there carrying the leaf's answer and the two figures, leaving the plan as it is, and write `step-escalated at=over-budget`. | Parking ends the run without a bail; `at=` tells it apart from a verifier-driven park. |
| CY12 | Exit 3: carry on with the step. This is not a bail, unlike the revise-exhausted exit 3 with no step ahead, because nothing has failed, and the branch writes no mark of its own; the earlier `step-over-budget` records the crossing. | A converging step is not bailed for being expensive. |
| CY13 | A supervisor exit 2 on the `over-budget` leaf (`wait-leaf.sh` exits 2) is a `killed-stuck` bail, and the step's budget stays crossed. | Exit 2 means no answer could be obtained from the leaf. |
| CY63 | The `over-budget` prompt is built with `<spent> <step-budget>`, so the leaf is told both figures. | The leaf decides with the numbers in front of it. |
| CY64 | After the split commit the run loops back to `next-unchecked.sh`, which now hands over the first replacement step. | The run continues on the smaller steps. |
| CY65 | On the split, `step-added` and `step-split at=over-budget` are written before `add-ledger-step.sh`, because the marks read the step's title from the ledger. | The insertion rewrites the ledger the marks read. |
| EV188 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| DU1 | *The step's cost check*, exit 0 (split) bullet: this bullet states the replacement split in full, in its code block (`git reset --hard HEAD`, `git clean -f -- <test-path>`, the `step-added` and `step-split at=over-budget` marks, one `add-ledger-step.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md instead-of …` call and `git add -A && git commit -m "plan: replace <STEP summary> with <count> smaller steps (#<n>)"`), and it calls itself a copy of no other path's split. | This is the one statement of the replacement split; REVISE.md's exhausted split names it. |
| CY361 | At §3 *The step's cost check* (the over-budget split), an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused` and `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again. | Split from CY177. The site names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Before the Red of a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step — is the suite still red?

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SR3 | When `next-unchecked.sh` hands over a step carrying `⟦suite-repair⟧`, SKILL.md sends the run to `<base directory>/SUITE-REPAIR.md`, which it `Read`s then and not before, and runs before that step's Red; a run that never reaches such a step never reads it. | Only a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step needs the 3,299 bytes the conductor otherwise re-reads on every turn (#3152); a repair step run without the file would launch a Red leaf for a red that may be gone, or skip the flake check. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Red — one failing test (developer leaf), then verify-red (blind)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY66 | The step's cost is also checked after the `develop-replaces-report` correction leaf. | That leaf's spend counts toward the step too. |
| CY14 | The `red` template instructs the leaf to write **exactly one** failing test pinning the criterion to observable external behavior, with **no production code**, and to run the suite holding that case, not the full suite, against the unmodified code. The only question Red asks is whether that one case fails. | Running the full suite in Red would answer a question Red does not ask, and cost the whole run's time for it. |
| CY15 | The template's definition of a valid test is behavior, not mechanism: no process, fork or call count and no timing across a suite run (those are `make profile`'s job). The one exception is a call count the criterion itself names, pinned with an in-process spy on the one wrapper called, never a `PATH` shim counting real processes. | A count or timing assertion pins how the code is built, not what it does, and breaks on a faithful refactor. |
| CY16 | Before the develop-red leaf is launched, the conductor runs `rm -f .tdd/develop-red.exit .tdd/develop-red.pid .tdd/red-prediction.md .tdd/red-run-output.txt`. | A prediction or run-output file left by an earlier attempt would be judged as the new leaf's. |
| CY17 | If the `wait-leaf.sh develop-red` wait exits 2 (killed-stuck), the leaf hung or ran away: the conductor bails cleanly (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`), after the stop check in *Launching a leaf*, and does not retry. | A pending `now` stop must go to Stopping and not to a bail, and a hung leaf is not retried. |
| CY18 | When the leaf reports `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: UNTESTABLE`, the conductor skips verify-red and verify-pin entirely and bails immediately with cause `untestable-criterion`, carrying the leaf's `WHY:` as the reason. This is a **process** failure: the criterion as phrased cannot be tested as behavior, not that the plumbing broke. | No test is on disk, so a verifier would only return a malformed-verdict `REJECT` for the wrong reason. |
| CY19 | On `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: FAILS`, before any verifier, the conductor runs `$S/red-divergence.sh .tdd/red-prediction.md .tdd/red-run-output.txt < .tdd/develop-red.out > .tdd/verify-red.reason; echo $?` and branches on its exit code. | Verify-red is blind, so the prediction check is the conductor's gate: no verifier can be told the developer's reasoning. |
| CY20 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 1 means the prediction file is missing or newer than the run's output. The conductor narrates "prediction not written before the run" in the `### 🔴 Red` comment, passed to `comment.sh red` through `--note-file`, not a divergence, and writes that line to `.tdd/verify-red.reason`. It still spends the attempt like a `REJECT`: it skips verify-red and verify-pin and enters the revise loop for red (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md` when a verifier answers `REJECT`). | A prediction written after the run is not a prediction, but it must not be called a divergence either. |
| CY21 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 0 (the test failed for a reason other than the one it was written to catch) or exit 2 (the report carries no readable `PREDICTION:` line; an unreadable prediction is not a matched one) skips verify-red and verify-pin. The conductor keeps the reason the script wrote to `.tdd/verify-red.reason` (on exit 2 it writes its own one-line reason there), marks `$S/event.sh prediction-diverged`, and enters the revise loop for red exactly as a verify-red `REJECT` does (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md`). The revise leaf answers `.tdd/verify-red.reason`, and the attempt spends `REVISE_BUDGET` like any other. | Beck stops on that surprise because the test is then the suspect; the divergence has to reach the run log and the revise leaf. |
| CY22 | Beside the `prediction-diverged` mark the conductor runs `$S/preserve-attempt.sh <n> "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" develop-red 1 prediction-diverged`, so the tree the surprising test came from survives the next leaf that overwrites it. It is best-effort: if it fails, the conductor notes it and carries on. | The revise leaf overwrites the tree the surprising test came from, and the attempt would be lost. |
| CY23 | The `### 🔴 Red` comment, posted by running `$S/comment.sh <n> red --note-file <path>`, carries the test, the failing output, the leaf's `PREDICTED_FAILURE:` and whether the prediction matched or diverged, with its `PREDICTION_NOTE:` when it diverged. Both halves, always. | The prediction is what the failing output is read against; a thread carrying only one of them cannot be read for divergence. |
| CY24 | Once verify-red PASSes, the conductor records the step's test files with `$S/tests-frozen.sh record --step "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" --red-case '<red-test-id>' <test-path>… > .tdd/verify-red.reason`, captures `rc=$?` and stops on a non-zero status before writing the comment or launching any leaf: exit 1 (the step's own Red case is gone) is a verify-red REJECT that spends `REVISE_BUDGET`, exit 2 is a bail. | Run #3771 launched a develop-green leaf after a refused record because nothing in the chained call stopped on rc != 0; the 66 s of green was discarded unjudged. |
| CY25 | With `--step "<step text>"` in place of a record file, the record file is the one `$S/tests-frozen.sh path "<step text>"` prints, `.tdd/tests-frozen-<k>.record` with `<k>` the first 16 hex digits of the step text's SHA-256: a step text is often longer than a filename may be and may hold a `/`, so the text itself is never the filename. | One definition of the record's name, shared by every record and check. |
| CY26 | A step marked `⟦suite-repair⟧` or `⟦ci-repair⟧` is never frozen: for it `record --step` writes nothing and `check --step` exits 0, because its fix is often an edit to the very test file the full run or CI left red. A `⟦ci-repair⟧` step therefore records nothing before its green, and its green may edit the test the CI job failed. | Freezing the test would forbid the very edit that repairs the step. |
| CY27 | On `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` the criterion is already satisfied: the test is never sent to verify-red, whose verdict on a passing test can only be `REJECT`. The conductor runs the pin check instead and continues at the pin, `Read`ing `<base directory>/PIN.md` when a Red reports `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES`. | A passing test cannot be judged as a failing one, and a faithful test would be rejected for it. |
| CY28 | A `⟦ci-repair⟧` step is the one exception to `PASSES`: its test failed on CI and may pass here, so on `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` it goes on to Green with the failing CI job's log (test id and assertion diff) as its red evidence, never to verify-pin and never to the `test: pin` tick-and-commit. | A CI failure that does not reproduce locally is still real evidence; it must not be pinned away as already satisfied. |
| CY29 | On verify-red PASS (exit 0) the step continues to Green. On `REJECT` (exit 1) the conductor enters the revise loop (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md`), which returns either PASS (continue to Green) or budget-exhausted (**bail**, a process failure). | The two outcomes of the red verdict go to different places, and an exhausted budget ends the run. |
| CY30 | On a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step, once the Red report is written and before verify-red launches, the conductor runs `$S/unreplaced-cases.sh "<step line>" .tdd/develop-red.out` against the current attempt's report (`.tdd/revise-red-<k>.out` after a red revise). | The check has to see the report that is current, or a corrected revise would be judged on the old one. |
| CY31 | `unreplaced-cases.sh` exit 1 prints each named case with no `REPLACES:` line, and the Red gets one chance to correct its report. The conductor launches one `develop-replaces-report` leaf (prompt built with `build-develop-prompt.sh replaces-report`, launched through `supervise.sh`), spending no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt and writing no `revise` mark. It then runs `$S/unreplaced-cases.sh` again on both the Red report and `.tdd/develop-replaces-report.out` (`.tdd/revise-red-<k>.out` in place of `.tdd/develop-red.out` after a red revise), so a case the Red report already replaced still counts and a correction naming exactly the missing cases does not bail, before verify-red launches. | A missing line in a report is a paperwork gap, not a rejected test, so it costs no revise attempt. |
| CY32 | A second exit 1 (on the corrected report) bails with cause=unreplaced-cases, naming each case it printed, and launches no second correction leaf and no verifier. Verify-green never runs on a step whose named cases lack a replacement. | One correction is the whole allowance; a case with no replacement would be deleted unreplaced. |
| CY33 | The first `unreplaced-cases.sh` exit 2 (no answer could be obtained: no readable report, bad arguments) keeps its stderr in `.tdd/unreplaced-cases.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-unreplaced-cases` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before verify-red (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`). `route replaces-report` launches the one `develop-replaces-report` leaf and runs the second check, exactly as a first-check exit 1 does; every other outcome, `retry` included, or a spent budget bails with cause=unreplaced-cases, calling `unreplaced-cases.sh` no second time and launching no correction leaf and no verifier. (§3 *Red*, sub-step 2.) | A guard that cannot answer is not a pass, and the only way past it is the route the site already defines. |
| CY34 | If the `develop-replaces-report` wait exits 1 or 2 (a killed-stuck correction leaf included), the conductor bails with cause=replaces-report-failed and launches no verify-red. | A correction leaf that failed leaves the report still missing its cases. |
| CY67 | The template also requires a valid test to be lightweight (~100ms, costly dependencies replaced unless the behavior under test IS the dependency) and self-contained (never runs another suite, whole or sliced, and never re-runs its own). | A slow or nested test makes every later cycle pay for it. |
| CY68 | A criterion that cannot be pinned within those rules is not weakened into one that can: the leaf reports it untestable (`AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: UNTESTABLE`) instead, rather than writing a test to force a pass. | A weakened test would pass for the wrong criterion (#331: "at most N python3" was satisfiable only by a shim that re-ran whole suites under a counter). |
| CY70 | The leaf's report is `.tdd/develop-red.out`, and ends with the lines the conductor branches on: `TEST_PATH:`, `TARGET_TEST:`, `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: FAILS \| PASSES \| UNTESTABLE`, `WHY:`, `FAIL_OUTPUT:`. The test path and target test id come from the first two, and the third decides the next sub-step; the file recounts no past run to explain why `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED` exists. | A criterion earlier work already satisfies cannot yield a failing test, and that fact has to reach the conductor as data (#30, step 3); the account lives here, not in `SKILL.md`, which is at its byte ceiling. |
| CY221 | *Red* runs `$S/red-setup.sh record <n>` on the Red report once after verify-red's PASS; on exit 0, 2 or 3 alike it goes on to Green, the Red not redone (so no exit of it bails, revises or relaunches the Red). | Only a verified Red's case counts, and the count must outlive the step's `.tdd/develop-red.out`; a missing count is a lost design signal, never a gate (PRD 032, TY4, TY9, C14). |
| CY71 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 3 (`PREDICTION: MATCHED`) prints nothing, and the conductor carries on with the verifier: `rm -f .tdd/verdict-red.exit .tdd/verdict-red.pid`, then `detach-leaf.sh $S/supervise.sh verdict-red $S/run-verdict.sh --step "<step>" --fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>' red .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md <test-path> <target-test-id>`, its stdout (the reason) redirected to `.tdd/verify-red.reason`, waited on in the foreground with `$S/wait-leaf.sh verdict-red` (exit 3: call it again). | The revise step takes the reason as a path, not as text the conductor retypes. verify-red asks the question verify-green will ask, so it never passes a red that no green could make pass. |
| CY72 | The wait on a verifier returns the verdict (0 PASS, 1 REJECT, 2 malformed or killed-stuck), not the supervisor's status. The conductor notes the wait's exit code and reads the reason back out of `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason`, a file named for the verifier that wrote it (`red`, `pin`, `green`, `refactor`) and overwritten by each verification of that phase. | The newest objection is the only one a revise leaf should answer. |
| CY74 | A step whose trailing marker is `⟦fixture-repair⟧` is frozen like any other, except for the one fixture file its text names (`repairs the fixture '<name>' in <path>,`): `check --step` exits 1 naming any other changed test file. | A fixture repair may edit its fixture, but not any other test. |
| CY75 | A verify-red verdict of exit 2 (malformed or killed-stuck) is a **bail** as a system failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`), not a clean process stop. | CY76 | The `### 🔴 Red` comment is followed by a comment posted by running `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-red`, `### 🔎 verify-red — PASS\|REJECT` comment carrying the verifier's reason. | The thread shows what the blind verifier said, next to the test it judged. |
| FZ1 | Before each verify-red and verify-green launch, the conductor checks every earlier step's frozen record in the ledger, not only the current step's; a changed or deleted case of an earlier step is a REJECT naming the case and its step, except a case the current step's route names (`red-contradicted`, `red-supersede`, `repair`, `⟦replaces-case⟧`, `⟦fixture-repair⟧`). A case renamed with its text otherwise unchanged is neither changed nor deleted; SKILL.md's "Before each verify-red launch" paragraph says so in the sentence that says what exit 1 prints. | A verified case stays as verified for the rest of the run unless a route that edits it on purpose names it; a rename alone loses nothing, so the name can be corrected to say what the case asserts. |
| EV189 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| PL254 | `$S/refresh-spec.sh <n> .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md` runs before each step's develop-red prompt is built. Exit 0 and exit 3 go on, exit 0 being named in the step's `### 🔴 Red` comment as a refreshed spec, through `comment.sh red --note-file`. Exit 2 goes on with the capture and says so in that comment. | Split from PL57. A criterion re-scoped on the issue during a run reaches the next leaf, and an unreadable issue never stops a run. |
| PL58 | `refresh-spec.sh` exit 1, a live criteria count that differs from the capture's, bails with cause `needs-user`, naming both counts, before any leaf of the step launches. | A renumbered criterion list would point `⟦criterion k⟧` at the wrong checkbox, and only a person can re-map it. |
| CY132 | `<red-test-id>` is defined once, where it first appears, as `<test-path>::<case>`: the path of the test file and the case name joined by `::`. | `tests-frozen.sh record --red-case` exits 2 on a bare case name, and the conductor passed one in clarion2 runs #3 and #6. |

(§3 *Red*, sub-step 2.) | The cause's site names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| CY179 | That wait's exit 1 is never adjudicated: it bails `replaces-report-failed` as before. (§3 *Red*, sub-step 2.) | Only an exit 2 is eligible. |
| CY180 | The second check's exit 2 is never adjudicated: it bails with cause=unreplaced-cases at once, launching no second correction leaf and no verifier. (§3 *Red*, sub-step 2.) | One correction is the whole allowance. |
| CY408 | The Red section, in its opening paragraph, calls `$S/run-step.sh <n>` once for a cycle step, again on exit 3, in place of the separate `red-half.sh` line, saying it runs Red, Green and the Refactor loop to sub-step 6's PASS branch and ends on `step-close.sh`'s `next=` line; a `seam=<stage>` last line is where the prose takes over, from the sub-step that stage's `stopped at` line names, and `--hand <stage>` and `--from <stage>` are named as the way to keep a stage and to hand one back. | One call runs the cycle step and stops at a seam, so the conductor reads no per-stage prose on a clean step (PRD 038.2, RN11). |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Green — minimum implementation (developer leaf), then verify-green (blind)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY35 | The develop-green leaf's prompt is built from the `green` template: the **minimum** production code, no generality the test does not demand, the test itself untouched, and the fast command and the changed files' tests green. | The leaf is held to the smallest change that passes, and to the cheap tiers, not the full suite. |
| CY36 | The develop-green leaf is launched through the supervisor, and its exit 2 is a **bail**, taken after the stop check in *Launching a leaf*. | A pending `now` stop must go to Stopping and not to a bail. |
| CY37 | After the `develop-green` wait exits 0 and before verify-green is launched, the conductor runs `$S/suite-reported.sh develop-green`. | CY146 | Where step 3 lists the answers of `wrong-test-route.sh`, it names `prep <case> …` as an exit-0 answer given once the step's free reroute is spent, and tells the conductor to `Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md` for what to do on it. | A route the conductor is not told about is not followed; the procedure itself lives in `REVISE.md` because `SKILL.md` is at its size limit. |
| CY148 | *Green — minimum implementation…*, sub-step 2 (**Verify (blind)**), says that with `green` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no verify-green launches; it names the `verify-green-skipped reason=switched-off` event and `skipped` in `.tdd/verdict-green.exit`, and says the checks before the launch and `mutation-parity.sh` still run first, then sub-step 3's two comments are posted (`comment.sh <n> verify-green` renders `SKIPPED (switched off)`) and its PASS branch follows. | The skip must be in the procedure the conductor follows. |
| CY149 | *Green…*, sub-step 1, in the `wrong-test-route.sh` list, says that with `green` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` the routes read from the green report (`red`, `repair`, `supersede`, `supersede-diff`, `fixture`) and a `mutation-parity.sh` REJECT's `missing` still fire, and only a verify-green reason is never routed. | The routing rule must be in the procedure the conductor follows; a green reporting `WRONG_TEST:` implemented nothing, and the PASS branch would tick an unimplemented step. |
| CY151 | *Green…*, sub-step 1, says that with `red` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no reroute launches verify-red, the `missing` reroute included: the event and `skipped` are recorded as Red's **Switched off** branch does, and the reroute then goes on as on its own PASS (re-record the frozen tests, retry green). | The reroutes are a second site of the same verifier, and one word skips every launch of it. |
| CY153 | *Green…*, sub-step 1, in the `supersede-diff` entry, says that with `diff` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no `diff` verifier judges the `red-supersede` edit; it names the `verify-diff-skipped reason=switched-off` event and `skipped` in `.tdd/verdict-diff.exit`, and says the PASS branch (re-record the step's frozen tests, retry green) follows. | The skip must be in the procedure the conductor follows; it is the same verifier, and `⚠️ Unverified phases: diff` names it. |
| CY175 | A `suite-reported.sh develop-green` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/suite-reported.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-report-missing` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before verify-green (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `report-missing` with no verifier, no second `suite-reported.sh` call and no relaunch. (§3 *Green*, sub-step 1.) | The cause's site in the procedure names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| CY176 | A green that stops because the test looks wrong stays unadjudicated: it keeps its verify-green REJECT. (§3 *Green*, sub-step 1.) | It is not an exit-2 bail today. |
| CY362 | At §3 *Green* sub-step 1 (the leaf's split), an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused` and `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again. | Split from CY177. The site names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| CY404 | At §3 *Green* sub-step 1, the `fixture` route's insertion of its `⟦fixture-repair⟧` step keeps an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2's stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused` and `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again. It may say so by pointing at the split's exit 2 rule in the same sub-step. (The `fixture` route, at its `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2.) | Every site of the cause is judged, as #4000 made the split's; pointing frees the bytes the size ceiling owes. |
| CY292 | *Green*, beside the `SPLIT:` paragraph, says only that a green report carrying a `TIDY_FIRST:` line in place of `SPLIT:` lines is handled by `Read`ing `<base directory>/TIDY-FIRST.md`. | The procedure lives in its own file; `SKILL.md` is at its byte ceiling (PRD 032, TI8, C7). |
| CY409 | The Green section, in its opening paragraph, no longer carries its own `green-half.sh` call line, since `run-step.sh` runs it; the paragraph names `run-step.sh`'s `seam=green-half` stop as the point to take Green by hand, from the sub-step it names, else sub-step 1. | The four fused calls become one, so the Green paragraph stops duplicating the runner (PRD 038.2, RN11). |
| CY403 | The develop-green leaf and verify-green run the fast tier and the test files covering the files the step changed, never the `affected` tier or any broader one. | A leaf's suite runs were 52% of run #4316's wall time. |
| CY407 | The Green section, right after its opening paragraph about `run-step.sh`'s `seam=green-half` stop, holds one line ending the hand path (Green done by the conductor itself): `$S/seam-settle.sh <n> green-half test_path=<path>`. | `refactor-half.sh` reads `test_path` from a record only `green-half.sh` writes, falling back to red-half's only when none names the step; the hand path hands back through the one script that writes it in that format (PRD 038.1, SC9). |
| CY412 | Each verifier call, a `diff` one included, passes `--branch-base <recorded sha>`, the conductor's record. | A verifier that derives its own base can be steered by a leaf. |
| CY415 | The Green section keeps every `wrong-test-route.sh` route bullet, and its opening line names `run-step.sh`'s `seam=reroute` stop and `--hand reroute` as the hand path for the answers `reroute.sh` runs. | The prose stays the fallback (PRD 038.10 RRT12, S8). |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Refactor loop — one leaf applies the step's transforms, then verify-refactor (blind, once)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RF1 | A step's refactor is one leaf for the whole step, not one per transform: the prompt is built from the `refactor` template, which carries the budget to the leaf as the number of transforms it may apply, and the leaf is launched through the supervisor. | The cold `claude -p` start and the re-read of the spec and of the touched files are paid once for the step instead of once for every transform. |
| RF2 | A supervisor exit 2 on the refactor leaf is a **bail**, taken after the stop check in *Launching a leaf*. | A stop request pending when the leaf ended is honoured first, and only a leaf that gave no answer ends the run in a bail. |
| RF3 | After every transform the leaf runs only the tests of the files it touched, and reverts a transform that turns them red rather than leave a red suite. | Each transform lands on a tree whose touched files' tests were just seen green. |
| RF4 | The leaf commits its own transforms, one commit each, `git commit -m "refactor: <what> (#<n>)"`; the atomic tick-and-commit stays on green (ADR-0007), and only the refactor commit moved to the leaf. | A break stays attributable to one change, and the ledger tick stays one atomic commit with its code. |
| RF5 | The leaf's report is `.tdd/develop-refactor.out` and ends with `RESULT:` (one line per transform applied, or `nothing left to tidy`), `CANDIDATES:` (one line per leftover it still sees, each carrying its class, or `none`) and `FLAGS:` (one finding per line, each carrying its class, `overfit`, `uncovered-spec` or `beyond-spec`, or `none`). | The conductor routes each leftover and each flag by a labelled line it can read by class. |
| RF6 | After the leaf exits, the conductor checks what it left by asking git, not the report: `git status --porcelain`, `git log --format=%s <green-sha>..HEAD` and `git rev-list --count <green-sha>..HEAD` (the transforms that landed, narrated against the budget); `<green-sha>` is the step's atomic green commit, the one carrying the ledger tick, so the range holds exactly what the step's refactor produced. | verify-refactor judges the settled tree and can see neither fault the check looks for, so the conductor's own check covers them before the verifier runs. |
| RF7 | A dirty tree (any `git status --porcelain` output) is not carried to verify-refactor: `git reset --hard HEAD` drops the transform a killed leaf left half-applied, which leaves the last committed transform, the one the leaf's own suite run found green, and the narration says it was dropped. | The verifier is only shown work some suite run has seen. |
| RF8 | A commit in `<green-sha>..HEAD` whose subject is not `refactor:` is not carried to verify-refactor either: `git reset --hard <green-sha>` drops every commit the leaf made for this step, the well-formed ones with it (a leaf that committed production code here is not one whose other commits can be taken on trust), the step goes to verify-refactor on its green state, and the narration names what was dropped. | Production code never reaches the blind verifier, which asks whether behaviour was preserved and not whether a test earned the code. |
| RF9 | A leaf `FLAGS:` line whose class is `overfit` (code that passes this step's test without implementing the criterion, with the case that would force the general implementation) is answered inside this step: the refactor loop stops there and, for that case, one triangulating red → verify-red → green → verify-green runs inside the same step, exactly as the Red and Green phases run them, after the commits the loop has landed have passed verify-refactor and their frozen cases been re-recorded. | The case is this step's own criterion caught late, so the same step settles it; the red's frozen-case check cannot pass over a refactor no verifier has judged. |
| RF10 | An `overfit` flag grows no ledger step, makes no plan amendment and posts no second step-done comment: a step is mirrored done once, at the end of its own cycle, and the triangulation interrupts the step rather than finishing it. | The ledger and the thread show each step once, whatever its refactor loop found. |
| RF11 | After the triangulation the step's refactor loop resumes at its first sub-step with the budget it had left, asking `$S/refactor-budget.sh <count>` with the transforms already landed as `<count>`. | An overfit flag costs the step a triangulation and never a second budget. |
| RF12 | A step's triangulation runs at most once: a flag raised inside a triangulation's own refactor loop about the same code is narrated as a follow-up in the `### 🛠️ Refactor` comment, not triangulated again. | One flag is answered once per step, and what the second leaf saw stays on the record without spending more. |
| RF13 | An `uncovered-spec` flag, a behaviour the issue's spec requires that no ledger step covers, is the one flag class a plan amendment is made from: its behaviour is carried verbatim as a criterion into the `add-ledger-step.sh` call at the PASS branch, and no other class amends the plan, since the plan never grows for what the spec did not ask. | A gap the planner missed becomes a step planned and built like any other, and the plan grows exactly where the spec has an uncovered behaviour. |
| RF14 | A `beyond-spec` flag is narrated as a follow-up in the `### 🛠️ Refactor` comment and in the PR body, and is not planned and not built in this run. | The follow-up outlives the run without costing it a step, and every behaviour the run builds is one a criterion of the issue states. |
| RF15 | Verify runs blind and once, on the settled result: one verdict on the final state, the verifier given the step's `<green-sha>` as the range it reviews and `$step_green`, the step's own green tick, as `--step-green`, whose files bound it (#4544). | One judgment covers everything the step's refactor produced, after any triangulation and after the git check. |
| RF16 | The verify-refactor verdict is on the fast tier plus only the tests of the files the transforms touched still green, no catalogue smell met, and no smuggled generality. | The verifier measures the cheap tiers the leaf kept green, and judges the code and the generality on top of them. |
| RF17 | The `### 🛠️ Refactor` comment says how many transforms landed (the `git rev-list --count` figure, never the leaf's own account of what it did) against the budget, in `refactor-budget.sh`'s own shape (`refactor 2/5`); a count above the budget is narrated as an overrun (`refactor 7/5 — overrun`), never clamped to the budget. | A count over the budget is the leaf ignoring the number it was given, and the narration exists to make that visible. |
| RF18 | The comment also says why the tidying stopped: the leaf reported nothing left to tidy, it spent the budget it was given, or `$S/refactor-triage.sh < .tdd/develop-refactor.out` says so, where exit 2 (a malformed report) is narrated as that rather than bailed on, exit 3 (nothing structural is left) stops the tidying owing nothing, and exit 0 (structural candidates remain) means a follow-up owes them. | Each ending of the loop is named, and only a leftover someone owes is recorded as owed. |
| RF19 | The comment lists what the step did not do, taken verbatim from the leaf's `CANDIDATES:` lines and filed in two separate lists, the structural leftovers (a follow-up owes them) and the cosmetic ones (nobody owes them); each list says how many candidates it holds and then lists them, one per line. | A reader can size the structural backlog, which a count with no items, or the two classes run together, does not allow. |
| RF20 | A `test-only` candidate, and an `out-of-scope` one RF54 leaves to the structural pass, is in neither list: it is recorded through `refactor-owed.sh` and is not narrated as a backlog. | A candidate no transform could land is tracked once, in the run's owed file, and not counted as work the step skipped. |
| RF21 | In step 6 the same three figures are marked in the log with `$S/event.sh refactor transforms=<count> budget=<VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET> stopped=<reason> pass="$pass"`, where `transforms` is the observed count, unclamped. | The run log carries what the comment states, so the report reads real refactor and stopped cells for the step. |
| RF22 | The mark's `stopped` value is `nothing-left`, `budget` or `malformed`, or `nothing-structural` for `refactor-triage.sh` exit 3, or `structural-owed` for exit 0. | The log tells triage exit 3 and exit 0 apart. |
| RF23 | The mark's `pass` is `1` for the step's first pass and one more each time the triangulation path resumes the refactor loop and reaches the narration sub-step again. | Two passes of one step can be told apart in the log. |
