| RF24 | The structural leftovers are recorded apart from the narration with `$S/refactor-owed.sh append .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md "<STEP>" "$touched"`, its input on stdin being the refactor leaf's report `.tdd/develop-refactor.out` once and, for each in-step candidate, in an append of its own run right after that candidate's cover leaf and before the next cover leaf is launched, that candidate's `CANDIDATES: in-step` line followed by its own cover leaf's report `.tdd/develop-cover.out`; it copies the structural leftovers, never the cosmetic ones, into the run's owed file under this step's name, where the Draft PR renders every step's, and records an in-step candidate exactly when its own cover leaf answered `COVERED: no`. | `append` is what makes the structural leftovers outlast the step, since the comment is narration and `.tdd/develop-refactor.out` is overwritten by the next step's leaf; only a candidate's own cover leaf report carries its `COVERED:` answer, so without it no in-step candidate is recorded by its own answer, and since every cover leaf writes `.tdd/develop-cover.out`, an append after the last cover leaf would read only the last candidate's report (#2349). |
| RF25 | `$touched` is the step's bounds: when all are test files, a structural candidate is recorded test-only ("test file" being `transform-touches-tests.sh`'s rule, `case_reader.is_test_path`: `tests/` directories, `*.test.*`, JS/TS `*.spec.*`, Go `*_test.go`, and Python `test_*.py` or `*_test.py` anywhere), since no transform could land it. | A candidate no transform could land is recorded as such. |
| RF26 | On a verify-refactor PASS the step's cycle is complete, tidied and not merely green, so the conductor grows the plan with the behaviours of the leaf's `uncovered-spec` flags and of the passing verdict's own, clears the refactor-pending mark with `clear-refactor-pending.sh`, and commits the grown, cleared ledger first (`git commit -m "chore: refactor verified for <STEP summary> (#<n>)"`), before anything outside the repository says the step is done. | A resume reads the committed ledger, so the step's finished state exists there before the thread or the issue announce it. |
| RF27 | `git add -A` carries `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` into that same commit. | What `append` wrote is untracked until then, so the owed file travels with the step's finished state. |
| RF28 | The verify-refactor verdict's own flags are the `FLAGS:` lines before the final `VERDICT:`/`REASON:` pair in `.tdd/verify-refactor.log`, carrying the same three classes as the leaf's: only its `uncovered-spec` lines go into the plan-growing call, and its `overfit` and `beyond-spec` lines are narrated as follow-ups in the `### 🛠️ Refactor` comment and the PR body, since the step's refactor loop has already closed and no triangulation reopens it. | An unhandled edge case the verifier reports instead of rejecting is routed by class exactly as the leaf's flags are. |
| RF29 | `<k>` is the number of the issue's numbered acceptance criterion the behaviour serves, read from the issue's `## Acceptance criteria`, not inherited from the anchor: the uncovered-spec behaviours (the leaf's and the verifier's) serving one criterion go in one `add-ledger-step.sh --criterion <k> … after "<STEP>"` call, and different criteria get one call each, anchored after `<STEP>` so the triangulating step lands next to the code it triangulates. | Each added step is tagged to the criterion it serves and sits beside the code it covers. |
| RF30 | Each per-criterion `add-ledger-step.sh` call emits its own `step-added` event (`$S/event.sh step-added anchor="$step" added=<count>`), anchored on the step and carrying that call's count. | The log counts every insertion with its size. |
| RF31 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 here means the call itself was malformed, a `<k>` that is not a whole number ≥ 1, and bails with cause `step-add-refused`. | A malformed call is a plumbing failure named by its cause. |
| RF32 | A criterion no ledger step serves yet is no obstacle: the script tags the new step `⟦criterion k⟧` all the same. | A step added mid-run for a criterion not yet planned still carries its tag. |
| RF33 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 is that one call meeting the plan-growth budget: the values are filed as owed steps rather than planned (the script's entry in `SCRIPTS.md` says how), `next-unchecked.sh` never finds them there, and the conductor narrates it as the owed steps it is and carries on to the clear, since the step verified PASS and the values are recorded; the grown ledger rides the same commit as the clear. | The step finishes with nothing lost, and the run does not plan what it recorded. |
| RF34 | The push (`$S/push-branch.sh`) copies the step's finished state to the remote on the same terms as Green's: exit 1 launches the reconcile leaf (`Read` `<base directory>/RECONCILE.md` and run it when that happens), exit 2 is a **bail**. | A push that the remote refused is reconciled or ends the run, and the finished state otherwise reaches the remote. |
| RF35 | *Refactor loop*, after the push: immediately after that push the conductor runs `$S/stop-requested.sh` as *Green* does after its own push, and handles its exit 0 and exit 2 by *Green*'s stop check, which this site names rather than restating those two branches; on exit 3 it carries on to the mirror. | A requested stop is honoured at the ledger boundary, where the step's state is committed and pushed, by the one statement of the check. |
| RF36 | The conductor then mirrors the step to the issue: it posts `### ✅ Step done` by running `$S/comment.sh <n> step-done`, asks `$S/criterion-finished.sh <k> < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` (k from the step's `⟦criterion k⟧`), ticks the criterion in the issue body with `tick-issue-criterion.sh` on exit 0, and ticks nothing on exit 3, since a step serving it is still open or pending. | The issue shows a criterion done only when the last step serving it is done. |
| RF37 | The step-done mark, `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$($S/ledger-steps.sh last-done < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" $S/event.sh step-done`, is written at the same moment as the mirror. | The log shows when each step closed. |
| RF38 | After the mirror the conductor asks whether the run can still afford to finish: when `next-unchecked.sh` exits 3 there is no next step and nothing to ask; otherwise `$S/run-affordable.sh` is given the count of steps still to hand over, the ledger's unchecked boxes under the same grammar, leaving out the `⟦full-suite⟧` and `⟦human-owed⟧` lines `next-unchecked.sh` skips. | A run about to finish is not parked by a count that includes lines no cycle closes. |
| RF39 | On `run-affordable.sh` exit 0, or exit 3 (no full step has completed yet, so there is no median to project with), the loop goes back to the top of step 3 for the next unchecked ledger step. | A run with nothing to project carries on to its next step. |
| RF40 | On `run-affordable.sh` exit 1 the projection is over the ceiling and the conductor parks the run there, while parking is cheap, posting `### ⏸️ Parked — run budget (needs the user)` with the spent, steps remaining, median and projection figures `run-affordable.sh` printed, the ceiling (`VERI_RUN_BUDGET`, the second line `step-budget.sh` printed), and the two answers available, in this order: split the issue, or raise `VERI_RUN_BUDGET` and resume. | The ledger is committed, so a resume loses none of what was spent, and the decision goes to the user, since no leaf has the authority to make it. Splitting comes first under P16. |
| RF41 | The affordability park is not a bail: nothing failed and the ledger is at a state a resume already handles, so `classify-bail.sh` is not called on it. | A park is recorded as a decision waiting on the user, not as a failure. |
| RF42 | On `run-affordable.sh` exit 2 (no answer could be obtained: a missing event log, a bad `VERI_RUN_BUDGET`, a count that is not a whole number) the conductor **bails**, since that is a system failure and not a projection; earlier steps' commits are preserved. | An unreadable budget is named as the plumbing failure it is. |
| RF43 | Neither the refactor leaf nor verify-refactor runs the full suite: a break in a suite none of the step's transforms touched surfaces at the one full-suite run in *4. Draft PR*, where a red result appends a repair step. | The full suite runs once per PR, so a step's refactor pays only for the suites its transforms touched. |
| RF44 | The loop's first call is `$S/refactor-budget.sh 0`, and that call decides whether the leaf runs at all: exit 0 launches the leaf, exit 3 (the budget is 0; `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET=0` switches refactoring off) goes on to the verify sub-step. | Refactoring is switched off by one number, asked before any leaf is built. |
| RF45 | `<touched-files>` is the bounds of the tidy-up: the files this cycle produced, as one comma-separated line, computed once with `$S/refactor-touched.sh "$step_green"` (that commit's files), `$step_green` being the step's own green tick, not a later re-pin step's, got as `RESUME.md` sub-step 2 says, adding anything still uncommitted (`git diff --name-only HEAD`) if an interrupted run left work on disk. | The leaf's bounds are fixed once, at the green commit, and cover work an interrupted run left behind; a re-pin step's tick after the green commit is not the step's files (#4510). |
| RF46 | The `refactor` template has the leaf triage what it sees in `<touched-files>` into structural and cosmetic candidates and apply the structural ones, most valuable first, up to its budget. | The budget is spent on the most valuable structural transforms, and cosmetic candidates are only reported. |
| RF47 | The `refactor` template has the leaf take no untested generality (that comes only from triangulation) and flag overfit code as needing a triangulating test instead of hand-generalizing it. | Generality enters the code only through a test that demands it. |
| RF48 | The triangulation's own green commit is the step's `<green-sha>` from then on. | The check after the resumed loop reads exactly what the step's refactor produced from the state the tidying now starts from. |
| RF49 | The conductor runs `$S/comment.sh <n> refactor` and `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-refactor`, which post `### 🛠️ Refactor` (the transforms applied, or "no refactor warranted", plus any triangulation flagged) and `### 🔎 verify-refactor — PASS\|REJECT\|SKIPPED`. | The thread shows what the tidying did and what the verifier decided. |
| RF50 | When the leaf reports no `uncovered-spec` flag and the verdict carries none, there is no `add-ledger-step.sh` call and no `step-added` mark at all. | A step with no plan gap leaves the ledger and the log as they were. |
| RF51 | On a verify-refactor REJECT (exit 1) the conductor enters the revise loop (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md`): a recovered PASS is mirrored and the loop goes on, budget-exhausted is a **bail**; earlier steps' commits are preserved. | A rejected refactor gets a bounded number of revisions, then ends the run with the earlier steps kept. |
| RF52 | A malformed or killed-stuck verify-refactor (exit 2) is a **bail**; earlier steps' commits are preserved. | No verdict is never read as a pass, and the earlier steps stay committed. |
| CY116 | Exit 2 of `criterion-finished.sh` at a step's mirror **bails** with cause `criterion-unreadable`, a system failure; it is never treated as exit 3. | A ledger the criterion check cannot read is a broken run, not a criterion that is still open. |
| RF53 | After the checks of RF6–RF8, when `git rev-list --count <green-sha>..HEAD` is 0 and `git status --porcelain` prints nothing — the leaf landed no transform, or the budget was 0 and no leaf ran — the conductor launches no verify-refactor: it records `$S/event.sh verify-refactor-skipped reason=nothing-landed`, treats the step as on a verify-refactor PASS whose verdict carries no flags (RF26 runs with the leaf's flags only), and posts `### 🔎 verify-refactor — SKIPPED (nothing landed)` by running `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-refactor --skipped`. | The tree is the one verify-green already passed, so a second blind verdict on it costs a leaf and can only repeat that verdict; 72.7% of passes land nothing. |
| RF54 | An `out-of-scope` candidate whose transform changes at least one file in `<touched-files>` is done in the step: a cover leaf mutates the code it will move first, and `COVERED: no` records it with `refactor-owed.sh` as RF20 does; when covered, its transform is bounded to `<touched-files>` plus the files the candidate names, checked before verify-refactor runs, and counts against the step's budget. | Measured after ADR-0031: for 73% of out-of-scope candidates a later step changed the code before the structural pass did; the cover leaf is the same guard the structural pass uses for code the step's tests never exercised. |
| RF55 | An `out-of-scope` candidate that changes no file in `<touched-files>` is never done in the step; it is recorded with `refactor-owed.sh` as `out-of-bounds`, apart from the candidates RF54 leaves to the structural pass. | The step's refactor tidies what the step changed, not unrelated code; a PR that refactors code its issue never touched grows out of scope (operator, 2026-10-24). |
| RF56 | SKILL.md's paragraph routing an `out-of-scope` candidate (Refactor section) names the line kind a refactor leaf reports a candidate RF54 does in the step on, `CANDIDATES: in-step | <files> | <transform>`, and says `<bound>` is `<touched-files>` plus the files that line names. | Without the line kind and the bound in the procedure, the conductor cannot find the candidates to cover, nor compute the check RF54 runs (PR #2330 review). |
| EV191 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| CY123 | After a step's last verify PASS and before its final tick, the conductor runs `$S/pin-recheck.sh --branch-base <base> <base-sha>`, `<base>` the recorded branch base. Exit 1 is a REJECT naming the pin (one that stayed green under its mutation, or a before-state pin green at `HEAD` and red on the range's base, which this range disarmed, or a mutation pin printed `not checked: <file> (<reason>)`); a `pin stayed green` finding is routed by `pin-reject-route.sh --recheck`, a `not checked` one prints `not-checked <file>` and bails with cause `needs-user`, a process failure naming the pin, never a refactor revise, and a reason with neither line prints `revise`, spending `REVISE_BUDGET`, or `revise-impossible`, which bails `needs-user` the same way, under the condition `SCRIPTS.md` states for the route; a pin this range disarmed is never given to a refactor revise leaf, which may not edit tests, but to a leaf that may (a Red re-pin of its case, or an added plan step), and a re-pin that leaves it still green under its mutation bails once as a process failure naming the pin; a before-state pin green on the base too is stale, printed `stale: <record file> <test path>` on stdout, and so is a mutation pin green under its mutation on the base too, printed `stale: <record file> <mutated file>`; neither changes the exit; exit 2 bails as a system failure (a base that cannot be resolved or replayed included); each pin it prints as needing a look is copied to the PR body's list of what a human owes. Exit 0 and exit 3 leave the `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` receipt, exit 3 included when every selected pin (a before record too) is retired because the range deletes its named test file, or a mutation pin's mutated file (a `retired:` line on stdout); a before record whose replay range names a file gone before the range is replayed without that file, never retired; the clear and the tick refuse without it (SC67, SC68). | A step can disarm an earlier pin and pass its own verification; nothing else compares them, and the cure is a test edit the refactor leaf may not make (#107 bailed at $2.30). |
| RF57 | *Refactor loop*, the verify-refactor PASS paragraph: only after that PASS, and before the step's ledger commit, the conductor re-records the frozen cases the refactor changed, including an earlier step's, when each kept its title and its assertion lines modulo whitespace; a case that did not is left as recorded, so `check-earlier` still refuses it. | A verified refactor otherwise leaves a stale record that makes every later step's gate unpassable (five sightings, 2026-10-28 to 10-30); the verifier's PASS is what makes the new bodies trustworthy, so the re-record never precedes it. |
| RF58 | *Refactor loop*, the verify-refactor PASS paragraph: `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-refactored` exit 2 is a bail with cause `rerecord-failed`, a system failure, and exit 1 a bail `needs-user` as a process failure, quoting each `skipped:` line (`BAIL.md`); after either, neither `clear-refactor-pending.sh` nor the step's ledger commit follows; a case a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step of the ledger names, in the suite it names, is exempt from the re-record and from that exit 1; on its exit 1 or 2 every frozen record is restored byte-identical to what it was before the call. | A re-record that could not answer, or that left a case stale, otherwise commits a step over a partial record, and the failure surfaces only at a later step's gate; a bail with no token cannot be classified or counted. |
| RF59 | In Refactor sub-step 3, when the loop has landed commits since `<green-sha>`, the conductor runs the sub-step 2 checks and verify-refactor over them before the triangulating red, and launches the red only after that PASS. | A frozen case a refactor changed is refreshed only after a verify-refactor PASS, and the triangulating red's `check-earlier` refuses it until then. |
| RF60 | Sub-step 3 states that after that PASS `rerecord-refactored` runs, the refactor-pending mark stays and no ledger tick is committed, and a REJECT goes the revise path with no triangulation. | Only the step's final PASS finishes the step; a failed early verification must not be skipped past. |
| RF73 | *Refactor loop*, the `overfit` flag paragraph: after the early verify-refactor PASS, an early `rerecord-refactored` exit 2 is a bail with cause `rerecord-failed`, a system failure, as at the final verify-refactor PASS, and no triangulating red is launched; its exit 1 stays a bail `needs-user`. (§3 *Refactor loop*, sub-step 3, the `overfit` flag paragraph.) | The second site of the same bail names the same token. |
| CY137 | The Refactor loop's step 5 says that with `refactor` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify`, a landed refactor launches no verify-refactor. It records `verify-refactor-skipped reason=switched-off`, posts `SKIPPED (switched off)`, writes `skipped` to `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit`, and takes the PASS branch with `pin-recheck.sh` and the pending-mark clear. | The skip must be in the procedure the conductor follows. |
| RF61 | In the Refactor loop's paragraph on an `out-of-scope` candidate, the in-step check is `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <green-sha> <bound> --touched <touched-files>`, and its exit 1 rejects the transform before verify-refactor runs. | The in-step bound lets the leaf change files the candidate merely names; a transform that changed none of the step's own files is out of scope (PRD 012 BG6). |
| RF72 | At the mirror to the issue, a `criterion-finished.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/criterion-finished.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-criterion-unreadable` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `criterion-unreadable`, `criterion-finished.sh` is not called again, and the exit 2 is still never treated as exit 3. (§3 *Refactor loop*, sub-step 6, the mirror to the issue.) | The cause's site names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| CY147 | The Refactor loop's step 5, **Switched off**, names the command that posts `### 🔎 verify-refactor — SKIPPED (switched off)`: after `skipped` is written to `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit`, `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-refactor`, posted once. | The paragraph said to post the heading but named no script, and step 6's post rendered a skip as `PASS`. |
| CY363 | At §3 *Refactor loop* sub-step 6 (the `--criterion` growth), an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused` and `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again. | Split from CY177. The site names the rule in one line, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| CY321 | The *Refactor loop* is marked as stage `refactor-loop`, exactly one `stage-end` per `stage-start`: `event.sh stage-start stage=refactor-loop` as it begins, unless `refactor-half.sh` got past its exit-2 refusals and so wrote it (not again when 3's triangulation resumes it, the stage staying open through the triangulation), and `event.sh stage-end stage=refactor-loop status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` when it ends: 0 once 6's `chore: refactor verified` push exits 0, unless `step-close.sh` made that push and so wrote that end itself, or its reconcile succeeds, and before that push's stop check, 1 at 6's `pin-recheck` `repin` branch before its ledger commit, 3 at a stop before that push, else the code of the call that ended it, a bail's included. (*Refactor loop*.) | A loop that ends without its end line shows as unended, not as passed, and a second start or end inside one loop would miscount it. |
| RF75 | The Refactor loop section carries no `refactor-half.sh` or `step-close.sh` call line of its own, neither in its opening paragraph nor at the start of sub-step 6's PASS branch, since `run-step.sh` runs them; its opening paragraph names `run-step.sh`'s `seam=refactor-half` and `seam=step-close` stops as the points to take the loop and that PASS branch by hand, from the sub-step the stop names, else from the loop's sub-step 1 and from that branch. | The four fused calls become one, so the Refactor paragraph stops duplicating the runner (PRD 038.2, RN11). |
| CY402 | The refactor leaf runs the test files covering the files the transforms touched, and verify-refactor the fast tier and those test files; neither runs the `affected` tier or any broader one. | The full suite at the PR's one full run catches what a narrow run misses. |
| RF74 | The Refactor loop section, right after its opening paragraph about `run-step.sh`'s refactor stops, holds one line ending the hand path (the loop done by the conductor itself): `$S/seam-settle.sh <n> refactor-half` with the keys `$S/script-help.sh seam-settle.sh` lists for that stage. | `step-close.sh` reads a record only `refactor-half.sh` writes; a hand refactor must hand back the same way (PRD 038.1, SC9). |
| RF76 | At the start of the *Refactor loop*'s sub-step 6 PASS branch, the conductor is told, after a hand loop's `seam-settle.sh`, to run `$S/run-step.sh <n> --from step-close`, and to follow the rest of the branch by its prose only at that call's `seam=step-close` stop. | A hand or revised refactor reaches this branch with no runner call left on its path, so `step-close.sh` never ran and the `refactor-half` record went unread (PRD 038.2). |
| RF77 | *Refactor loop*, sub-step 3, the `overfit` flag paragraph: a flag whose case is the criterion of a later unchecked step of the ledger (as `refactor-half.sh` matches it) is not triangulated: it is narrated in the `### 🛠️ Refactor` comment's note as owed by that step, no triangulating red is launched, and the refactor loop goes on to 5 (verify-refactor) without stopping; a flag no later unchecked step owns is triangulated as before. (§3 *Refactor loop*, sub-step 3, the `overfit` flag paragraph.) | A triangulating red for another step's behaviour is rejected by verify-red and bails the run (#4432), though the later step will build and pin it. |
| RF78 | *Refactor loop* sub-step 5's "unless nothing landed" branch writes `skipped` to `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit` beside its `verify-refactor-skipped reason=nothing-landed` mark and its `comment.sh <n> verify-refactor --skipped` post, as its "Switched off" branch does. | Without the write, step-close's `rerecord-refactored` reads an earlier step's verdict file, or exits 3 on a missing one, for a step whose refactor landed nothing. |
| RF79 | Sub-step 1's code block runs `$S/near-duplicates.sh "$step_green"` into a file under `.tdd/` after `refactor-touched.sh` and passes that file to `build-develop-prompt.sh refactor` as its last argument; the prose under the block says exit 3 is no pair and the leaf is still launched, and exit 2 is a **bail** as a system failure. (Sub-step 1, the code block and the paragraph after it.) | The conductor's own route and `refactor-half.sh` build the same prompt, and a detector that could not answer is never read as "no duplication". |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#A `⟦diff-checked⟧` step — develop-diff, then verify-diff (blind)

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| DC1 | When `next-unchecked.sh` hands over a step carrying `⟦diff-checked⟧`, SKILL.md sends the run to `<base directory>/DIFF-CHECKED.md`, which it `Read`s then and not before, and runs in place of Red, Green and Refactor; a run that never reaches such a step never reads it. | Only a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step needs the 3,778 bytes the conductor otherwise re-reads on every turn (#3152); a diff-checked step run without the file would improvise its step base sha, leaf and verifier invocations, and the bails around them. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#A `⟦phases: …⟧` step — each listed phase, a developer leaf then a blind verifier

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY121 | When `next-unchecked.sh` hands over a step listing `⟦phases: …⟧`, SKILL.md sends the run to `<base directory>/PHASED.md`, which it `Read`s then and not before, and runs in place of Red, Green and Refactor. | No measured run held a phased step (0 of 60, #2076); a phased step run without the file would improvise the phase order and its freeze checks. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#4. Draft PR

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EV192 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| DP93 | When `next-unchecked.sh` exits 3 (every ledger step is checked off but the `⟦full-suite⟧` and `⟦human-owed⟧` lines, which it skips), SKILL.md sends the run to `<base directory>/DRAFT-PR.md`, which it `Read`s then and not before, and runs as step 4, *Draft PR*; a run that never reaches that step never reads it. | The Draft PR section is 33,605 bytes the conductor otherwise re-reads on every turn (#3152) and needs once, at the end of the run; a run that reached the step without reading the file would ready a PR with none of its full-suite, rebase, CI and body rules. |
| DP95 | In the Draft PR step, right after the structural pass (or its `nothing-structural` skip) and before the PR is readied, the conductor runs `$S/refactor-candidates.sh <n> .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` once: exit 0 prints the candidates issue's url, which goes in the PR body's `## Refactor owed` section; exit 3 (nothing out-of-bounds or not done) does nothing; exit 2 bails as a **system** failure (`no-verdict`). | The human can groom and decide on what the run left, from one issue outside the queue, without reading each PR body. |
| DP96 | The step says the candidates issue is never labelled with the opt-in label and is filed at most once per run: a resume runs the same command and it replaces the existing issue's body. | A run's leftovers never enter the queue by themselves, and a resume never files a duplicate (#1609 was one). |
| DP359 | Sub-step 4 posts a final `### ✅ Done — <pr-url>` comment by running `$S/comment.sh <n> done`, which puts the same `### 📊 Metrics` block under it, verbatim from the file just committed — or, when `$metrics` is not 0, the failed-render line in its place; it mirrors the `event.sh done pr=<pr-url>` mark sub-step 2 wrote, and writes no second one. | The thread ends with the committed cost report and exactly one `done` mark in the log. |
| CY416 | The Draft PR step's PR-body instruction says that a non-empty `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` adds `⚠️ Unverified phases: <list>`, and the ✅ Done comment instruction says `comment.sh done` adds that line. | Visibility belongs at the two sites a reviewer reads. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Detecting the test command

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EV193 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| DT1 | The conductor does not pick the test command by hand: it runs `$S/detect-test-command.sh --tier fast --issue <body-file> --at <base>` once, from the target repository's root, with the issue body preflight step 0.3 saved and `<base>` the run's recorded branch base, as PREFLIGHT.md states it. | The spelled call matches PF6, so the two accounts of the same command cannot drift. |
| DT2 | The script prints the one test command of that tier on stdout and exits 0. | A single command on stdout, or a non-zero exit — never a guess. |
| DT3 | On exit 3 the script prints nothing, because no marker it trusts matched, and the conductor asks the user for the command rather than guessing. | A wrong suite silently run is worse than a question asked once. |
| DT4 | That stdout is `<test-cmd>` everywhere it is needed: the baseline run in preflight step 0.3 and the `<test-cmd>` argument of every `$S/build-develop-prompt.sh` call, so every leaf runs the same suite. | The baseline and the leaves judge the same thing. |
| DT5 | The command is detected once, with `--issue`, and that one string is passed on, since a second detection spelled without the flag would hand the leaves a different command from the one the baseline proved green. | Two detections can disagree, and the disagreement would be invisible. |
| DT6 | A `Test command: <cmd>` line in the issue body wins over everything else for that issue's full tier (`--tier all`), for that issue's run only: an issue that named its suite in words has already answered the question for the full suite. It never changes the fast tier, so it never changes `<test-cmd>`, the value every leaf receives as `VERI_TEST_CMD` and as the `<test-cmd>` argument. This is the per-issue escape hatch. | An issue can name its own suite without changing the skill, and without making every leaf run the full suite. |
| DT7 | Exceptions (#1225): for `--tier all`, a `Test command:` line equal to the command the fast tier resolves to is dropped, and so is one that is the CLAUDE.md `veri: all tests:` command with a `TESTS=` selector appended; the full tier then falls through to CLAUDE.md's `veri: all tests:` line and then the usual detection, so an issue naming the fast tier or a subset of the suite never stops the full suite running. | Naming a subset must not silently shrink the full run (#3025 readied a PR on one file's 8 tests). |
| DT8 | When the inference guesses wrong for a particular repository rather than for one issue, the fix is that repository's, not this skill's: its `veri: <tier> tests:` CLAUDE.md lines, recorded by `/vinstall`, are used ahead of every inference, and they are the per-repo escape hatch. The section names no file under `.veri/`. (*Detecting the test command*, last paragraph.) | A repository's own quirk is fixed in the repository, through the one declaration veri still reads. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL.md#Guardrails

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| EV194 | In this section, every sentence that sends the run into an event (a bail, phrased as "**bail**", "bails as …", "bail with cause …", "bails with cause …", "→ bail", "is a bail" or a bare "bail" instruction, bolded or not; a resume; a skill-edit step) names the file to Read for that event under `<base directory>/` (`BAIL.md`, `RESUME.md`, `SKILL-EDIT.md`). | Split from EV1. A leaf or conductor that reaches an event with no pointer improvises the procedure instead of reading it (PRD 010). |
| GD1 | The conductor writes no code: developer leaves write the test, the implementation and the refactor, and the conductor orchestrates. | A conductor that writes code has no blind verifier for it. |
| GD2 | Red and green are always performed by leaves distinct from the verifier. | A leaf cannot verify its own claim. |
| GD3 | Every leaf is launched through `supervise.sh`, never `run-leaf.sh` or `claude` directly, because the supervisor is the one place stuck-detection lives. | One launch path, so one place can notice a leaf stop answering. |
| GD4 | The conductor signals only processes it started, by the pid it recorded when it started them, never by name or pattern (`pkill`, `killall`, `kill $(pgrep …)`), which matches every process on the machine whose command line fits, another live run's supervisor included. | A pattern kill reaches other people's runs. |
| GD5 | An experiment's processes and leaf names carry the `exp-` prefix, which no live run uses. | An experiment can be found and stopped without touching a run. |
| GD6 | The run lives in its own worktree, made by `$S/issue-worktree.sh`; the conductor never `git switch`es or `git checkout`s the caller's checkout onto the issue branch. | The caller's checkout is never moved under them. |
| GD7 | The conductor enters the worktree once, in step 1.2, and stays there for the whole run. | One working directory for the whole run, so paths mean one thing. |
| GD8 | The conductor never takes over a branch another worktree already has checked out: the script's exit 2 is that refusal, to be honoured and not worked around. | Two runs on one branch lose each other's work. |
| GD9 | Every developer prompt is built by `$S/build-develop-prompt.sh` and never composed from prose, so every run of a phase sends the same instructions — they live in its template — and only the variables differ, and the report lines the conductor branches on are the template's own footer rather than something asked for this time. | Identical instructions make one run comparable with the next. |
| GD10 | Per step the order is one failing test, then minimum code, then the refactor loop; production code never precedes its failing test. | The test is what makes the code's necessity visible. |
| GD11 | A test that passes against unmodified code is never sent to verify-red: it is either a blind-verified pin or a bail, never a Green — save on a ⟦replaces-case⟧ step, whose pin is followed by the green that deletes exactly the named cases. | A test that passes before the change proves nothing about it. |
| GD12 | The committed ledger is the source of truth: the conductor loops the cycle over its steps until none remain, checks each step off atomically with its green commit, and treats the issue thread as only a mirror of the ledger. | The record a resume reads is the committed one. |
| GD13 | The ledger is never hand-edited: the conductor uses `tick-ledger.sh`, or `reorder-ledger-steps.sh` to reorder unchecked steps. | Every ledger change goes through a tool that can refuse. |
| GD14 | Generality comes only from triangulation: overfit code is flagged for a triangulating test and never hand-generalised in a refactor. | Generality no test demanded is untested generality. |
| GD15 | Every phase is blind-verified before the conductor proceeds, and every verdict is mirrored to the issue thread. | No phase advances on the developer's own word. |
| GD16 | The full suite must be green before the conductor commits and before it opens the PR: that is `detect-test-command.sh --tier all`'s command, run through `detach-command.sh` at the Draft PR step — the branch's own full run, again after each ⟦suite-repair⟧ step a red result adds, and under `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE=local` one more on the rebased tip when sub-step 1 rebased the branch. Under `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE=ci` no local full run of the branch is made, and CI's run on the readied PR is the branch's full run. | Each run of the full suite judges a tip that something changed, and where CI runs the same command on the readied head a local run of it is the same verdict paid for twice (PRD 028). |
| GD17 | The PR is opened as a draft at the plan commit and, unless a stack branch is named, readied for review at the Draft PR step. | The PR exists from the start as the run's claim, and is offered for review only when finished; a stacked run's PR stays a draft, since a draft triggers no CI (PRD 034 D9). |
| GD18 | The PR always carries `Closes #<n>`. | The claim is machine-readable, so the issue is not taken twice. |
| GD19 | The conductor never merges the PR. | Landing is a human decision. |
| GD20 | On a `REJECT` the conductor runs the bounded revise loop — by default 2 attempts, each a fresh leaf carrying the reason — and when a verifier answers `REJECT` it `Read`s `<base directory>/REVISE.md`. | A rejection is retried a bounded number of times, by the book. |
| GD21 | If the revise loop exhausts, the conductor bails as a process failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`). | A spent verdict budget is the methodology's answer. |
| GD22 | On a killed-stuck leaf past its retry budget, or an unreadable verdict, the conductor bails as a system failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`). | No answer obtained is not the same as an answer of no. |
| GD23 | The conductor always preserves commits and classifies the cause, and never hand-fixes the code itself or loops past the budget. | A stop keeps its evidence and its reason, and buys nothing with extra attempts. |

## skills/tdd/DIFF-CHECKED.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY83 | A `⟦diff-checked⟧` step runs no Red leaf, and no Green or Refactor phase either: its criterion is a property of the branch's own diff, so there is no failing test to write and its step adds none. | A property of the diff has nothing for a failing test to fail on, so the step goes straight to the change and its diff check. |
| CY84 | On a fresh run and on a resume alike, a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step's base sha is the last commit that changed the ledger (the previous step's tick), never a `HEAD` taken after a killed `develop-diff` attempt's commit; it is recorded with `git log -1 --format=%H -- .tdd/issue-<n>.md` before the `develop-diff` leaf runs. | The verifier's own diff then starts at the same commit however the step was reached. |
| CY85 | On a fresh step, `git rev-parse HEAD` taken before the `develop-diff` leaf runs names that same commit, since every step ends in a ledger commit; on a resume it may not. | The shorter command is safe where a step began from its predecessor's ledger commit, and only there. |
| CY86 | The `develop-diff` leaf is built by `build-develop-prompt.sh diff <spec-file> "<STEP>" '<test-cmd>' '<fast-cmd>'`, and makes the change the criterion names, or makes no commit when the criterion is that something stays unchanged. | A "stays unchanged" criterion is met by an empty diff, and the leaf is told so. |
| CY87 | Before it reports, the `develop-diff` leaf keeps the fast tier and then the test files covering the changed files green, never running the `affected` tier. | The leaf pays for the fast tier and the tests of the files its change touches, no more. |
| CY88 | The blind `diff` verifier, run by `run-verdict.sh --step "<STEP>" --fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>' --branch-base <base> diff <spec-file> <step-base-sha>`, runs the fast tier and then the test files covering the changed files, never the `affected` tier, and rejects the step when either is red. | The verifier checks the same two runs the developer leaf kept green, and a red one is a rejection. |
| CY89 | The `diff` verifier judges the step against the run's recorded branch base `<base>` (`.tdd/issue-<n>-branch-base` in the main working tree, never a remote-tracking ref) and against its own diff from the step base sha; the branch base goes to `run-verdict.sh` as `--branch-base <base>`, the step base sha after the spec file. | The verdict covers the whole branch and the step's own change as two separate things. |
| CY91 | A supervisor exit 2 on the `develop-diff` leaf is a `killed-stuck` bail, as for any developer leaf. | No answer from the leaf is a plumbing failure, handled as for every developer leaf. |
| CY92 | After the `develop-diff` wait exits 0 and before the verifier is launched, the conductor runs `$S/suite-reported.sh develop-diff`: exit 2 means the leaf left no `SUITE:` line, so no verifier is launched and the conductor **bails** as a system failure with cause `report-missing`. | A verifier is only launched over a report that says whether the suites ran. |
| CY93 | The `diff` revise leaf asks for the `SUITE:` line too: after its wait exits 0 the conductor runs `$S/suite-reported.sh revise-diff-<k>` for the `revise-diff-<k>` leaf before the re-verify, with the same exit 2 outcome (**bail**, `report-missing`). | A revised attempt is held to the same report check as the first. |
| CY94 | On the `diff` verifier's PASS (exit 0), the conductor ticks the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step with `tick-ledger.sh` without `--refactor-pending`, since no refactor loop follows, then commits the ledger and pushes it as Green does. | The step is finished at the tick, with no refactor pending to resume into. |
| CY95 | After that commit and push, the conductor posts `### ✅ Step done` by running `$S/comment.sh <n> step-done`, composing none of it. | The thread shows the step closed. |
| CY96 | On that PASS the conductor then ticks the step's criterion in the issue body with `tick-issue-criterion.sh` on `criterion-finished.sh` exit 0, ticking nothing on exit 3, exactly as a Refactor loop's PASS does. | The issue's criterion is ticked only once its last step is done. |
| CY97 | On the `diff` verifier's REJECT (exit 1), the conductor runs a revise of the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step's `develop-diff` work under `REVISE_BUDGET`, `Read`ing `<base directory>/REVISE.md`: on a recovered PASS it ticks as above, on budget-exhausted it **bails**. | A rejected diff gets a bounded number of revisions, then ends the run. |
| CY98 | A malformed or killed-stuck verifier (exit 2) means no verdict on the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step could be obtained, so the conductor **bails** as a system failure (`no-verdict`), see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`. | No verdict is not a verdict, and the bail is labelled a system failure. |
| CY99 | A `⟦fixture-repair⟧` step's verify-green PASS closes it the same way as a `⟦diff-checked⟧` PASS: ticked with `tick-ledger.sh` without `--refactor-pending`, committed with a `test:` subject, no Refactor loop run, and its criterion ticked with `tick-issue-criterion.sh` on `criterion-finished.sh` exit 0, ticking nothing on exit 3. | A fixture repair is finished at its green and carries a test commit subject. |
| CY140 | The `develop-diff` leaf's commit, and the `revise-diff-<k>` leaf's, are the step's work: the conductor leaves each as made and runs no `git reset --soft`, squash or re-commit on it. | Rewriting a leaf's commit hid history the verifier judges and the run's log records. |
| CY141 | After a verify-diff PASS the ledger tick is committed as its own commit on top of the leaf's commit, so a step that changed something leaves the leaf's commit and the tick commit in that order. | The tick marks the step finished; the change it finished is a commit of its own. |
| CY142 | After the `develop-diff` leaf's wait exits 0, and after each `revise-diff-<k>`, before its verifier, the conductor runs `$S/diff-leaf-deviation.sh <leaf> <step-base-sha> .tdd/issue-<n>.md`. Exit 0 means a deviation was found and recorded as a `diff-leaf-deviation` event; exit 3 means the one-commit flow, or no commit for an unchanged criterion. Both go on to the verifier. Exit 2 bails the run. The conductor never resets, squashes or repairs the leaf's commits. | A leaf that strays from its one commit leaves a record, and a broken check fails loudly instead of passing silently. |
| CY152 | The paragraph that launches the blind `diff` verifier says that with `diff` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no verifier launches; it names the `verify-diff-skipped reason=switched-off` event and `skipped` in `.tdd/verdict-diff.exit`, and says the PASS branch follows with `pin-recheck.sh`. | The skip must be in the procedure the conductor follows. |
| CY171 | A `suite-reported.sh develop-diff` or `suite-reported.sh revise-diff-<k>` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/suite-reported.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-report-missing` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before the verifier (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `report-missing` with no verifier. (The `suite-reported.sh` paragraph.) | Every site of the cause. |
| CY251 | For a `⟦test-quality⟧` step, after its verify-diff PASS and before its tick, the conductor runs `$S/mutation-probe.sh <test file> <old> <new> -- <command…>` from the leaf's `FLIP:` line, `<command…>` the repo's `veri: one test file:` command (`detect-test-command.sh --tier "one test file"`) run on that test file or, when the repo declares none (that call exits 3), its all-tier command (`detect-test-command.sh --tier all`): exit 0 goes on to the tick; exit 1 is a verify-diff REJECT naming the test file it left green, spending `REVISE_BUDGET`; a missing or malformed `FLIP:` line is a REJECT the same way; exit 2 bails `no-verdict`. | Each fix is proven live by a script, not the verifier, in any repo, as PRD 031.2's mutant table is (PRD 032, TX4, C6, C15; #2506). |
| CY291 | A `⟦lint-repair⟧` step runs as any `⟦diff-checked⟧` step does, and its verify-diff prompt carries the repo's declared lint command, which the verifier runs, rejecting while it exits non-zero. | The repair is judged by the command it repairs, under a blind verifier (PRD 032, LR3). |
| DC2 | The `develop-diff` leaf and the blind `diff` verifier run the fast tier and the test files covering the changed files, never the `affected` tier. | The diff phase was 3384 s wall for 115 s of leaf API time. |
| CY411 | The branch-base sha handed to the `diff` verifier is the conductor's recorded base, never `git merge-base HEAD origin/<default>`. | A leaf can rewrite the ref. |
| CY413 | `DIFF-CHECKED.md` opens with one call line: run `$S/run-step.sh <n>`, again on exit 3, and on 1 or 2 go on by the paragraph its last line names; `diff-step by hand ends $S/step-tick.sh <n>`. Every paragraph stays. | The prose stays the fallback (DST13, S8). |
| CY414 | The PASS bullet names the ledger commit's subject for a ⟦diff-checked⟧ tick: `chore: diff-checked — <step summary> (#<n>)`. | Runs commit it under that subject today and no skill file names it (DST6, S14). |
| CY418 | The PASS bullet says that on a re-pin step, the one `pin-reject-route.sh --recheck` adds, `pin-recheck.sh` runs from `<green>~1`, `<green>` the re-armed step's green tick, the ledger's last commit before the step base that changes a line other than a re-pin step's, never from the step base. | From the step base, the re-pin plan commit, the range leaves out the fix that disarmed the pin, `pin-recheck.sh` exits 3 and a re-pin that left the pin green is ticked; a step that disarmed two pins gets a second re-pin after the first ticks, and the ledger's plain last commit would be that tick (#4511). |
| CY419 | The PASS bullet says that on a re-pin step, before `pin-recheck.sh`, a net diff since the step base (`git diff --name-only <step-base-sha> HEAD`) holding no test file is a verify-diff REJECT that spends `REVISE_BUDGET`, naming the pin's file in its reason. | #4512: a re-pin step's deliverable is the edited case of its pin; one that edits no test file would otherwise tick with the pin still disarmed. |

## skills/tdd/GO-RED.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| GRD1 | The section opens with one call line: run `$S/run-step.sh <n>`, again on exit 3, which runs `go-red-step.sh`, the section in one call. | The conductor reaches the step through `run-step.sh` like any other, the prose staying the fallback. |
| GRD2 | A `⟦go-red⟧` step names its probe as ``probe `<file>` `<old>` → `<new>` on `<case>` ``, runs no Red leaf, no Green and no Refactor, and adds no test: `go-red-step.sh` runs `mutation-probe.sh` on that existing case with the `veri: one test file:` command (break, red, restore, the probe appended to `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md`), then `step-tick.sh <n>` ticks the step against that record and commits it under `test: go-red — <STEP summary> (#<n>)`. | A go-red is a procedure on an already pinned case; its deliverable is the mutation evidence, not a new test (#4432). |
| GRD3 | It says `tick-ledger.sh` exits 2 on a `⟦go-red⟧` step unless the mutation record holds an entry of its own probe (the same file, old and new, its command naming the case), so no go-red step is ticked without its probe, and a repository with no `veri: one test file:` line gets exit 2, never the whole suite. | The tick keeps evidence and tick together, and another probe on the same file, or a red from another case, is not that evidence. |
| GRD4 | Exit 1 ticks nothing and has two last lines: `stopped at probe <case> stayed green …`, the case staying green under the break, bails with `cause=needs-user`; ``stopped at probe <file> no longer holds `<old>` `` is neither a bail: the step's probe is rewritten against `<file>` as it now stands, committed as a `plan: rewrite` commit and checked by verify-plan, then the step is rerun. Exit 2 (no open `⟦go-red⟧` step, no probe named, no `veri: one test file:` line, the case red on the unmutated file, a probe or `detect-test-command.sh` exit 2, the file not restored, any `step-tick.sh` exit but 0) ticks nothing and bails as a system failure. | A break the case cannot see is a methodology answer only the human can settle; a broken probe is plumbing. The two exits stay apart, and a probe whose text is gone is a stale plan line, mended in the plan, not a bail. |

## skills/tdd/DRAFT-PR.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| DP1 | After the Draft PR step's last rebase, the regrade block re-signs every unit the run signed before the full suite runs on the rebased tip, so a GRADED line made stale only by the rebase never turns that suite red; a REJECT from that regrade appends the repair step, and a grader exit 2 bails `no-verdict`. | The rebased text is new text, so it is graded before the suite judges it, and every SKILL.md-editing run reaches its full suite with current signatures. |
| CY118 | The Draft PR regrade states that once a unit's text has been REJECTed, a regrade of the same text (same signature input) may not re-sign it; only an author run that changes the text, followed by a PASS, may. | #2121: a suite-repair regrade re-rolled the grader on unchanged text and overturned a REJECT. |
| DP2 | The step is reached when `next-unchecked.sh` exits 3: every ledger step is checked off but the `⟦full-suite⟧` and `⟦human-owed⟧` lines, which that script skips and no cycle closes, so an unchecked line carrying either does not hold the run back from this step. | A run whose only open lines are ones no cycle can close still reaches its PR. |
| DP3 | Under `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE=local` the full suite must be green before this step: each time the run enters this step, the conductor runs `$S/detect-test-command.sh --tier all`'s command via `detach-command.sh` once, in the same start-then-wait shape as the preflight baseline — the branch's own full-suite gate, since every leaf runs the fast command, the test files covering the files its step changed, or both, and none runs a broader tier; a run that comes back to this step after a repair step's cycle runs it again, and no sentence of the step calls the full run "exactly once" or once per run. | A break outside every step's changed files' tests is caught on the whole branch each time it reaches this step, a repaired branch included, before a human is asked to look. |
| DP4 | A red result on that full run does not bail: the conductor appends a repair step to the ledger, commits it, and returns to step 3, *The cycle loop*, the failing suites being that step's red evidence. | A suite the steps broke gets a verified repair cycle instead of ending the run. |
| DP5 | The repair step's text lists every suite the full run left red, each with its first `FAIL -` line; the develop and verify prompts take that list from the step's text through their templates, and the conductor never edits a built prompt to add a suite one of them left out. | Both leaves see the same red suites, carried by the ledger, and every prompt stays what its template built. |
| DP6 | The repair step's developer adds a unit-tier test when the failure exposes a gap the fast tier missed, and its verifier judges the fix against the full command, not the fast one. | The repair closes the fast tier's blind spot and is judged by the run that found it. |
| DP7 | The full-suite repair step is appended with `add-ledger-step.sh` after the last done step (`ledger-steps.sh last-done`), as `'<fix the full-suite failure: <suite> — <its FAIL - line>> ⟦suite-repair⟧'`, with an `event.sh step-added` mark anchored on that step beside it (`added=1`), then `git add .tdd/issue-<n>.md` and `git commit -m "plan: repair the red full suite (#<n>)"`. | The repair is a committed, marked ledger step whose text names what it repairs, and the log shows the plan grew. |
| DP8 | The `⟦suite-repair⟧` and `⟦ci-repair⟧` `add-ledger-step.sh` calls pass no `--criterion` on purpose: the repair step serves its anchor's criterion. | A repair is counted toward the criterion of the step it follows, and adds no criterion of its own. |
| DP9 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 on the `⟦suite-repair⟧` insertion bails with cause `suite-red`, naming every suite still red, exactly as the second-red-run bail does. | A refused repair ends the run with the full list of what is broken. |
| DP10 | A second red result on the full run after a repair step does **bail** — the repair step is one more cycle, not a loop — naming every suite still red, not only the first, with `event.sh bail cause=suite-red classification="$($S/classify-bail.sh suite-red)" suites='<every suite still red>'`, a line that reads nothing from the ledger. | A repair that did not hold ends the run once, with every red suite on the record. |
| DP11 | The cap is one `⟦suite-repair⟧` step per run (a resume starts a new run), and `add-ledger-step.sh`, not the conductor's judgement, decides it: its exit 1 on the insertion is the whole answer, and the conductor does not count repair steps in the ledger itself. | The cap is enforced by one script the same way every time. |
| DP12 | Under `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE=local` the branch's own full run comes first and, with any `⟦suite-repair⟧` step it adds, has to finish green before sub-step 1 runs `rebase-onto-default.sh`; the rebased tip's run there is the extra run a rebase owes, never a replacement for the branch's own. | Where the branch's full run is local, the branch is proven green on its own before it is combined with the default branch. |
| DP13 | A `⟦full-suite⟧` ledger line is the one step no cycle ticks; when a local full run was made before the PR is readied, this step ticks it only once the last such run is green — the rebased tip's run when sub-step 1 rebased the branch under `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE=local`, this step's own run under `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE=local` otherwise, including under `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE=ci` — without `--refactor-pending`, and for each line's `⟦criterion k⟧` ticks the issue criterion it finishes with `tick-issue-criterion.sh`. A red local run ticks nothing, whatever an earlier run said. Under `VERI_BRANCH_SUITE=ci` with no local full run made on the head being readied, the tick is owed to CI as row DP101 states. (The `⟦full-suite⟧` paragraph before sub-step 1.) | A criterion met by "the suite passes" is never ticked on a red run, and a tick pushed on its own after CI would be a new head, cancelling the run being waited on. |
| DP14 | With a stop request pending, this step is not interrupted and runs to completion: once `next-unchecked.sh` exits 3 nothing left launches a leaf or loses work, so no checkpoint runs here; sub-step 4 asks once, clears the request with `$S/stop-requested.sh --clear` and says so in the final comment. | A finished run is not paused at its last step, and a late stop does not outlive it. |
| DP15 | The exception is the repair step a red full run appends: it launches leaves and ends in a ledger commit like any other step, so both checkpoints apply to it as they do in step 3. | A stop requested during a repair is honoured at the repair's own commits. |
| DP16 | Sub-step 1 asks, from inside the worktree and before the PR is readied, `git status --porcelain` and `$S/next-pending-refactor.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md`, and refuses to go on while either says no: a non-empty status is work no verifier has seen (the transform a killed refactor leaf left), an exit 0 is a step checked but still only green; on either, **do not ready the PR**. | A human is asked to look only at a tree every verifier has passed. |
| DP17 | On either answer the run goes to `<base directory>/RESUME.md` sub-step 2 (`Read` it now), whose reset runs before its ask and so discards that work whatever the ask answers, and comes back only once both questions have gone quiet (an empty status, `next-pending-refactor.sh` exiting 3); for a pending step that sub-step also re-enters the step's refactor loop. | The remedy is the one Resume already uses, and a pending step finishes its refactor before the PR. |
| DP18 | With no step pending and none unchecked the reset is the whole remedy: RESUME.md's sub-steps 2 and 3 both exit 3, sub-step 4's `### ▶️ Resuming` comment (`comment.sh resuming --note-file`) names the paths the reset discarded, and the run comes back here and readies the PR — refusing the same tree for ever is not an outcome, discarding it loudly is. | A stray tree never blocks a finished run, and what was discarded is on the record. |
| DP19 | Once both questions have gone quiet, sub-step 1 brings the branch up to the default one with `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh`, after the two questions and before everything sub-step 2's first block does: the `⟦full-suite⟧` ticks owed to CI, the `done` mark, `metrics.sh`, the `metrics:` commit, its push and `gh_pr_ready`. (Sub-step 1, and the order sub-step 2's opening gives.) | The PR is readied, and its record committed, on a branch current with a default branch that may have moved while the run worked. |
| DP20 | `rebase-onto-default.sh`'s exit code decides the next action, so nothing after it — the rebased tip's full test run in particular — is started until it has returned, and never in the same message as the rebase; sub-step 1 says so itself, and the CI-conflict rebase of sub-step 2 says so again. | A rebase that changes nothing or fails never has a full run already racing it. |
| DP21 | An exit 3 from `rebase-onto-default.sh` (the branch already contains `origin/<default>`) continues unchanged to sub-step 2; an exit 2 (no answer, such as an origin that cannot be fetched) **bails** as a **system** failure (`no-verdict`), with the PR left a draft. | A branch already current goes straight on, and an unanswered rebase never readies a PR. |
| DP22 | An exit 0 (the rebase applied cleanly) changed the tree: under `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE=local` the full test command runs once on the rebased tip. A red result there appends one `⟦suite-repair⟧` step through the block the branch's own full run uses, keeps the rebased branch, and keeps `<pre-rebase-sha>` for the later pinned-lease push; only when that insertion exits 1 does it **bail** as a **process** failure (`rebased-tip-red`, not `suite-red`) with the PR left a draft, never readied, and before `<base directory>/BAIL.md`'s push, `git reset --hard <pre-rebase-sha>` puts the branch back, so that push is a fast-forward and the un-rebased branch is what is kept. Under `ci`, sub-step 1 runs no full test command on the rebased tip. | Two green branches that combine red never reach review in `local`, and the repair happens on the tree where the failure exists; a run past its one repair keeps the branch that was green; in `ci`, the CI run on the same commit judges that tree without a lock turn. |
| DP23 | On green, the rewritten history is published with `$S/push-branch.sh --force-with-lease=<branch>:<pre-rebase-sha>`: the lease is pinned to `<pre-rebase-sha>`, so a remote copy another writer moved is refused rather than clobbered; never a bare force. | The run replaces only the remote copy it rebased from. |
| DP24 | An exit 1 (the rebase conflicted and was aborted, HEAD back with a clean tree) prints the conflicting files; resolving them is new code, so a `develop-rebase` leaf, launched through the supervisor, resolves them (prompt `build-develop-prompt.sh rebase` with `<onto-sha>`, `<pre-rebase-sha>` and the conflicting files), and a blind `verify-rebase` judges the resolution before the PR is readied. | A conflict resolution is verified like any other code before review. |
| DP25 | A verify-rebase `REJECT` goes round the revise loop with phase `rebase` (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md`), within `REVISE_BUDGET` like any other phase; a malformed verdict **bails** as a **system** failure (`no-verdict`), and so does `verify.sh`'s exit 2 for a tracked file the leaf left modified and uncommitted, which launches no verifier and names the paths in `.tdd/verify-rebase.err`. | A rejected resolution gets bounded revisions, and no verdict is never read as a pass. |
| DP26 | Only on a verify-rebase `PASS` does the run go on to ready the PR, and first as on exit 0. That one sentence, which names both `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE` modes together (`local` and `ci`) and does so within its first 500 characters, says: under `VERI_REBASED_TIP_SUITE=local` the full test command once on the rebased tip, a red result taking DP22's repair step, or DP22's `rebased-tip-red` bail with `git reset --hard <pre-rebase-sha>` before BAIL.md's push when that insertion exits 1, and on green the push under the lease pinned to `<pre-rebase-sha>`; under `ci`, that push with no full test command before it. | A verified resolution still has to pass the whole suite on the tree it produced, locally or in CI; the mode statement is made once, early, so no reader has to find the `ci` half further down. |
| DP27 | A rebase not resolved within budget — the leaf reports `REBASE: FAILED` or exits 1 or 2, a killed leaf among them, or `REVISE_BUDGET` runs out on verify-rebase `REJECT`s — first runs `git reset --hard <pre-rebase-sha>`, leaving the branch un-rebased, and the run **bails** with cause `rebase-unresolved`, a **process** failure, with the PR left a draft, its bail comment naming every conflicting file `rebase-onto-default.sh` printed. | No unverified resolution stays on the branch, the bail is labelled by its cause, and the human handed the conflict knows where it is. |
| DP361 | Once the `develop-rebase` leaf has resolved a conflict, and before the regrade block and `verify-rebase`, the conductor runs `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh --resolved <onto-sha>`, keeping its stdout as `<replaced-base>`; its exit 2 bails `no-verdict`, a **system** failure. When the rebase is then not resolved within budget (DP27), after `git reset --hard <pre-rebase-sha>` it runs `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh --resolved <replaced-base>` when `--resolved` re-recorded the base. (Step 4 sub-step 1, conflict prose.) | A resolved conflict moves the branch's base; a stop or resume before the verify would otherwise read the old base, and a reset branch would carry a record that is no ancestor of HEAD, which a resume refuses (#4553). |
| DP42 | No candidate — `list` exits 3, and a missing `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` is the `list` exit-3 case — skips the pass with no bail, marked `event.sh structural-pass stopped=nothing-structural` before the PR is readied. | A run with nothing structural goes on, and its log still says the pass had nothing to do. |
| DP43 | Sub-step 2 marks the PR ready, reads its CI, closes the run's log with the PR's URL, renders the metrics, commits them and rewrites the PR, in that order, from inside the worktree; it opens no PR (step 2.3 opened it at the plan commit), and it reads the existing PR's URL with `gh api 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?head={owner}:{branch}'` rather than assuming the run remembers it, so a resumed run edits the PR its branch already has. | One branch has one PR, and the page a human merges is the claim other machines read. |
| DP44 | The PR is readied by running an executable script under `$S/` that calls `gh_pr_ready`, given `<pr-number>` and `"$(git rev-parse HEAD)"`; the conductor never sources a bash library into its own shell. | The conductor's shell may be zsh, where the bash library fails, and the ready-for-review CI run starts on the head the run just pushed. |
| DP45 | The readied PR's CI is read fail-closed, the way the `merge` skill reads it: an empty rollup is a pass only where `wait-ci.sh` exits 0 on it because `VERI_CI_ON_PR=0`, or with the variable unset the target `CLAUDE.md`'s `veri: ci on pr: 0` line, says no check will come or, with neither set, no workflow triggers on `pull_request` (the PR body then carries the no-checks warning line), and otherwise never a pass, a completed check whose conclusion is anything other than `SUCCESS`, `NEUTRAL` or `SKIPPED` counts as failed, and a pending check never counts as green. | A PR is never treated as green on missing or unfinished evidence. |
| DP46 | The wait is `wait-ci.sh`, one bounded slice per Bash call (`$S/wait-ci.sh <pr-url> 540 "" "$(git rev-parse HEAD)"`), given the sha just pushed so a rollup still reporting the previous head reads as pending: its exit 3 (a check still pending) means call it again in a new Bash call; its exit 2 (a check pending past the deadline its first call recorded, checks still queued `VERI_CI_QUEUE_WAIT` seconds after the first call that saw them queued, a ready, mergeable PR whose head has no check run and no commit status for `VERI_CI_NO_RUN_WAIT` seconds (default 600, a non-negative whole number; the grace restarts for each new head) while a `pull_request` workflow exists, its stderr naming `no CI run for <sha>`, or `gh` unable to read the checks until that deadline) **bails** with cause `ci-timeout` rather than waiting on. The wait and the rollup read come after `gh_pr_ready`, which comes after the `done` mark, the `metrics:` commit and their push. (Sub-step 2's first block and the CI-read paragraph after it.) | No single Bash call outlives its limit, the CI read is of the head just pushed, and a hung or never-started CI ends the run instead of stalling it. |
| DP47 | The deadline is `wait-ci.sh`'s own bound, since the call gives no total-seconds: twice the mean of the last 5 completed CI runs, capped at 10 minutes, or 540 s when fewer than 5 exist or `gh` cannot answer. | The wait scales with the repository's real CI time and stays bounded. |
| DP48 | A failed check does not bail: the conductor reads its run's log with `gh run view <run-id> --log-failed`, appends a repair step whose text quotes the failing suite and its `FAIL -` line (or the error line, when the suite died before writing one), commits the ledger, and returns to step 3, *The cycle loop*, without the `done` mark; the quoted line is that step's red evidence, as a red full run's repair step's is. | A CI-only failure gets one verified repair cycle with its evidence in the ledger. |
| DP49 | The CI repair step is appended with `add-ledger-step.sh` after the last done step as `'<fix the CI failure: <suite> — <its FAIL - line>> ⟦ci-repair⟧'`, with an `event.sh step-added` mark beside it, then `git add .tdd/issue-<n>.md` and `git commit -m "plan: repair the red CI check (#<n>)"`. | The CI repair is a marked, committed ledger step the run can recognise later by its mark. |
| DP50 | A second red CI result after a CI repair step **bails** with cause `ci-red`, the way a second red full run does. That bail keys on `⟦ci-repair⟧` only, asked with `$S/ledger-steps.sh has-mark ci-repair < .tdd/issue-<n>.md`: a ledger holding a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step and no `⟦ci-repair⟧` step still gets its one CI repair attempt. | Each kind of repair gets exactly one attempt, told apart by its mark. |

| DP112 | The same sub-step says that a `wait-ci.sh` exit 0 after a `ci-flake.sh` re-run is a flake: the conductor writes `$S/event.sh ci-flake head=<sha> run=<run-id> files=<targets>`, names the targets in the PR body, appends no `⟦ci-repair⟧` step, and goes on as after any green wait; that wait's exit 1 takes the `⟦ci-repair⟧` step or the `ci-red` bail (rows DP50, DP51), and its exit 2 and 3 are handled as at any wait. Neither a re-run nor a `⟦ci-repair⟧` step returns the readied PR to draft. | A green re-run is CI's proof; a red one is a real red with its one repair, and a re-run is counted by its own budget, never by the repair step's. |
| DP113 | A conductor re-runs a failed CI job only through `$S/ci-flake.sh`, at most once per head and `VERI_CI_FLAKE_RERUNS` times per run (default 1; `0` re-runs none), and only after every test target the failed log names has passed alone; it never runs `gh run rerun` otherwise, and a re-run that is red again is a real red. | A real failure never reads as a flake: a red that reproduces locally is never re-run, and a re-run that is red again is real. |
| DP109 | A run is stacked where `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` exists and `$S/stack-branch.sh` exits 0 printing the name it holds; any other `stack-branch.sh` answer for a run holding the record bails `no-verdict`. A stacked run's sub-step 2 runs, instead of its two blocks, a numbered list of lines, each in a Bash call of its own, each naming its command and what its exits route to, none reading a variable another line set: no `pr-ready.sh` and no `wait-ci.sh` call, before or after the `metrics:` push; no `⟦full-suite⟧` tick and no `⟦ci-repair⟧` step; the `WIP:` prefix dropped from the title without readying it; and the run ends ✅ Done with its pull request a draft. Every other part of the step runs as for a run that is not stacked. | PRD 034 D4, D7, D9: CI on the stack's tip is the full suite, so no full suite ran for the run alone, and the draft keeps the claim, Done detection and the dispatcher reading the pull request as before; an agent's Bash calls share no variables, so a skipped line must not leave another reading an unset one. |
| DP110 | In a stacked run, `origin/<default>` in the step's rebases and in the regrade block's `git merge-base HEAD origin/<default>` means `origin/<stack>`. | A stacked run's base is the stack; rebasing or diffing against the default branch would carry every joined but unlanded commit into the run's own diff. |
| DP114 | At the red full run's repair-step insertion and at both `⟦ci-repair⟧` insertions of step 4 sub-step 2, an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused`, `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again, and at the repair-step insertion neither a `step-added` nor a `step-start` event is written. (Each insertion's `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 line.) | Every site of the cause; the bail is the one it was. |
| DP115 | Sub-step 1, after the structural pass (or its skip) and before `refactor-candidates.sh`, each time the run enters the step, runs the command `$S/detect-test-command.sh --tier lint` prints once at the worktree root through `$S/detach-command.sh start lint` and `$S/detach-command.sh wait lint`; on `--tier lint` exit 3 it runs nothing, writes no event and adds no PR body section. | Farley: fast feedback, once per delivery, on the code the PR ships; a repo that declares no lint command is never guessed at (PRD 032, LN2, LN3, C11). |
| DP116 | After the lint run the conductor writes `event.sh lint exit=<code>`, and the PR body written in sub-step 2 has a `### Lint` section naming the command and, on a non-zero exit, its exit code and the last 20 lines of its output, each cut at 300 characters. | The reviewer and the run log both see it (PRD 032, LN4). |
| DP117 | A non-zero lint exit never bails the run and never holds the PR: at most it appends the one repair step DP228 describes. | Reporting is PRD 032.7's capability, repairing PRD 032.10's (PRD 032, LN5, C12). |
| DP208 | *4. Draft PR* sub-step 2 runs `$S/ledger-growth.sh split .tdd/issue-<n>.md` when it composes the PR body and, on exit 0, puts its output under a `## Plan growth` heading placed before `## Owed steps`, saying the owner is strongly advised to split the work after the criterion it names; on exit 3 it adds no such section; on exit 2 the section holds the one line `plan growth could not be measured: ledger-growth.sh split exited 2`. No exit of it bails the run, holds the PR back or asks for anything: a plan that grew went ahead, and this only reports it. | The owner allowed the growth and wants no hard ceiling and no wait (#4075 follow-up); the PR body is what the human deciding the merge reads, so the growth and the place to split reach him there. |
| DP158 | Sub-step 1 runs `$S/red-setup.sh worst <n> .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` after the structural pass (or its skip) and before `refactor-candidates.sh`; exit 0 and exit 3 go on, and exit 2 bails `no-verdict` as a system failure. | The candidates issue is filed from the owed file in that call (PRD 032, TY8). |
| DP219 | The full suite run before readying, when `branch-suite.sh` does not leave it to CI, is wrapped in `event.sh stage-start stage=full-suite` before it and `event.sh stage-end stage=full-suite status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` after it, whatever its exit; a run left to CI writes neither. | A suite CI runs is CI's time, and a stage never run must not show as one. |
| DP188 | Sub-step 1, after the structural pass (or its skip) and before `refactor-candidates.sh`, unless `.tdd/issue-<n>-test-quality` exists, launches one `develop-test-quality` leaf through the supervisor, prompted by `build-develop-prompt.sh test-quality` with the spec, the test command and the test files `git diff --name-only <branch-base>..HEAD` names that `case_reader.is_test_path` accepts and that still exist (as `test-quality.sh files` prints them; none launches no leaf and writes the record empty), then runs `$S/test-quality.sh record <n>` on its report; exit 2 bails `no-verdict` as a system failure. | Once per run; a resume re-entering the step does not pay it again (PRD 032, TQ1, TQ3). |
| DP189 | When the `develop-test-quality` leaf leaves a changed working tree or a commit, the conductor resets to the HEAD it recorded before the launch and still records the leaf's report. | The leaf has no verifier, so it may change nothing (PRD 032, TQ4, C4). |
| DP190 | The PR body written in sub-step 2 has a `### Test quality` section listing each finding `.tdd/issue-<n>-test-quality` records with its kind, case and why, or saying none was found. | The reviewer is the reader (PRD 032, TQ5). |
| DP191 | After `test-quality.sh record` exits 0, for each recorded `weak-assertion` or `implementation-coupled` finding, in recorded order and up to `VERI_TEST_QUALITY_FIXES`, the conductor appends one step with `add-ledger-step.sh … after` the last done step carrying exactly one `⟦criterion k⟧` tag, as `test-quality.sh anchor` prints it, whose text names the case, the kind and the why, says it edits only that case and no production code, and carries `⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦test-quality⟧`; it commits the ledger and returns to the cycle loop. A `redundant` or `fixed-wait` finding gets no step, and a resume appends none for a finding whose case a `⟦test-quality⟧` step of the committed ledger already names. (Sub-step 1, the *Test-quality fixes* paragraph.) | Each fix its own step under a blind verifier; a redundant case is a reviewer's call, and removing a fixed wait can need a production signal a test-only step cannot add; the committed ledger is the source of truth (PRD 032, TX1, TX2, TX8, C5, C17). |
| DP192 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 on such an append, and `test-quality.sh anchor` exit 3 (no done step with one criterion tag, or a ledger step carrying two), leave the finding unfixed and write no bail; `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 bails `step-add-refused`. | A quality fix is never worth losing a finished run (PRD 032, TX6). |
| DP193 | The `### Test quality` section marks each finding `fixed` with the fixing commit's subject, or `not fixed` with why: `redundant`, `fixed-wait`, `budget`, `step growth` or `no anchor`. (Sub-step 2, the PR body.) | The reader sees what the run did and did not do (PRD 032, TX7); a `fixed-wait` finding is never fixed by the run, so the section is the only place it is acted on (#4578). |
| DP178 | After `refactor-candidates.sh` and before the PR is readied, the conductor runs `$S/unwired.sh <branch-base>`: exit 1 bails with cause `unwired`, naming every file it printed, and leaves the PR a draft; exits 0 and 3 go on; exit 2 bails `no-verdict` as a system failure. | Code nothing reaches is not delivered (PRD 032, WN4, C9). |
| DP179 | Sub-step 1, after the structural pass (or its skip) and before `refactor-candidates.sh`, runs `$S/coupling.sh <branch-base>`; on exit 0 the PR body written in sub-step 2 has a `### Coupling` section holding every line it printed; exit 3 drops any `coupling` row an earlier entry recorded (`$S/refactor-owed.sh clear .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md coupling`) and goes on; exit 2 bails `no-verdict` as a system failure. | The reviewer sees what the run's change left mutually dependent, and a resume leaves no stale candidate (PRD 032, CU2, CU3). |
| DP180 | On `coupling.sh` exit 0 the conductor records exactly one row, `$S/refactor-owed.sh not-done .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md 'mutual reference: <a> <b>' coupling`, for the pair whose two files the range changed the most lines in (`git diff --numstat`, added plus deleted), the first in byte order on a tie; a resume replaces that row rather than adding one. Upward imports are not reported. | At most one candidate per run, the worst (PRD 032, CU3, CU4, C10). |
| DP181 | The conductor runs `unwired.sh` with `--accepted .tdd/issue-<n>-unwired-accepted`; on exit 1 it appends the printed files to `.tdd/issue-<n>-unwired-pending`, never to the accepted record, before the `unwired` bail.
