A resume without a release bails `unwired` again; after the human's `release-issue.sh` the resumed run goes on when every file it would print is accepted and bails `unwired` again when one is not. | A file wired by framework discovery is never named by stem; only the release is a human accepting it, since a resume-once relaunches without one (PRD 032, WN4). |
| DP228 | On a non-zero lint exit the conductor appends one step after the last done step with `$S/add-ledger-step.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md after …`, whose text `$S/lint-repair.sh step < .tdd/lint.out` composes in the same Bash call (the lint command, its output's first finding line capped at 200 characters, local paths, host and account redacted; its exit 3, no lint command declared, appends no step and writes no bail, and its exit 2 bails `no-verdict` as a system failure) and which carries `⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦lint-repair⟧`, commits it as `plan: repair the lint findings (#<n>)`, and returns to the cycle loop; `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 bails `step-add-refused`. | Repair what the run reports, in the same shape as the red full suite's repair step (PRD 032, LR1, LR5). |
| DP229 | A non-zero lint exit after the `⟦lint-repair⟧` step's cycle, or an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 on the append, appends no step and writes no bail; the `### Lint` section says the command is not clean after one repair, or that no repair step could be added. | A lint finding is not a broken product, and a bail at the last step loses a finished run (PRD 032, LR4, C12). |
| DP338 | At the start of the step, before its opening paragraphs settle the branch-suite mode, the conductor is told to run `$S/branch-gate.sh <n>` first, to call it again on exit 3, to take `branch_suite=` from its last lines on exit 0, and on any other exit to continue by the opening paragraphs from the point its last line names; every sentence of those paragraphs stays. | The branch's suite gate becomes one request (PRD 027.7), and the prose remains the path for everything not clean (PRD 027 D4). |
| DP339 | At the start of sub-step 1, the conductor is told to run `$S/pr-prepare.sh <n>` first, to call it again on exit 3, on exit 0 to take `rebased`, `rebased_from` and `rebased_tip_suite` from its last lines and go on from the lint run (the structural pass skipped), and on any other exit to continue by sub-step 1 from the point its last line names; every sentence of sub-step 1 stays. | Sub-step 1's fixed calls up to the structural-pass skip become one request (PRD 027.9), and the prose remains the path for everything not clean and everything after the skip (PRD 027 D4). |
| DP348 | At the start of sub-step 2's first block, the conductor is told to run `$S/pr-ready-wait.sh <n>` first, to call it again on exit 3, to take `pr_url`, `pr_number`, `ci=`, `metrics=` (only when the render failed) and `no_checks=` from its last lines on exit 0, and on any other exit to continue by that block from the point its last line names, the `pr-ready-failed` bail included; that a stacked run never calls it (exit 2 with `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` present) and that a missing ledger or a failing `git` or `gh` call inside it is its exit 2; every sentence of the block stays. (Sub-step 2's opening.) | Closing the run's record, readying the PR and its first CI wait become one request per slice (PRD 027.10, #4332), and the prose remains the path for everything not clean (PRD 027 D4). |
| DP349 | At the start of sub-step 2's second block, reached after the first `wait-ci.sh` exits 0, the conductor is told to run `$S/pr-finish.sh <n>`, that it never exits 3, to take `.tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed-render` for the body from its last lines on exit 0, and on any other exit to continue by that block from the point its last line names; that it only reads the PR's url and renders, with no commit, no push and no CI wait; that a stacked run never calls it (exit 2 with `.tdd/issue-<n>-base` present) and that a failing `git` or `gh` call is its exit 2; the PR's title and body stay the conductor's, and every sentence of the block stays. (The paragraph before sub-step 2's second block.) | The render becomes one request (PRD 027.11) that leaves the readied head as CI read it, and the prose remains the path for everything not clean (PRD 027 D4). |
| DP350 | At the start of sub-step 4, the conductor is told to run `$S/run-done.sh <n>` first, that it never exits 3, to take its values from its last lines on exit 0, and on any other exit to continue by sub-step 4 from the point its last line names, and that its tree check before the comment leaves out `.tdd/issue-<n>-metrics.md` when `.tdd/issue-<n>-pr-ready-wait` holds a non-zero `metrics=`; every sentence of sub-step 4 stays. | The done comment becomes one request (PRD 027.12), and the prose remains the path for everything not clean (PRD 027 D4). |
| DP351 | Sub-step 1's lint wait is bounded in total: `detach-command.sh wait lint` runs under the bound `VERI_LINT_TIMEOUT` (default 1800 seconds, counted from the lint's `start`), and when the bound expires with the command still running the command is stopped, no `lint` event is written, and `.tdd/issue-<n>-lint.md` holds `### Lint` followed by a line saying `<command>` did not finish within the bound and was stopped; this neither bails the run nor holds the PR. | A lint command that never exits would otherwise keep the conductor calling `wait` every 590 seconds for the life of the run, and the PR would never be readied. |
| DP352 | The PR body spec in sub-step 2's second block carries, when `$S/pr-ready-wait.sh` printed `no_checks=1`, the line '⚠️ CI ran no checks on the readied head: the ⟦full-suite⟧ ticks were owed to CI and nothing proved them.' (Sub-step 2's second block, the body heredoc.) | The pre-readying ticks are not proof: a head CI ran nothing on is named to the human deciding the merge (#4332). |
| DP353 | Sub-step 2's opening states that the `done` mark, which every reader of a run's end (`run-finished.sh`, the run's activity, the conductor's needs-user check) takes as the run finished, is written only once CI's first wait is green and is never committed or pushed; the second block's first line writes it (`VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor $S/event.sh done pr=<pr-url>`), before the refactor-owed render, and a `⟦ci-repair⟧` step's cycle returns to step 3 without it. (Sub-step 2's opening, its second block, and the failed-check paragraph.) | A run whose first CI wait is pending, or whose conductor died in it, is never read as done or safe to merge, and a dead conductor there still gets the needs-user bail (#4332). |
| DP354 | Sub-step 1, right after the regrade block, says that the `GRADED` files the block modified, and only those, are committed as `docs: regrade the prose the branch signed (#<n>)`, with or without a rebase, and nothing is committed when it modified none. | The regrade had no named commit, so runs made it by hand under different subjects (PRD 038.7, PRG1, PRG4). |
| DP355 | Sub-step 1, where the lint run begins, tells the conductor to run `$S/pr-checks.sh <n>` after `pr-prepare.sh`, again on exit 3, and on exit 1 or 2 to go on by the check its last line names; the prose of the lint run, the test-quality check, `red-setup.sh worst`, `coupling.sh`, `refactor-candidates.sh` and `unwired.sh` stays. | One call runs the checks, and the prose stays the path for everything not clean (PRD 038.8, PCK6). |
| DP356 | The regrade block in sub-step 1 hands `grade-prose.sh` each unit it lists decoded (a `%20` in the `GRADED` field back to a space, a `%25` back to `%`) and finds its `REQUIREMENTS.md` from the part before `#`, and a `GRADED` line present at the merge-base is not listed as one the branch signed. (Sub-step 1's regrade block and the paragraph after it.) | A heading unit's field is `%`-encoded in `GRADED`, so the raw name made `grade-prose.sh` exit 2 and bailed run #4391 at Draft PR. |
| DP360 | `DRAFT-PR.md`'s opening line names `$S/run-pr.sh <n>` in place of `branch-gate.sh`'s: run it, again on exit 3, at the `title` seam set the title and call it again, at the `body` seam write the body and go on to sub-step 3, and on any other stop go on by the point its last line names. Every stage's call line stays. | One call runs the stages and the prose stays the fallback (RPR11, S8). |

## skills/tdd/SUITE-REPAIR.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SR1 | Before the Red of an unchecked `⟦suite-repair⟧` step, the conductor runs the full suite once on the current tip; if it is green, the step is ticked as retired (a `suite-repair-retired` event naming the step, the ledger line ticked and committed), no leaf is launched for it, and the run continues at the Draft PR step; if it is red, the step proceeds to Red as today. | A transient red retires its own repair step, so a resume never bails on a step with nothing to test. |
| SR2 | *Before the Red of a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step*: when the pre-check's full run is red, the conductor first runs each test file the red output names alone once, with the repo's own runner. When all of them pass, it writes a `suite-flake` event naming the files and retires the step exactly as the Green branch does. When any stays red, or the output names no file, the step proceeds to Red as today. | A pre-check that flakes under load has no defect to repair, and must not end the run on a `needs-user` bail. |
| CY135 | A `⟦suite-repair⟧` step whose Red leaf changed test files only, with every suite the step names then passing and no case deleted and no assertion removed, finishes without a red bar: no verify-red, no revise leaf, no `revise-impossible` bail. A step whose diff touches a production file takes the ordinary Red unchanged. | A fixture-only repair cannot fail against unchanged production code; the run on #3099 bailed `revise-impossible` on exactly that and a human had to retire the step. |
| SR4 | When a `⟦suite-repair⟧` step is retired, retiring also deletes that step's mutation records in `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` whose test no longer exists, as `tick-ledger.sh` does for `--unpinnable` and `--already-holds`. | A pin for a test the retired step never wrote stays behind and makes `pin-recheck.sh` print "base run failed" on every later PR touching the pinned file. |

## skills/tdd/PREFLIGHT.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PF1 | Preflight runs `leaf-launchable.sh` before the baseline suite. | A suite, worktree, spec and public `Resuming` comment are wasted if no leaf can launch (#187). |
| PF2 | Preflight checks that `gh api user` (the REST route) succeeds and that `claude auth status` reports `"loggedIn": true`. | An expired session makes every `claude -p` leaf fail at launch, which has no bail classification. |
| PF3 | The fast-tier baseline runs inside the issue's worktree, after `issue-worktree.sh` has cut the branch and the conductor has entered it. The caller's checkout is never what is proven. | The baseline must prove the commit the cycles build on. |
| PF4 | The fast-tier baseline runs in preflight, before the plan section. When every red test target (a failing pytest node id, else a test file) passes when run alone once with the repo's own runner (`make test TESTS="<target>"` on veri), preflight writes a `suite-flake` event naming the targets and goes on as on a green baseline. | A cycle must not start on a red baseline, but a load flake is no defect of the baseline, and refusing it can deadlock the fix for that very flake. |
| PF5 | `run_conductor.py` claims the run's worktree `.tdd/conductor.pid` with its own pid and start time before the conductor's preflight runs; the forked `claude -p` child's `record_pid` then overwrites it with the child's own pid and its own start time, and on exit the launcher removes the file only when it still names this conductor. The conductor itself never writes it. The lock is taken in preflight step 3, never in step 1.2. A launch whose `.tdd/conductor.pid` already names a live process, started at the time the file records (or with no time recorded), refuses before it writes any run record or runs preflight, so a second launch seconds after the first is refused while the first is still in preflight: exit 2, `already live (pid <pid>)` on stderr, the pid file unchanged. A file naming a dead pid is taken over. | `$PPID` from a Bash call names whichever claude ran it, which outlives an interactive run. Only the launcher knows the process whose lifetime is the run's (#2249). The worktree guard still has to cover the window before the baseline (#170). A second launch overwriting a live run's lock is the defect #3633 fixes; a claim taken only after preflight let two launches seconds apart both run (#3774). |
| PF6 | The run's test command comes from `detect-test-command.sh --tier fast --issue "$body" --at <base>`, `<base>` the run's recorded branch base, on a resume as on a fresh launch. The issue body is read into a temp file before the baseline, so the baseline and every later leaf use one command. The baseline item runs and times that command. | Detecting after the baseline would be too late to pin the issue's own test line. |
| PF7 | The baseline is timed once, as `baseline`: elapsed wall time in whole seconds, rounded up, taken once so a suite that slows mid-run does not move the timeout. Then `full` is settled once: under `VERI_BASELINE_FULL_TIER=reuse`, from `$S/full-tier-time.sh` when it holds a time for this run's full-tier command; otherwise the full tier is timed once (whole seconds, rounded up), run once through `detach-command.sh` like the baseline, with the same single-measurement rule, and a green run records its time through `$S/full-tier-time.sh`. Step 7 sizes the timeout from `full`, not `baseline`, because the full tier is the work a leaf actually does. A red full tier whose red test targets (a failing pytest node id, else a test file) each pass when run alone (`--alone`), as for the baseline in PF4, is a flake: a `suite-flake` event names the targets, and preflight goes on. | Leaves run the full tier, so a timeout sized from the fast tier bounds work nobody does (#300). A load flake in the full tier is no defect of the baseline (#2912). |
| PF8 | `leaf-timeout.sh "$baseline" --full "$full"` is called only after the full-tier measurement. `--full` sizes the timeout from the full-tier number, which displaces the fast one. | See PF7. |
| PF9 | The settled timeout is recorded in `.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout`, and `supervise.sh` reads it when `VERI_TIMEOUT` is unset. The prose never claims an export in the conductor's own shell is in force for later launches. | Every Bash call is a fresh shell, so an export does not survive. Prose that said otherwise was fixed in #954. |
| PF10 | `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` must be unset or a whole number ≥ 0, set-but-empty refused; `VERI_STEP_GROWTH` is retired and must be unset: any value, empty included, is refused, naming `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`. | A bad value caught here costs nothing. Caught later it surfaces mid-cycle as a refused plan insertion; a value set for the old absolute count would otherwise be silently ignored. |
| PF11 | Preflight runs `step-budget.sh` with no arguments, beside `refactor-budget.sh 0`. | A budget that cannot be read is a poisoned baseline. |
| PF12 | Preflight calls `leaf-model.sh` with a developer name, a revise name and a verifier name, once each. | Each of `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER`, `VERI_MODEL_REVISE` and `VERI_MODEL_VERIFIER` is read only for its own name, so skipping a name lets its bad value through. |
| PF13 | An empty or whitespace-only model variable counts as unset and never stops the run. | A weaker rule would stop runs on blank variables (#1485); a value with whitespace cannot be a model name. |
| PF14 | A run that `issue-worktree.sh` handed back an existing worktree — whether or not its ledger exists — runs `rebase-onto-default.sh` before its baseline; only a run on a freshly cut worktree does not rebase there. | The baseline must run on the rebased tip (#1297), and a worktree left by an attempt that bailed before its ledger is as stale as any other (#2460). |
| PF15 | Exit 3 of the resume rebase goes on unchanged. Exit 1 goes on from the old base and leaves the conflict to the Draft PR step. | The three exits are different outcomes and must not be collapsed. |
| PF16 | `<pre-rebase-sha>` is introduced as the first stdout line of `rebase-onto-default.sh`, before it is first used. | The lease push and the reset both depend on it. |
| PF17 | A resume rebase that moved the branch is marked in the run log as its own event (`resume-rebase`, carrying `from=<pre-rebase-sha>` and `rebased=<HEAD>`). Its name is not one the Draft PR step's rebase writes. | The two rebases must be told apart in the log. |
| PF18 | Before the resume rebase, every handed-back worktree's dirty-tree discard captures `git status --porcelain` into `$stray`, then runs `git reset --hard HEAD`. `$stray` is kept for the `RESUME.md` sub-step 4 comment. | A rebase refuses a dirty tree, and the resuming comment must name what was discarded. |
| PF19 | A resume rebase that moved the branch is published only after the baseline is green, with `push-branch.sh --force-with-lease=<branch>:<pre-rebase-sha>`. The negation attaches to the bare force: a bare force is never used. | A pinned lease refuses the push when the remote has moved since the run rebased from it. |
| PF22 | Check 1: `git rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` succeeds and `git status --porcelain` is empty in the caller's checkout. The check stays although the run works in a worktree. | The conductor still reads the issue and the default branch from the caller's checkout, and a dirty one means something else is mid-work in it. |
| PF23 | Check 4: the default branch is captured for the PR base with `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} -q .default_branch`. | The PR must target the repository's real default branch, not an assumed `main`. |
| PF24 | `refactor-budget.sh 0` runs once: exit 0 and exit 3 both mean it parses (3 only says the budget is 0, the refactor loop switched off). | Catching it here is what lets the refactor loop treat exit 2 as impossible. |
| PF25 | `refactor-budget.sh --issue 0` runs once and checks `VERI_ISSUE_REFACTOR_BUDGET`, the structural pass's budget, the same way: exit 0 and 3 both mean it parses. | The structural pass's budget gets the same early check. |
| PF26 | `step-budget.sh`'s exit 0 prints the step budget, then the run budget, one per line. | Spells out which two money budgets PF11's call checks, and what a pass prints. |
| PF27 | The `stop-requested.sh --clear` beside the lock write drops a stale stop request a killed run never got to clear, so a `.tdd/stop` left behind cannot stop the run that resumes it at its first checkpoint. | Otherwise a resume stops itself at its first checkpoint. |
| PF28 | The handed-back run, its uncommitted `.tdd/` appends kept and its ledger ticks reconciled when a ledger exists, runs `$S/rebase-onto-default.sh` before its baseline, so the baseline runs on the rebased tip. | The run must test a tip current with the default branch. |
| PF29 | The working ledger is judged against the committed ledger by step text, counted: a working-copy `- [x]` line stays ticked only when the committed ledger holds a tick of the same step text (without its trailing markers), so n committed ticks of a text keep at most n working ticks of it; every other `- [x]` line is unticked and loses its `⟂ refactor pending` mark, and every other working-copy line is kept as written. | The committed ledger is the source of truth for done steps; a tick whose green commit is not on the branch would skip work on resume. |
| PF31 | When the target repo's CLAUDE.md declares `veri: setup: <cmd>`, preflight, before the baseline, runs `$S/worktree-setup.sh <n>` inside the run worktree on every start and every resume, with no condition on the ledger or on the last bail; the script runs `<cmd>` at most once per worktree. Exit 3 (no such line, or setup already done in this worktree) leaves preflight unchanged. | A nested package's dependencies are otherwise never installed in a run worktree, so a run's suite passes while the merge gate fails on a missing module (gs-marketplace); a worktree re-created for an existing branch gets its ledger back but not its dependencies, so no ledger or bail condition may skip it. |
| PF33 | Preflight checks `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` the way `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` is checked, with `[[ "${VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET:-3}" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]`. It is a seventh budget axis, never folded into `REVISE_BUDGET`, `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES`, `VERI_NO_VERDICT_RETRIES`, `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET`, `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` or `VERI_SKILL_EDIT_BUDGET`. | A budget that cannot be read is a poisoned baseline (PF11); caught here it costs nothing. |
| DP84 | Preflight removes a `.tdd/ci-conflict-rebased` an earlier run left, so DP54's one CI-conflict rebase is counted per run, never inherited by a resume. | A resume otherwise bails `ci-conflict` on its first conflict over a rebase an earlier run spent (#2194). |
| PF34 | After a resume rebase exits 0 and its `resume-rebase` event is logged, and before the baseline, the conductor runs `$S/tests-frozen.sh rerecord-rebased .tdd/issue-<n>.md <pre-rebase-sha>`. Exit 0 or 3 goes on. | A rebase moves upstream's edits under earlier steps' records; refreshing only the cases the branch left alone lets upstream move without ever erasing a finding about the branch. The refusal has two causes, and a message naming one sends the reader to look for a conflict that is not there. |
| PF37 | `detach-command.sh wait` answers 0 when the command passed and 3 while it is still running (call again). (Step 3, the baseline.) | The conductor branches on these four answers. |
| PF38 | The conductor waits with `$S/detach-command.sh wait <name>` in the foreground with no max-seconds argument and the Bash tool's `timeout` of 600000 ms, calling `wait` again each time it returns 3, and never backgrounds it and ends the turn. (Step 3, the baseline.) | A fixed wait would end the turn on a slow suite. |
| PF35 | Step 3 says `VERI_BASELINE_FULL_TIER` takes `reuse`, the default, or `run`, and tells the conductor to run the script that settles it and follow its output: under `reuse` with a usable recorded time from `$S/full-tier-time.sh` no more than `VERI_FULL_TIER_MAX_AGE_DAYS` days old (default 7), `full` is that time and the full tier is not run; a time older than that, or with no recorded age, is not reused, and otherwise the full tier runs, as above, its green run recording the new time. The fast-tier baseline runs in both modes. | `reuse` was prose only and a conductor ran the full tier anyway (#3436); step 3 must name the script that settles it, and a time that only grows older must not size every timeout. |
| PF39 | Step 5 (the tunables check) checks `VERI_SKIP_VERIFY`: each comma-separated word must be a phase this release can skip, and `plan` is never one. | A bad list must stop the run before it spends anything. |
| PF59 | When a preflight check refuses (a failing check, a red baseline or full tier, or any exit the check names as a stop or a bail), PREFLIGHT.md sends the run to `<base directory>/PREFLIGHT-FAILURES.md`, which it `Read`s then and not before, and runs; a preflight that passes never reads it. | Preflight's failure branches are read only by the runs whose preflight fails (#3152 measured the conductor carrying PREFLIGHT.md, 18,664 bytes, from turn 2 of every run); a refusal handled without the file would stop without its `preflight-refused` event and comment. |
| PF60 | The resume exception states that when every unit the red is explained by has a pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` step but a non-skill-edit unchecked step separates them, `suite-red-resume.sh insert` inserts no `⟦suite-repair⟧` step and exits 2 with the reorder named. | A repair step cannot regrade a signed unit; the refusal names the one reorder that unblocks the run. |
| PF62 | Preflight checks the issue's `## Requirements` rows against the units' lists, and each `⟦skill-edit⟧` step's unit against the issue's `### <path>` tables, and refuses with exit 2 naming every defect at once, before any leaf is launched. | A defect found at its skill-edit step costs a bail, a resume and an operator round trip each (#3257: 3 bails; #3550: 1). |
| PF63 | Step 5 (Tunables parse) checks `VERI_ADJUDICATE_BUDGET` as a non-negative integer. A bad value exits 2 before the baseline, naming it. | A bad budget stops the run before it spends anything. |
| PF64 | Before its step 1, `PREFLIGHT.md` says `SKILL.md` step 0 ran `$S/preflight.sh <n> <slug>` before this file was read, and that the file is read only when the script exits neither 0 nor 3: a fresh run and a resume alike continue from the step the script's last line names by the steps below. It does not say a resume runs steps 1 to 7 without the script. Steps 1 to 7 stay stated. | The prose remains the path for everything that is not clean (PRD 027 D4); since PRD 027.2 the script runs the resume path too, and three resumed sessions of #3793 called the script a second time. |
| PF65 | Step 5 checks `VERI_EFFORT_VERIFIER`: unset, empty, or one of `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or `max` passes; any other value stops the run at step 5 (`stopped at 5 VERI_EFFORT_VERIFIER`, exit 2) before any leaf. (Step 5, after `VERI_SKIP_VERIFY`.) | The CLI ignores an unknown effort with only a warning, so a conductor re-running step 5 by hand must find the check that stopped it (PRD 024.8). |
| PF66 | Step 2 says preflight runs `$S/ready-check.sh --no-suites` on the target repo after the authentication and launch checks and before step 3 creates the issue's worktree; a failing `fast tier`, `all tier`, `pull_request`, `gh auth`, `push access` or `label <VERI_LABEL's value>` row stops the run, and any other failing row is printed and the run goes on. | A resume runs steps 1 to 7 from this prose, not from the script; the cheap checks come before the expensive baseline. |
| PF68 | Step 2 says preflight passes `--no-permissions` to `ready-check.sh` when `.tdd/permissions-checked` in the main working tree holds the current gh login, a sha256 of `origin`'s current URL (never the URL) and the check's current version and `VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS` is unset or `0`, printing `permission rows skipped: checked <date>`; that a missing or stale record, or `VERI_RECHECK_PERMISSIONS=1`, runs the permission rows; and that the record is written only after a check in which no permission row failed. | A resume runs step 2 from this prose; the probes, the paid call included, are paid once per clone. |
| PF69 | Step 3 says that on a resume the conductor writes `event.sh stage-start stage=baseline` before the baseline run and `event.sh stage-end stage=baseline status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` after it, whatever its exit, the code being the baseline run's exit code. (Step 3.) | A resume's baseline counts in the metrics as a fresh run's does, and a red one must show as failed, not missing. |
| PF70 | Step 3 skips the baseline (fast tier and full tier) when either source is green and runs it otherwise: (1) CI's `CI` workflow concluded `success` in a `push` run on the exact commit sha being baselined, read through `gh` (a `pull_request` run, which tests the merge commit, never counts); (2) a record on this machine of a green baseline keyed by that tree's `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}`, the baseline command, the skill commit pin and the dev venv lock hash. A CI result that is red, pending, absent or unreadable, a commit CI ran whose tree differs from the tree under test, and a record that is red, absent, corrupt or keyed differently each run the baseline; only a green baseline writes a record. | A baseline re-proving a commit already proven green cost 422 s in #4316 and 1113 s over 3 preflights in #3832, and a gate that skips on a doubtful answer would let a red tree start cycles. |
| PF71 | Step 3 states the accepted trade-off beside the CI source: CI is Linux and the machine is macOS, so a test failing only locally can slip past the gate; the owner accepted it, and the local record is the second source for a tip CI never ran. | A reader who sees the skip must also see what it gives up and who accepted it. |
| PF72 | Step 3 says the fast command is read at the run's recorded branch base: a fresh launch writes the record once the worktree is cut, as the merge base with `origin/<stack>` when the cut recorded a stack, and stops with exit 2 writing nothing when the merge base cannot be read; a resume reads it back and stops with exit 2 when it is missing or invalid. | A resume must not let a rewritten ref choose the base. |

## skills/tdd/PIN.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PN1 | Intro paragraph: a step whose criterion earlier work already satisfies has no failing test to write, because the honest test passes against the unmodified code. That is an outcome of the step, not a failure of it: the behaviour exists and what it wants is a regression pin. The conductor reaches this section from Red when the developer leaf reported `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` and it ran `$S/verify.sh pin …` in place of verify-red; the verify-pin verdict replaces the `### 🔴 Red` / `### 🔎 verify-red` pair that `$S/comment.sh <n> red` and `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-red` would otherwise post. | A criterion the code already meets is pinned and finished, not forced through a failing test or bailed on. |
| PN2 | `⟦replaces-case⟧` paragraph: a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step whose Red reports `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` takes a different path after verify-pin, because deleting the named bash cases is still owed. Such a step skips verify-red, so on that verify-pin PASS the conductor first runs `$S/tests-frozen.sh record --step "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" <test-path>` to record its replacement cases before the deletion green checks them; `tests-frozen.sh` exit 2 is a bail. | The deletion green is checked against a recorded set of replacement cases, and a record that cannot be written ends the run rather than letting the deletion go unchecked. |
| PN3 | `⟦replaces-case⟧` paragraph: after the record the conductor commits nothing yet and launches a green leaf exactly as *Green* does, whose only change is deleting exactly the named cases. By then the unreplaced-cases check from *Red* has bailed with `cause=unreplaced-cases` only on a second exit 1 after its one correction leaf, or on exit 2. | Nothing is committed or ticked before the deletion is judged, and the deletion touches no case beyond the named ones. |
| PN4 | `⟦replaces-case⟧` paragraph: after the deletion green the conductor runs `$S/replaces-case-survivors.sh "<step text>"` before that verify-green launches. On exit 1 it relaunches the green leaf naming the survivors, as *Green* does, records the relaunch as an event (`$S/event.sh`), and launches no verifier on that green. Each relaunch spends one `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, and when it is spent the run bails with `cause=revise-exhausted`. | A named case that survived the deletion is fixed by a bounded redo and is never judged by a verifier. |
| PN5 | `⟦replaces-case⟧` paragraph: the deletion is judged by the replaces-case verify-green, whose PASS is accepted only once `$S/mutation-parity.sh <pairs-table>` on that step's pairs table exits 0, as on *Green*'s verify. The conductor then commits the pytest cases, the deletion and the ticked ledger in one tick-and-commit, as *Green*'s PASS does. That tick carries `--refactor-pending`, so the ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark follows the deletion green and the Refactor phase runs on the step as after any Green. | A deletion is accepted only when the replacement cases kill every mutant the deleted ones did, lands as one commit, and its new code is refactored like any green's. |
| PN6 | PASS (exit 0) bullet: on a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step the conductor takes the deletion path (PN2 to PN5) instead; on any other step it commits the test as the pin, ticking the ledger atomically. The shape is the same tick-and-commit as Green, but the commit carries the test alone and the `test:` type, because no production code was written: the verifier reverted its own mutation, so the tree holds only the new test and the ticked ledger. | A pin never carries production code, and the tree the commit takes is exactly the test and the ledger tick. |
| PN7 | PASS (exit 0) bullet: before the pin's tick-and-commit the conductor runs `$S/pin-fast-tier.sh '<fast-cmd>'`. On exit 1 it first runs `$S/rerun-alone.sh '<alone-cmd>' .tdd/verify-pin.reason`, `<alone-cmd>` being the value of the repo CLAUDE.md's `veri: one test file:` line (the repo's own runner for one test target, a test file or a pytest node id, taking the target as `$1`: `make test TESTS="$1"` on veri), read with `$S/detect-test-command.sh --tier "one test file"`. When the repo declares no such line (that call exits 3), the conductor skips `rerun-alone.sh` and, on `pin-fast-tier.sh` exit 1, enters the revise loop for red as on a `rerun-alone.sh` exit 1, never guessing a runner. On its exit 0 (every named target, a failing pytest node id else a file, passed alone; the script itself writes the one `suite-flake` event naming them) the conductor ticks and commits `test: pin` as on exit 0. On its exit 1 (a target stays red alone, or the output names none) the pin tripped a fast-tier guard: it commits nothing and enters the revise loop for red (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md` when a verifier answers `REJECT`) with `.tdd/verify-pin.reason` (the fast tier's output) as the reason, spending a `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt as a verify-pin REJECT does. On `pin-fast-tier.sh` exit 0 it ticks and commits `test: pin`; on exit 2 from either script it bails as a system failure. | A pin that breaks the fast tier is never committed, and is redone inside the same budget as any other rejected pin. A load flake in a file that passes alone is not the pin's defect, and must not cost the step its budget or the run a `revise-impossible` bail. |
| PN8 | PASS (exit 0) bullet, the command block: the pin ticks the ledger with `$S/tick-ledger.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)"` and commits with `git add -A && git commit -m "test: pin <STEP summary> (#<n>)"`. | The ledger tick and the test land in one commit. |
| PN9 | PASS (exit 0) bullet: immediately after that commit the conductor runs `$S/stop-requested.sh`, because the pin commit is a ledger commit like Green's, and handles its exit 0 and exit 2 by the stop check `SKILL.md`'s *Green* runs after its push, which the bullet names rather than restating those two branches; on exit 3 it carries on. | An operator's stop request is honoured at the pin's commit, the same checkpoint as Green's, by the one statement of the check. |
| PN10 | PASS (exit 0) bullet, the mirror: after the stop check the conductor posts a `### 📌 Already satisfied — <STEP>` comment carrying the verifier's reason, through `$S/comment.sh <n> pin`. | The issue shows why a step ended with no production code. |
| PN11 | PASS (exit 0) bullet, the mirror: the conductor asks whether the step's criterion is finished with `$S/criterion-finished.sh <k> < .tdd/issue-<n>.md`; on exit 0 it ticks that criterion in the issue body with `$S/tick-issue-criterion.sh <n> "$(gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/<n> -q .body \| $S/ledger-steps.sh criterion <k>)"`, and on exit 3 it ticks nothing. | The issue's checkbox is ticked only once every step serving the criterion is done, and its text is read from the issue itself. |
| PN12 | PASS (exit 0) bullet, the mirror: the conductor marks the pin in the log with `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$($S/ledger-steps.sh last-done < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" VERI_PHASE=pin $S/event.sh pin`. | The run log records a pinned step under the `pin` event, attributed to the step just ticked. |
| PN13 | PASS (exit 0) bullet: the step is done with no Green and no Refactor, since there is no new production code to write or to tidy, and the conductor loops back to the top of step 3 for the next unchecked ledger step. | A pin costs no further leaf and resumes the cycle loop. |
| PN14 | REJECT (exit 1) bullet: the conductor enters the revise loop for the red phase (`Read` `<base directory>/REVISE.md` when a verifier answers `REJECT`), building the prompt as `revise red … .tdd/verify-pin.reason`: the phase argument is the developer phase being redone, while the reason file is named for the verifier that rejected, here `pin`. | The redo is of the developer's red work, and its carry-forward reason is found under the verifier's name. |
| PN15 | REJECT (exit 1) bullet: the rejection reason is the carry-forward signal that tells the fresh leaf which way to go: either the pin is weak (trivial, over-coupled, or it survives the mutation) or a genuinely failing test exists after all. The conductor re-verifies with the phase the revise leaf's own `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED` line selects: `pin` on `PASSES`, `red` on `FAILS`. That line's third value, `N/A`, cannot arise here, because a revise of red always touches a test. | The redo is re-judged by the verifier that fits what the new test does, and a weak pin and a missed failing test are both recoverable. |
| PN16 | REJECT (exit 1) bullet: on a recovered `PASS` from `pin` the conductor does the pin's tick-and-commit (PN6 to PN13), or the deletion path on a `⟦replaces-case⟧` step; from `red` it continues to Green. On budget-exhausted it bails as a process failure. | A recovered pin lands the same way a first-try one does, and an exhausted budget is a methodology failure, not a system one. |
| PN17 | Malformed / killed-stuck (exit 2) bullet: the conductor bails as a system failure (`no-verdict`), see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`. | No answer from the verifier is a plumbing failure and is never read as a pass or a reject. |
| PN18 | The pin paragraph says `verify-pin`'s probe is recorded in `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md` by the probe itself, and that the file is committed with the pin's tick. | A later issue's run can only re-check a pin whose mutation survives its own run. |
| PN19 | The verify-pin REJECT paragraph runs one call, `$S/pin-reject-route.sh [--missing-reroute] .tdd/verify-pin.reason .tdd/issue-<n>.md "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" <target-test>`, passing `--missing-reroute` on a step reached by a `missing` reroute. It prints `retired` or `revise`: exit 0 retires the step (committed ledger, no `revise` mark, no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, retirement posted, loop back to `next-unchecked.sh`, a `missing`-reroute step's green not re-run), exit 1 (a failing target test, or under `--missing-reroute` a reason reporting a surviving mutant of a case red on the before-state) enters the revise loop for red, exit 2 bails as a system failure, except that PN22 bails an `unpinnable-missing` print as a process failure. The paragraph says the routing is the script's decision, never the conductor's reading of the reason, and that `build-develop-prompt.sh revise red` refuses (exit 2) only a `verify-pin.reason` reporting the before-state green, not every `stays green` reason. | One script owns the routing, so the conductor cannot misread a reason, and a step whose behaviour already holds does not burn revise attempts until the run bails. |
| PN20 | In the REJECT branch (the paragraph that runs `pin-reject-route.sh`, which prints `retired` or `revise`), the command carries a final `<target-test>` argument, the step's `TARGET_TEST` from the red leaf's report; the paragraph says the script retires the step only when that case passes, running it, and that a failing case makes it print `revise` and exit 1, leaving the line unticked and unmarked with no retirement event, so the conductor enters the revise loop for red (or parks the step when the revise budget is spent), never retiring it by hand. | A step whose green never landed must not retire as if its behaviour held; the step's own red case passing on the before-state is the evidence it already holds, and exit 1 is a clean negative, as in #3300. |
| PN21 | The same paragraph says exit 2 still means bad arguments (including a missing `<target-test>`) or no answer, so the conductor reads exit 1 as the methodology speaking and exit 2 as the plumbing breaking, except that an exit 2 printing `unpinnable-missing` (PN22) is the methodology speaking, a process failure. | The two codes keep their meanings; the conductor never collapses a refused retirement into a system bail. |
| PN22 | The REJECT branch bails with cause `unpinnable-missing`, a process failure, when `pin-reject-route.sh --missing-reroute` exits 2 printing `unpinnable-missing`; a bare exit 2 stays a system bail. | An unpinnable missing case is the methodology speaking, not the plumbing breaking. |
| PN23 | The verify-pin REJECT paragraph says that under `--missing-reroute` exit 2 means the reason has a before-state-green clause (a clause naming the before-state and green or pass, with no `fail`, `red`, `not`, `n't` or `after` in it; unpinnable; a reason with no such clause and no `stays green` retires via `--already-holds`), while a surviving mutant of the case is exit 1 and enters the revise loop for red. | The conductor bails only on the unpinnable case, never on a case that can be strengthened. |
| PN24 | PIN.md's REJECT branch takes `<target-test>` on a step reached by a `missing` reroute from `pin-target.sh`, the step's own red report's `TARGET_TEST:`, never from the `red-missing` leaf's report. | `pin-reject-route.sh` retires the step it is handed; the wrong target retires or rejects the wrong case. |
| PN25 | The opening section (*Already satisfied — the pin*) says that with `pin` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no verify-pin launches; it names the `verify-pin-skipped reason=switched-off` event and `skipped` in `.tdd/verdict-pin.exit`, and says the PASS branch follows with `pin-fast-tier.sh`, with no mutation recorded and no retirement possible. | The skip must be in the procedure the conductor follows. |
| PN26 | The same section says that a step whose text names a go-red control (`mutation-probe.sh --before <sha>` or `the go-red control is <file> at <sha>`) runs verify-pin even with `pin` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify`, because the criterion itself names the probe and `tick-ledger.sh` exits 2 on that step without its line in the mutation record. | The exception must be in the procedure the conductor follows. |
| PN27 | The opening section's **Switched off** paragraph says that on a skipped pin the `### 📌 Already satisfied — <STEP>` mirror comment carries `verify-pin — SKIPPED (switched off)` where it would carry the verifier's reason. | There is no verifier reason to carry, and the comment must not look verified (PRD 018.5, PN5). |
| PN28 | REJECT (exit 1) bullet: on `pin-reject-route.sh` exit 0 the conductor posts the step's retirement to the thread through `$S/comment.sh <n> retire`. | The thread shows a retired step under its own heading, and the conductor never composes that comment by hand. |

## skills/tdd/STRUCTURAL.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| DP28 | The issue's one structural pass runs once, before the PR is readied: the structural candidates the steps recorded rather than applied, each taken up by one `structural` transform leaf (`build-develop-prompt.sh structural`), bounded to every file the issue touched plus the files the candidate names (`refactor-touched.sh --issue`), counted against the issue's budget (`refactor-budget.sh --issue`), not a step's. | Cross-cutting tidying the steps deferred is done once, on the finished branch, within its own budget. |
| DP29 | The conductor runs the pass whenever `refactor-owed.sh list` prints a candidate and never skips or shortens it for cost: the way to spend less is to set a lower `VERI_ISSUE_REFACTOR_BUDGET` before launch. A run does a refactor now, or drops it: the pass files no issue per candidate; what it does not apply is listed in the run's one candidates issue, which the Draft PR step files (ADR-0034). | Spend is set by the operator before the run, and every candidate ends applied or recorded, never as a queue-bound issue; ADR-0034 partly reverses ADR-0031's "never files". |
| DP30 | A candidate recorded test-only is never listed, so it launches no predicate or transform leaf; an out-of-scope candidate is listed like any other. | No leaf is spent on a candidate no transform could land. |
| DP31 | Candidates are taken in the order `refactor-owed.sh list` prints them, most valuable first, `<count>` being the transforms the pass has already landed. | When the budget runs out, what was left is the least valuable. |
| DP32 | For each candidate the conductor asks `$S/refactor-budget.sh --issue <count>` (exit 0 launch, exit 3 the pass is over), records `git rev-parse HEAD` as `<base-sha>`, and computes `<bound>` with `$S/refactor-touched.sh --issue <issue-base> --candidate '<candidate>'`, `<issue-base>` being `git merge-base HEAD origin/<default>`. | Each transform has a budget check, a point to reset to and a file bound before it starts. |
| DP33 | A candidate the budget stops is recorded, with every candidate after it, by `refactor-owed.sh not-done .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md '<candidate>' budget-spent`. | Every candidate the pass did not reach is on the record with why. |
| DP34 | After a predicate exit 0 and before its transform leaf, a `develop-cover` leaf (prompt `build-develop-prompt.sh cover` with the spec, the candidate and the test command) mutates the code the candidate will move, runs the suites, expects red and restores; its report is read through `$S/cover-outcome.sh`: exit 2 bails as a **system** failure (`no-verdict`), exit 3 (`COVERED: unreachable` or `type-only`) goes on, exit 1 (`COVERED: no`) launches no transform, records the candidate `uncovered` with `refactor-owed.sh not-done`, and neither bails nor files anything. | A structural move is made only where a suite would catch it going wrong. |
| DP35 | For each candidate, before the cover leaf, a predicate leaf (`build-develop-prompt.sh predicate` with the spec, the candidate, the test command and `<bound>`, launched through the supervisor as `develop-predicate`) reports one `PREDICATE:` line, which the conductor checks and records with `$S/structural-predicate.sh .tdd/issue-<n>-predicate`; that record is the test path the transform verifier runs, in place of the prose candidate. Exit 3 (`PREDICATE: NONE`) is recorded `no-predicate` with `refactor-owed.sh not-done` rather than handed to a cover or transform leaf; exit 1 (a command already true) means the candidate is already applied, recorded as applied at the current HEAD with no cover or transform leaf; exit 2 (no answer) **bails** as a **system** failure (`no-verdict`). | Each transform is judged against a checkable predicate, a candidate already true costs no leaf, and a predicate that could not be read ends the run as the plumbing failure it is. |
| DP36 | Each transform leaf's prompt is `build-develop-prompt.sh structural`'s (the spec, the candidate as `list` printed it, the test command, `<bound>`), written to `.tdd/develop-structural.prompt`, and launched through the supervisor as `develop-structural`; after its wait exits 0 and before its commits are checked, `$S/suite-reported.sh develop-structural` runs, and its exit 2 (no `SUITE:` line) launches no transform verifier and **bails** as a system failure with cause `report-missing`. An out-of-bounds skip (the leaf left the tree as it found it and said so) is exempt: it keeps a transform `REJECT` and the next candidate, never a `report-missing` bail. | No transform is judged without the leaf's own suite report, and a leaf that declined out of bounds is not mistaken for a broken one. |
| DP37 | A transform leaf's commits are checked with `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha> <bound>` (exit 1 rejects the transform: a test file, or a file outside `<bound>`; exit 2 is no answer), then judged by the long refactor's blind `transform` verifier against `<base-sha>`, launched with `--fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>'` so it runs the fast tier and then the `changed` run, one commit per transform — both before the next candidate's leaf is launched. | A transform that edits tests or strays out of bounds never reaches the verifier, and each transform is judged alone; a transform that breaks a fast-tier guard its own module's test files cannot see is rejected at the transform, not one step later (#4444). |
| DP38 | A `REJECT`, or a transform `transform-touches-tests.sh` rejected, resets to `<base-sha>`, records the candidate `transform-rejected` with `refactor-owed.sh not-done`, and moves on to the next candidate. | A rejected transform leaves no trace on the branch and a reason on the record. |
| DP39 | A `PASS` records the candidate as applied with `refactor-owed.sh applied .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md '<candidate>' "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" "$(git log -1 --format=%s)"` (the fourth argument is the applying commit's subject, which `render` prints beside the sha), so `render` lists it as applied rather than owed; then `git add .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` and `git commit -m "chore: record the structural pass's applied candidate (#<n>)"`. | The PR body shows each applied candidate with the commit that applied it, from a committed record. |
| DP40 | Every `refactor-owed.sh not-done` call (`budget-spent`, `uncovered`, `no-predicate`, `transform-rejected`, `out-of-bounds`) is followed at once, before the next candidate's leaf is launched, by `git add .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md` and `git commit -m "chore: record the structural pass's not-done candidate (#<n>)"`, so a later candidate's `REJECT` reset to its `<base-sha>` keeps the row. | Every not-done row survives the resets that follow it. |
| DP41 | Once the pass ends (candidates run out, or the budget is spent) it is marked once: `event.sh structural-pass transforms=<count> budget=<VERI_ISSUE_REFACTOR_BUDGET> stopped=<reason>`; `stopped=` takes only `candidates-done`, `budget-spent` or `nothing-structural`, and `budget-spent` is marked only when `refactor-budget.sh --issue` exited 3, i.e. `transforms` equals the budget. | The log records what the pass landed and why it stopped, in values `event.sh` accepts. |
| DP73 | Every candidate the pass does not apply is recorded with `refactor-owed.sh not-done` and its reason, and `render` shows it under `### Not done` in the PR body; a test-only candidate still shows under `### Test-only`. | The reviewer sees each candidate that was not applied, and why. |
| DP91 | For each candidate the predicate leaf (`build-develop-prompt.sh predicate`, launched as `develop-predicate`) runs before the `develop-cover` leaf and its report is recorded with `$S/structural-predicate.sh .tdd/issue-<n>-predicate`: exit 1 (a command already true) records the candidate as applied at the current HEAD with `refactor-owed.sh applied` and launches neither the cover nor the transform leaf; exit 3 (`PREDICATE: NONE`) records it `no-predicate`; exit 0 goes on to the cover leaf and the transform; exit 2 bails as a system failure (`no-verdict`). | A candidate a landed step already satisfied is reported as applied, not as `uncovered`, because cover can only be judged on code that still has the move to make. |
| DP357 | In the per-candidate block, after `refactor-touched.sh --issue <issue-base> --candidate '<candidate>'` computes `<bound>` and before the predicate leaf, the conductor runs the same command with `--check`; exit 1 (the candidate names only files the issue's commits never touched) launches no predicate, cover or transform leaf and records the candidate `refactor-owed.sh not-done .tdd/issue-<n>-refactor-owed.md '<candidate>' out-of-bounds`, followed by the commit DP40 names. | A PR does not grow out of its issue's scope (PRD 012 BG6); no leaf is spent on a candidate the PR must not carry. |
| DP94 | In the transform check, `transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha> <bound>` also gets `--touched "$($S/refactor-touched.sh --issue <issue-base>)"`; its exit 1 rejects the transform like any bound violation (DP38). | A candidate that names no file cannot be judged before the leaf, so the commits themselves must change at least one file the issue touched. |
| DP358 | A `suite-reported.sh develop-structural` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/suite-reported.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-report-missing` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before the transform verifier (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `report-missing` with no verifier. (The `suite-reported.sh develop-structural` paragraph.) | Every site of the cause. |
| DP239 | An out-of-bounds skip stays unadjudicated: it keeps its transform `REJECT` and the next candidate. (The same paragraph.) | It is not an exit-2 bail today. |
| DP238 | The structural pass is marked as stage `structural`: `event.sh stage-start stage=structural` before its first candidate, and one `event.sh stage-end stage=structural status=<exit code> secs=<seconds>` on every way it ends, its status 0 after the pass's `structural-pass` mark, 3 at a stop (whichever stop check catches it, a leaf's exit 2 read as a stop included), else the code of the call that ended it (a bail's); a run whose `refactor-owed.sh list` exits 3 runs no pass and marks no stage. | A prose stage is marked at its site, or its run time and failures go unrecorded; a stop counted as passed or left unended would misreport it, and a pass never run must not show as one. |

## skills/tdd/PHASED.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| CY100 | A step listing `⟦phases: …⟧` runs no Red, Green or Refactor phase: it runs the phases it lists, in order, one at a time, and `$S/ledger-steps.sh next-phase < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` names the next one. | The ledger's own phase marks decide what runs next, so a phased step runs only the phases it lists and each in its turn. |
| CY101 | For each phase, `git rev-parse HEAD` is recorded as that phase's base sha; the phase's developer leaf makes that phase's change and commits it, and the blind verifier judges the diff from that base sha. | Each phase is judged on its own commit, from the point it started. |
| CY102 | On a phase's PASS the conductor marks it with `tick-ledger.sh --phase <phase> .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<STEP>"`, commits the ledger with that phase's commit, pushes, and goes back to `next-phase` for the step's next phase. | Each finished phase is a durable ledger mark, so a resumed run starts at the next unmarked phase. |
| CY103 | On a phase verifier's REJECT the conductor revises under `REVISE_BUDGET` as `<base directory>/REVISE.md` sets out, `Read`ing it when a verifier answers `REJECT`; on exit 2 it **bails** as a system failure (`no-verdict`). | A rejected phase gets a bounded number of revisions, and no verdict is labelled a system failure. |
| CY104 | Once `next-phase` exits 3, every listed phase is marked and the step reads as done; the loop goes back to `next-unchecked.sh`. | The step is finished when its last listed phase is marked, and the loop moves to the next step. |
| CY105 | After a phase leaf's wait exits 0, `$S/suite-reported.sh develop-<phase>` runs before its verifier is launched: exit 2 means no `SUITE:` line, so no verifier is launched and the conductor **bails** as a system failure with cause `report-missing`. | A phase verifier is only launched over a report that says whether the suites ran. |
| CY106 | A phased step spends one refactor of the budget for the whole step, not one per phase: wherever `refactor-budget.sh <count>` counts the transforms already landed, the step adds one to `<count>` once its last phase is marked, however many phases it listed. | A phased step costs the refactor budget the same one unit however many phases it lists. |
| CY107 | The freeze check runs on a phase leaf's commit before the verifier: an expand, migrate or contract-b commit runs `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha>` and a cover-new or contract-a commit runs `$S/production-frozen.sh <base-sha>`; exit 1 rejects the commit (tests are frozen in the first three, production in cover-new and contract-a), a rejection is revised as a REJECT is, and exit 2 **bails** as a system failure. | Each phase's commit is held to the files its phase may touch before a verifier sees it. |
| CY108 | Contract-a deletes the old interface's cases, each deletion proved by mutation parity against a kept case: its PASS is accepted only by the mutation-parity gate `SKILL.md`'s *Green* states for a `⟦replaces-case⟧` verify, run on that phase's pairs table; the paragraph names that gate rather than restating its exit codes, the PR body's pairs table and `## Owed by a human`. | With cover-new in place, contract-a only removes old cases the new ones make redundant, and what it cannot prove reaches the human through the one statement of the gate. |
| CY109 | Contract-b, the phase that deletes the interface, starts only once `$S/no-product-caller.sh <old-interface>` on the old interface exits 0, run before it launches; exit 1 names each product file still calling the old interface, so contract-b does not start and the step **bails** as a process failure (the migrate phase left a caller behind); exit 2 **bails** as a system failure. | The interface is deleted only when no product file calls it any more. |
| CY110 | Contract-a starts on the migrate phase's PASS without the `no-product-caller.sh` scan: it removes the tests' routing to the old interface, not the interface, and a shim kept on purpose is never scanned. | A shim that stays by design never blocks the contract that retires only the tests' routing. |
| CY111 | A contract phase that exhausts `REVISE_BUDGET` does not abandon the step: the contract is reset to its own base sha with `git reset --hard <base-sha>`, so the expand and migrate commits are kept, and it is filed under the ledger's `## Owed steps` as a plain bullet appended under that trailing heading, the heading being added when the ledger has none. | An exhausted contract leaves the earlier phases' work in place and the contract itself on the owed list. |
| CY112 | The exhausted contract phase is marked owed with `tick-ledger.sh --phase-owed <phase> .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<STEP>"` before the ledger is committed, so `next-phase` never hands it back; the conductor then commits the ledger and goes back to `next-unchecked.sh`. | The owed contract is never offered again, and the run continues with the next step. |
| CY113 | A cover-new phase adds cases that call the new interface directly, and its PASS is accepted only once those cases pass against the expand commit and go red under a mutation of the new interface's behaviour. | The new interface's own tests are shown to catch a broken interface before callers move onto it. |
| CY114 | A migrate phase does not start until the step's cover-new phase has passed. | Callers move only onto an interface that already has tests of its own. |
| CY124 | The freeze check runs `transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha>` on a `beside` commit and `production-frozen.sh <base-sha>` on a `dual-stub` commit, each rejection revised as a REJECT is. | `beside` is production only and `dual-stub` tests only; each is judged by its own freeze. |
| CY125 | A `beside` phase adds the new interface and leaves the old one's behaviour unchanged, with no proxy. A `dual-stub` phase makes the cases that pin the old transport stub the new one too, and they stay green against unmoved production code. A migrate phase does not start until a listed `dual-stub` phase has passed. | With both stubs in place, moving callers turns no frozen case red, which is what made the earlier plans bail. |
| CY154 | A `suite-reported.sh develop-<phase>` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/suite-reported.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-report-missing` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before the phase's verifier (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `report-missing` with no verifier. (The `suite-reported.sh` paragraph.) | Every site of the cause. |

## skills/tdd/REFACTOR-KIND.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PL22 | In a refactor-kind run, whose spec carries the `## Refactor` section `capture-spec.sh` writes for the `refactor` label, each step that is neither `⟦diff-checked⟧` nor a pin step is planned as one named refactoring whose criterion is a shell predicate, false before the step and true after it, and the plan verifier REJECTs such a step not written that way; a test-only step is REJECTed only when it is planned as anything but `⟦diff-checked⟧` or a pin step (#679, #1915). | A refactor changes no behaviour, so the smell being present is the step's Red; a test-only criterion has no production code to transform, and #1417 bailed `revise-exhausted` at verify-plan when every plan holding one was rejected. |
| PL23 | In a refactor-kind plan, a deletion is folded into the step that makes the code dead: a no-op is never left in place for a later step to delete (#679). | That later step's Cover would run on code nothing reaches. |
| PL24 | Each refactor-kind step opens with a cover leaf that mutates the code the step will move, runs the suite, expects red, restores, and reports `COVERED:` and `CAUGHT_BY:`; if `git status --porcelain` prints anything once it exits, the run stops (#679). | The transform would otherwise commit the leftover mutation as a refactoring. |
| PL25 | The cover report is read through `$S/cover-outcome.sh`: exit 2 bails as a system failure (`no-verdict`); exit 3 (`COVERED: unreachable` or `type-only` with its `PROOF:`) goes on to Transform without bailing or running `coverage-blocker.sh`, posting the proof it prints in the step's Cover comment; exit 1, `COVERED: no`, refuses the step with a bail of cause `uncovered`, narrating the remedy (find a seam and surround the behaviour with tests first) and running `$S/coverage-blocker.sh <n>`, which files the coverage issue as a `legacy` issue and blocks this one on it (#679). The filed issue has a `## Problem` section, a `## Region` section holding the refactor step's ledger line, a `## Acceptance criteria` section with one criterion per `MUTATION:` line of the cover report (omitted when the report has none), `## Blocked by` with `None.`, and a last line `Test command: <the all-tier command>`; it carries the `legacy` label when the repo has one, the opt-in label (`VERI_LABEL`, default `ready-for-agent`) and `filed-by:<lane>`, but only `filed-by:<lane>` beside `legacy` when there is no `MUTATION:` line (#3721). | An uncovered step must not be retaken by the queue only to be refused again, and its coverage issue must run as the kind that surrounds code (PRD 031). |
| PL26 | For a `type-only` step, the text `cover-outcome.sh` printed after `type-only: ` is saved to `.tdd/issue-<n>-proof`, and after the transform commits the conductor itself, not a leaf, reruns that saved proof command; a non-zero exit rejects the transform as `transform-touches-tests.sh` exit 1 does (#679). | The proof command is what the transform is checked against, and a leaf must not grade its own work. |
| PL27 | A refactor-kind step runs Cover → Transform → Verify, not Red → Green → Refactor, unless it is `⟦diff-checked⟧`, which runs develop-diff then verify-diff and never Cover or Transform: a transform leaf applies the step's one named refactoring and commits it, and `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha>` exit 1 rejects the transform because a refactoring that edits a test file has moved the goalposts, while exit 2 is no answer. The blind `transform` verifier reads the diff from the same base sha and is launched with `--fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>'`, so it runs the fast tier and then the `changed` run (#4444). An abandoned refactor-kind step is reset with `git reset --hard <base-sha>`, having no untracked test to clean (#679, #1915). | Its transform commits are the work, so the step is abandoned by resetting to its base; a test-only step would trip the transform's test freeze, so it takes the diff-checked route. |
| PL48 | A refactor-kind plan may mix refactoring steps with `⟦diff-checked⟧` or pin steps for its test-only criteria (#1915). | A `refactor`-labelled issue with test-only work gets a plan past the verifier instead of bailing at verify-plan. |
| PL64 | After a refactor-kind step's transform leaf's wait exits 0 and before the transform verifier is launched, the conductor runs `$S/suite-reported.sh develop-transform`: exit 2 means the report carries no `SUITE:` line, so no verifier is launched and the run bails as a system failure with cause `report-missing`. (The Cover → Transform → Verify passage.) | A transform with no reported suite result cannot be judged. |
| PL265 | A `suite-reported.sh develop-transform` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/suite-reported.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-report-missing` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event and before the transform verifier (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `report-missing` with no verifier. (The `suite-reported.sh develop-transform` sub-step.) | Every site of the cause. |

## skills/tdd/LEGACY.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| LY1 | The file states the legacy kind's phases in order, Seam → Surround → Verify, and that each plan step names one region (a file and a symbol or line range) and one seam (a command or callable outside the region that drives it: an entry-point CLI, an exported function, an HTTP handler), and lists `⟦phases: seam,surround⟧`. | The seam is what makes the test black-box, and the phase list written at plan time is what a resume reads. |
| LY2 | The file says it, not `PHASED.md`, runs a legacy step, and that a legacy step is never run through a fused call of PRD 027: its leaves are launched by the conductor through `supervise.sh`. | Stuck detection lives only in the supervisor, and a fused call would run the wrong kind's phases. |
| LY3 | The file says each step's Seam phase launches one `develop-seam` leaf through `supervise.sh`, whose report a script reads; a non-empty `git status --porcelain` once the leaf exits stops the run, as after a cover leaf. On `REACHES: no` the conductor launches one `develop-break` leaf for the region instead of bailing; after a `verify-break` PASS `develop-seam` runs again, and `REACHES: no` then bails with cause `no-seam`, the note carrying both leaves' reasons. | A region nothing drives gets one bounded chance to be made reachable before the run gives up on it (PRD 031, D8). |
| LY4 | The file says `REACHES: yes` is recorded with `$S/tick-ledger.sh --phase seam`, so a resumed run does not confirm a seam twice. | Resume reads the committed ledger. |
| LY5 | The file says that once every legacy step's seam is confirmed the run goes on to Surround: per step, one `develop-surround` leaf then one `verify-surround` leaf, each launched through `supervise.sh`. | The run ends with the characterization tests committed, not with a `surround-owed` stop. |
| LY6 | The file says that after a `verify-surround` PASS the conductor runs `$S/surround-mutants.sh` over the `develop-surround` and `verify-surround` reports, its command running each test file the step's Surround commit added through the repo's `veri: one test file:` command (the all tier when the repo declares none), and that exit 0 is recorded with `$S/tick-ledger.sh --phase surround`. | A region counts as surrounded only when every mutant goes red under a script's run (PRD 031, D2, D4). |
| LY7 | The file says a `verify-surround` REJECT spends `REVISE_BUDGET` like any other reject and a leaf ending without a verdict spends `VERI_NO_VERDICT_RETRIES`, with no new budget, and that `VERI_SKIP_VERIFY` never skips `surround`. | Budgets stay on their axes, and a legacy run with its one verifier skipped proves nothing. |
| LY8 | The file says `surround-mutants.sh` exit 1 with a `survived` row is revised as a REJECT, its output the reason, spending `REVISE_BUDGET`; exit 1 `short` records the step's one free relaunch with `$S/tick-ledger.sh --surround-short` on its committed ledger line and relaunches `develop-surround` once to add rows without spending `REVISE_BUDGET`, and a `short` once that relaunch is recorded, a resumed run's included (`tick-ledger.sh --surround-short` exit 1), bails with cause `surround-short`; exit 2 bails as a system failure, `no-verdict`. | A survivor is a rejected product and a short table is not (ADR-0020), and a resume must not spend the free relaunch twice. |
| LY179 | The file says each `MUTATION:` criterion of a legacy issue's spec (the criteria `coverage-blocker.sh` writes) is a row `develop-surround` owes in its step's mutant table, beside the rows it proposes. | The refactor's refusal already produced the region's mutations (PRD 031, D5). |
| LY180 | The file tells the conductor to run `$S/release-covered.sh <n>` after `DRAFT-PR.md` sub-step 4 (the ✅ Done comment, or `run-done.sh` where it makes that comment); exits 0 and 3 go on, and exit 2 is reported in the run's final report and is not a bail. | The run is already done, so a failed release must not turn it into a bail; the refactor issue it blocked goes back on the queue without a human. |
| LY181 | The file names the catalogue of edits a `develop-break` leaf may apply: `sprout` (the region's code moved unchanged into a new function or class that the old code now calls and a test can call directly) and `extract and override` (a dependency call extracted into a method a test may override); no other edit is allowed. | Editing uncovered code with no net is bounded to edits known to be safe (PRD 031, D8). |
| LY182 | The file says a region gets at most one break; that `$S/transform-touches-tests.sh <base-sha>` exit 1 on the break commit rejects it, and the fast tier and the test files covering the files the break changed must stay green; and that a `verify-break` REJECT spends `REVISE_BUDGET`. | No test exists to move, the existing suite is the only net, and budgets stay on their axes. |

## skills/tdd/BAIL.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| BL1 | A bail terminally stops work on the issue while preserving the progress already made (ADR-0011). (Opening paragraph.) | A stopped run keeps the steps it finished. |
| BL2 | Each bail carries a cause token, and the token maps to a classification through `$S/classify-bail.sh <cause>`. (Opening paragraph.) | One token decides whether the methodology spoke or the plumbing broke. |
| BL3 | `killed-stuck`: `supervise.sh` exited 2 for a developer leaf even after its stuck-retry budget; a **system** failure. | A leaf that stopped answering is plumbing, not a verdict. |
| BL4 | `no-verdict`: exit 2 from `verify.sh` / `run-verdict.sh`, `grade-prose.sh` (the Draft PR regrade), the ⟦skill-edit⟧ runner `skill-edit-step.sh`, `resume-clean.sh`, `rebase-onto-default.sh`, `ledger-growth.sh` (the growth check at a ledger commit) or `cover-outcome.sh`; a **system** failure. | No answer was obtained, so there is nothing to act on. |
| BL5 | `unreplaced-cases`: `unreplaced-cases.sh` exited 1 a second time, after the one correction leaf, naming ⟦replaces-case⟧ cases with no `REPLACES:` line — or exited 2 on its first check and the adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) did not route it to the correction leaf, or exited 2 on its second check, which bails at once — before verify-green; a **process** failure, keeping its cause and class after an adjudication. | A second refusal is the methodology's answer, not a glitch. |
| BL6 | `replaces-report-failed`: `wait-leaf.sh develop-replaces-report` exited 1 or 2, the correction leaf not having finished cleanly, before verify-red; a **system** failure. | The correction leaf itself broke, so no report exists to judge. |
| BL7 | `revise-exhausted`: a step spent its `REVISE_BUDGET` and the blind verifier still says `REJECT`; a **process** failure. | The verdict budget is spent and the answer is still no. |
| BL8 | `untestable-criterion`: a Red developer leaf reported `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: UNTESTABLE`, the criterion not being pinnable as a lightweight behaviour-level test; a **process** failure. | A criterion no case can pin is a planning answer. |
| BL9 | `step-too-big`: a split was refused for the growth cap, ⌈`VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` × P ÷ 100⌉, P the steps of the verified plan, so the smaller steps are filed under `## Owed steps` instead of the plan; a **process** failure. Three splits lead here: a Green leaf's `SPLIT:`, the split the revise-exhausted leaf answers, and the split the over-budget leaf answers. | Growth is capped, and a refused split is recorded rather than lost. |
| BL10 | `step-added-off-route`: `ledger-growth.sh` exited 1 at a ledger commit — a step line the plan commit did not hold has no matching `add-ledger-step.sh` record, or lacks ⟦added⟧ when its record shows no repair or `--planned` insertion, or the ⟦added⟧ lines exceed `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`; a **process** failure, with stderr naming the line. | A ledger may only grow through the recorded route. |
| BL11 | After a `step-added-off-route` bail no commit is made over the line; to resume, take the named line out of the uncommitted ledger, or put it back through `add-ledger-step.sh` within `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`, commit, and resume. | The refused line is never frozen into history. |
| BL12 | `step-add-refused`: `add-ledger-step.sh` exited 2 at any call site — the anchor matched no step or several, `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` was unreadable or the retired `VERI_STEP_GROWTH` was set, or `--criterion` was malformed; a **system** failure. | A tool that cannot answer is plumbing, whatever it was asked. |
| BL13 | `ci-timeout`: a readied PR's check was still pending when the Draft PR step's bounded CI wait ran out; a **system** failure. | Waiting longer is not a verdict on the change. |
| BL14 | `ci-conflict`: a readied PR still conflicted with its base after the CI step's one rebase, so no workflow could start; a **system** failure. | No CI ran, so CI said nothing. |
| BL15 | `ci-red`: a readied PR's CI check failed again after a CI repair step; a **process** failure. | A second red after a repair is CI's answer. |
| BL16 | `suite-red`: the Draft PR step's full run was red again after a ⟦suite-repair⟧ step; a **process** failure. | The repair was given its chance and the suite is still red. |
| BL17 | `rebased-tip-red`: the Draft PR step's full run was red on the tip `rebase-onto-default.sh` rebased; a **process** failure. | The branch is red where it would land. |
| BL18 | `rebase-unresolved`: a rebase was not resolved within budget; a **process** failure. | Conflict resolution is bounded, and the bound is an answer. |
| BL19 | A bail reached first stays a bail, whatever stop is waiting behind it, because the methodology or the plumbing has already answered and a pause would hide that answer from the resume. | The reason a run stopped is never overwritten by a later stop. |
| BL20 | The conductor asks `$S/stop-requested.sh` once before sub-step 1 and keeps what it printed. | One read, so the answer cannot change mid-bail. |
| BL21 | A `REJECT` that exhausts `REVISE_BUDGET` bails even with a stop pending, and a killed-stuck leaf with no `now` pending bails as it always has; only a pending `now` turns a leaf's exit 2 into the stop (see *Launching a leaf*). | Only an immediate stop outranks a leaf's own failure. |
| BL22 | When that exit 0 said a stop was waiting, sub-step 6's bail comment adds that a stop was pending, naming its mode. | The thread records that a stop was queued behind the bail. |
| BL23 | Sub-step 1: preserve the attempt first, before the reset, from inside the worktree, with `$S/preserve-attempt.sh <n> "<last done step>" <phase> <attempt> bail`, because the tree the troubled leaf left is the evidence and the next sub-step throws it away. | The evidence is captured before anything discards it. |
| BL24 | `<phase>` is the leaf whose trouble caused the bail, and `<attempt>` its number: 1 outside the revise loop, k inside it. | The snapshot names which attempt it came from. |
| BL25 | The snapshot is best-effort: if it fails, note it and bail anyway. | A failed snapshot never blocks the stop it documents. |
| BL26 | Sub-step 2: revert only the half-done step, with `git reset --hard HEAD`; completed steps' commits and the ledger's checked steps stay intact. | Only the unfinished step is undone. |
| BL27 | Sub-step 3: classify deterministically with `classification="$($S/classify-bail.sh <cause>)"`, `<cause>` being one of the listed tokens; an unknown token exits non-zero, so the right one must be picked. | The classification is computed, never judged by the conductor. |
| BL28 | Then mark the stop in the log, before anything renders it, with `event.sh bail cause=<cause> classification="$classification"` (actor conductor, step the last done step, `VERI_PHASE=<phase>`); sub-step 6's `### ⛔ Bailed` comment mirrors this mark. | The log carries the stop before any report is built from it. |
| BL29 | Sub-step 4: render and commit the metrics from inside the worktree and before the push — `$S/metrics.sh <n> > .tdd/issue-<n>-metrics.md; metrics=$?` — committing it as `metrics: what bailing on #<n> cost (#<n>)` only when `$metrics` is 0, so the pushed branch carries the account of the bail. | What the bail cost travels with the branch. |
| BL30 | Sub-step 5: push the branch with `git push -u origin tdd/<n>-<slug>` from inside the worktree, so the completed steps, the metrics commit and the leaf logs are recoverable; best-effort, noted if it fails. | Nothing of the run is left only on one machine. |
| BL31 | Sub-step 6: post a `### ⛔ Bailed — <phase> (<classification> failure)` comment naming the phase by running `$S/comment.sh <n> bailed --note-file <path>`, the note carrying what only the agent knows. | The thread says where and how the run stopped. |
| BL32 | That comment carries the `### 📊 Metrics` block just committed, or, when `$metrics` is not 0, the line `metrics could not be rendered: metrics.sh exited $metrics` in its place. | The cost is reported, or its absence is. |
| BL33 | It carries the last reason: `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` on a `revise-exhausted` bail, the develop-red leaf's `WHY:` line on `untestable-criterion`, `.tdd/pr-ready.err`'s text on `pr-ready-failed`, the kill cause otherwise; a `ci-red` reason names each CI check that failed, from the `failed=` field of the bail mark read off the head's check runs. | The reader sees the actual last word, not a category. |
| BL34 | After the reason it carries the skill-drift block: the stdout of `drift="$($S/skill-drift.sh)"; drift_exit=$?`, or, when `$drift_exit` is not 0, the line `Skill drift: unavailable — skill-drift.sh exited $drift_exit`; the bail goes on either way. | Whether the skill moved under the run is part of the record. |
| BL35 | It points at the progress log, `.tdd/<phase>.log` or `.tdd/revise-<phase>-*.log`, for forensics. | The reader is told where to look next. |
| BL36 | Sub-step 7: leave the draft PR open and never close it. It is the run's claim on the issue, since `solvable-issues.sh` omits an issue an open PR says `Closes #<n>` for, so closing it would hand the issue back to the pool to be re-taken and re-bailed, and would discard the visible record. | An open draft PR is what stops the issue being taken again. |
