| BL37 | Put the bail diagnostic on the PR: look `<pr-url>` up with `gh api 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?head={owner}:{branch}' -q '.[0].html_url // empty'` (`<pr-number>` is its last path segment) and post to `repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/<pr-number>/comments` the same Bailed comment, by running `$S/comment.sh <n> bailed --pr --note-file <path>`, so the page a reader opens stops saying a run is in progress. | The PR a reader lands on tells the truth about the run. |
| BL38 | Either way the PR carries sub-step 6's skill-drift block; a link-only comment quotes that block beside the link. | The drift is visible without following a link. |
| BL39 | The PR stays a draft: nobody is asked to review a run that stopped. | Review is for finished work. |
| BL40 | The ledger's completed steps stay checked, so a re-run resumes from the failing step (see `RESUME.md`) and edits this same PR rather than opening another. | One issue, one PR, and no finished step repeated. |
| BL41 | Sub-step 8: report to the user the phase, the classification (system or process), the reason, sub-step 6's skill-drift block, the branch name and the worktree path — the logs are inside it, and it is where a re-run resumes. | The human is told what stopped and where to pick it up. |
| BL42 | Leave the worktree in place; only a merged PR retires it. | The evidence and the resume point survive the bail. |
| BL43 | `missing-reroutes-exhausted`: a step's repeat `missing` reroutes spent `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` and verify-green still names a behaviour no test pins; a **process** failure. | The reroute budget is spent and the tests still leave a behaviour unpinned. |
| BL44 | `killed-external`: a developer leaf's `supervise.sh` exited 2 because the leaf died with no recorded exit and veri did not kill it (the supervisor's `supervise[<name>]: killed-external` status line), never retried; a **system** failure, distinct from `killed-stuck`. | An OS kill (memory exhaustion, a signalled shell) is plumbing, and is not evidence that the leaf hung. |
| BL45 | `BAIL.md`'s cause list has a `pr-ready-failed` bullet: `pr-ready.sh` exited non-zero at the Draft PR step (GitHub refused or never answered the ready request, after its refusal retries) → **system** failure (#2581). | A refused GitHub call is plumbing, and naming it apart from `no-verdict` lets `classify-bail.sh` and an operator tell it from a verifier that gave no verdict. |
| BL46 | `BAIL.md`'s cause list has a `graded-edit-outside-skill-edit` bullet: a step not marked `⟦skill-edit⟧` changed a unit listed in a `GRADED` file → **process** failure. | The methodology put graded prose outside the loop that grades and signs it; naming that apart tells the operator to move the edit into a `⟦skill-edit⟧` step. |
| BL47 | The cause list has a `revise-impossible` bullet: a revise leaf answered `IMPOSSIBLE`, saying no revision can meet the objection, which is a **process** failure distinct from `revise-exhausted`, where the attempts ran out. | A reader of the bail must be able to tell the two apart. |
| BL48 | `repair-reroutes-exhausted`: a step's `WRONG_TEST` naming no case took its `repair` once more after the free reroute, and the next such report still names no case; a **process** failure. | The red was repaired twice and the green still calls it wrong without naming it, so the human decides. |
| BL49 | `unpinnable-missing`: a missing-reroute's demanded case is green on the before-state and cannot be pinned; a **process** failure. | The tests cannot pin the criterion; the plumbing worked. |
| BL50 | The cause list has a bullet for a skill-edit step whose runner refused with exit 2 and a refusal message: cause `skill-edit-refused`, a **process** failure that names the refusal; a bare exit 2 with no refusal message stays the `no-verdict` system failure. | `classify-bail.sh` listed no process cause for a skill-edit refusal, so a bad issue read as `system` (#3584). |
| BL51 | `BAIL.md` names the cause `prep-owed`: the bail when `add-ledger-step.sh` refuses, with exit 1, the step a `prep` answer asks for, a process failure whose comment names the cases the step was to update. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described. |
| BL52 | The `ci-conflict` bullet says the bail can follow an adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `ci-conflict` bullet.) | Same sentence as the `ci-timeout` bullet: the bail list stays true. |
| BL53 | The `pr-ready-failed` bullet says the bail can follow an adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `pr-ready-failed` bullet.) | The bail list stays true. |
| BL54 | The cause list has a `report-missing` bullet: a phase leaf's report carried no `SUITE:` line (`suite-reported.sh` exited 2) → **system** failure; the bail can follow an adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `report-missing` bullet.) | `classify-bail.sh` knows the cause; the bail list does not. |
| BL55 | The `step-add-refused` bullet says the bail can follow an adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `step-add-refused` bullet.) | The bail list stays true. |
| BL56 | The `replaces-report-failed` bullet says the wait's exit 2 can be adjudicated first (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and the bail keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `replaces-report-failed` bullet.) | The bail list stays true. |
| BL67 | The cause list has a `reconcile-failed` bullet: the reconcile of a rejected push failed (the `develop-reconcile` leaf exited 1 or 2, the second `push-branch.sh` exited other than 0, or the ledger's ticks or marks changed) → **system** failure; its exit-2 sources can be adjudicated first (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`), and the bail keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `reconcile-failed` bullet.) | `classify-bail.sh` knows the cause; the bail list does not. |
| BL68 | The cause list has a `criterion-unreadable` bullet: `criterion-finished.sh` exited 2 at a step's mirror (the criterion could not be read) → **system** failure; the bail can follow an adjudication (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`) and keeps its cause and class. (Cause list, `criterion-unreadable` bullet.) | The bail list stays true. |
| BL69 | The cause list has a `rerecord-failed` bullet: `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-refactored` exited **2** (no answer) after a verify-refactor PASS or a `skipped` one → **system** failure. (Cause list, `rerecord-failed` bullet.) | The bail list names every token `classify-bail.sh` knows. |
| BL107 | The cause list has a `no-seam` bullet: a legacy step's `develop-seam` leaf answered `REACHES: no`, the region having no seam that drives it without an edit → **process** failure, carrying the leaf's reason. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described. |
| BL108 | The cause list has a `surround-owed` bullet: the planned stop of a legacy run made before the Surround phase existed, every step's seam confirmed and its next phase `surround` → **process** failure. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described, and an older bail must still read true. |
| BL117 | The cause list has an `unwired` bullet: `unwired.sh` found a non-test file the run's range adds that no other non-test file names → **process** failure; a human wires the file or removes it, then runs `release-issue.sh`. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described, with the way out (PRD 032, WN5). |
| BL118 | The `unwired` bullet says the bail records the listed files as pending (`.tdd/issue-<n>-unwired-pending`) and only a successful `release-issue.sh` (exit 0) accepts them, every pending file: a resume without a release bails `unwired` again, and a released run's resume goes on when every file `unwired.sh` prints is accepted and bails `unwired` again when one is not. (Cause list, `unwired` bullet.) | A file a stem search cannot see as wired has a way past that a human approved, not a meaningless reference (PRD 032, WN5). |
| BL177 | The cause list has a `surround-short` bullet: a legacy step's mutant table stayed shorter than `VERI_SURROUND_MUTANTS` after its one free relaunch of `develop-surround` → **process** failure. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described. |
| BL167 | The cause list has a `tidy-first-owed` bullet: a green leaf's `TIDY_FIRST:` step could not be inserted because `add-ledger-step.sh` exited 1 (the ledger at `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`), the bail naming the refactoring → **process** failure. | Every cause the procedure can bail with is named where bails are described (PRD 032, TI6). |
| BL178 | Just before sub-step 1, *Bailing* tells the conductor it may run `$S/bail.sh <n> <cause> <phase> --note-file <path> [--attempt <k>]` for sub-steps 1–8, and that on its exit 2, or with the script unavailable, it bails by the sub-steps below, every one of which stays. | One call bails a run, and a bail never depends on that call (ADR-0019; PRD 038.12, BLS6). |

## skills/tdd/SCOPE-LOOP.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SL1 | The file is read only when `## Inputs` of `SKILL.md` resolves the argument to a scope, a branch a single-issue run never takes; `$S` and every script, budget and role the file names are `SKILL.md`'s, already read by then. (Opening paragraph.) | `SKILL.md` stays small for every conductor request, and the loop file reuses its names instead of redefining them. |
| SL2 | A scope asks for a drain: `SKILL.md`'s Procedure, which drives one issue, run repeatedly inside a loop whose only new job is selection: which issue is next and when to stop. This is rung 2 of the scope ladder, and it lives in this file rather than a second skill so the selection layer and the scripts it calls are one thing; its steps are numbered `Loop 0`–`Loop 5` because they are their own sequence, not the Procedure's steps. (`## Draining a scope — the loop`, first paragraph.) | The loop adds selection and nothing else, and its step numbers are never confused with the Procedure's. |
| SL3 | Everything below the selection layer is unchanged, and the loop adds no new failure mode of its own: every outcome of it is one of rung 1's outcomes, a draft PR or a bail classified system or process, and the committed ledger makes any interrupted run resumable by re-invoking the same command. (`## Draining a scope — the loop`, second paragraph.) | A drain fails only the ways one issue fails, and an interrupted drain loses nothing. |
| SL4 | The loop never merges anything: each solved issue ends on a draft PR for a human, exactly as rung 1 leaves it. (`## Draining a scope — the loop`, last paragraph.) | Landing code stays a human's decision at any scale. |
| SL5 | `$ARGUMENTS` may carry `--continue-on-bail`, or the user may say "keep going past bails" (see Loop 4); it may also carry `--repo <owner/name>` to select a repository other than the current one, passed straight through to `solvable-issues.sh`. (`### Loop inputs`, first bullet.) | The two loop-level options, and which of them the loop does not interpret itself. |
| SL6 | `VERI_MAX_ISSUES` caps how many issues one invocation attempts, read as `${VERI_MAX_ISSUES:-0}`; 0 means no cap and is the default, because the solvable set is finite and the `ready-for-agent` label is already the human's opt-in; it is set for a short, watchable run. (`### Loop inputs`, second bullet.) | The default drains the whole pool, and the operator has a knob for a short run. |
| SL7 | The scope's selection label is derived from the scope's own word: the scope `plain` drains the bare `ready-for-agent` label (every issue opted in today carries it, so the bare invocation keeps draining them), and any other scope `<s>` drains `ready-for-agent:<s>`. That colon namespacing makes one label per loop the rule without anyone spelling a label, so `/tdd docs` and `/tdd scripts` draw from disjoint pools instead of racing for the same issues. (`### Loop inputs`, third bullet.) | Two scopes never race for one issue, and the bare command keeps its meaning. |
| SL8 | `solvable-issues.sh` knows nothing of scopes: it reads a label from the environment, so deriving the label is the conductor's job: it puts the derived label in `VERI_LABEL` before Loop 1 calls the script. (`### Loop inputs`, third bullet.) | The script stays scope-free, and the derivation has one owner. |
| SL9 | `VERI_LABEL` is an override of that derivation, so the label the loop drains is `${VERI_LABEL:-<the scope's label>}`; `solvable-issues.sh` reads it, so it is passed through the environment, not as an argument. Set, it moves the gate and does not widen it: the pool becomes exactly the issues carrying that name, whatever the scope derived, which is what a launcher needs when the pool it wants is one no scope word spells. (`### Loop inputs`, fourth bullet.) | A launcher can aim the loop at any one label without opening the pool to more. |
| SL10 | Two loops given the same `VERI_LABEL` value still collide, and that is the human's choice; set but empty is refused with exit 2 rather than matching every issue. (`### Loop inputs`, fourth bullet.) | A shared label is the operator's explicit decision, and an empty one never selects everything. |
| SL11 | `VERI_PARALLEL` is how many conductors run at once, read as `${VERI_PARALLEL:-1}`; at 1, the default, nothing about rung 2 changes and Loop 1–Loop 5 run as written. (`### Loop inputs`, fifth bullet.) | The sequential loop is the default and is untouched by the parallel option. |
| SL12 | Above 1 the loop is rung 3: run `$S/parallel-conductors.sh [--continue-on-bail]` detach-then-wait, with the derived label in `VERI_LABEL`, in place of Loop 1–Loop 4; then relay its report (one line per issue in launch order and the line naming what stopped it) as Loop 5's report, unedited. (`### Loop inputs`, fifth bullet.) | Parallel runs go through the one script that owns them, and the report reaches the user as the script wrote it. |
| SL13 | `VERI_PARALLEL` is 1 by default because N conductors are N concurrent `claude -p` sessions, billed as such; the guardrails hold unchanged at any value. (`### Loop inputs`, fifth bullet.) | Parallelism costs money and is opt-in, and it never relaxes a guardrail. |
| SL14 | Loop 0 has no preflight of its own and does not duplicate the Procedure's: the first single-issue run performs it (git repo, clean tree, `gh` and `claude` authenticated, suite green at baseline, default branch captured), and a poisoned baseline makes that run refuse to start before any branch or cycle exists; that refusal is a bail like any other, so it stops the loop there too, with nothing done and nothing to revert. (`### Loop 0. Preflight — none of its own`.) | One preflight, owned by rung 1, and a refusal ends the drain cleanly. |
| SL15 | Loop 1 runs `$S/solvable-issues.sh [--repo <owner/name>]`, which prints the numbers veri may take right now, one per line, ascending: exit 0 when there is at least one, exit 3 with empty stdout when there is none. (`### Loop 1. Select`.) | The loop's only selector and its two answers. |
| SL16 | On exit 3 the loop reports `nothing solvable` and stops, naming the label it drained, `${VERI_LABEL:-<the scope's label>}`, as in `nothing solvable under ready-for-agent:docs`; a loop that says `no ready-for-agent issue` while launched on another label sends the reader to the wrong list. It is not an error: no issue is open, carrying that label, unblocked and unclaimed, and nothing was started. (`### Loop 1. Select`, Exit 3 bullet.) | An empty drain names the pool it looked in. |
| SL17 | On exit 0 the loop takes the first number and does not keep the rest of the list. (`### Loop 1. Select`, Exit 0 bullet.) | The list is recomputed, never carried. |
| SL18 | The order is banded: `priority` bugs, then `priority`, then `bug`, then the rest, each ascending, a `low-risk` bug counting as the rest unless it is also `priority`, and an issue that blocks a `priority` issue inheriting that issue's band, so the first number is not always the lowest one; when it is not, the loop says so and names the band that did it (`#220 (priority) before #108`, `#120 (bug) before #117`), and for an inherited band also names the issue it came from, taken from `solvable-issues.sh`'s stderr (`#3050 (priority bug, blocks #3051) before #2897`), because a jump is otherwise invisible in the report and the operator who labelled the issue cannot tell the label was read. (`### Loop 1. Select`, Exit 0 bullet.) | A reordering by label is visible to the person who set the label, including when the label sits on the issue being held up rather than on the one taken. |
| SL19 | Loop 2 runs `SKILL.md`'s Procedure for that number end to end (its preflight, branch, ledger, cycle loop, bail semantics and draft PR) unchanged and un-second-guessed: the loop is the same agent, the run happens wherever that procedure puts it, and the loop does not relocate it or invent a branch of its own. (`### Loop 2. Solve one issue`, first paragraph.) | The loop adds no second way to solve an issue. |
| SL20 | The loop records the run's outcome: the PR url, or the phase and classification of its bail. (`### Loop 2. Solve one issue`, last paragraph.) | Loop 5's report has each issue's outcome to print. |
| SL21 | After each run the loop goes back to Loop 1 and runs `solvable-issues.sh` again, never continuing down the list it printed last iteration, which is stale: the issue just solved now has an open PR saying `Closes #<n>`, which removes it from the set, and closing its dependencies may have unblocked others; the list is recomputed every iteration, and that is the whole of the loop's state. (`### Loop 3. Re-select, do not walk the list`.) | Selection always reflects the repository as it is now. |
| SL22 | A bail stops the loop, system or process either one, unless the user passed `--continue-on-bail` or said to keep going past bails. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, first bullet.) | The first failure ends an unattended drain unless the operator asked otherwise. |
| SL23 | With `--continue-on-bail` the loop records the bail and continues at Loop 1; the bailed issue leaves a pushed branch and no PR, so it stays in the solvable set and would be retried immediately, so the loop skips it for the rest of this invocation rather than re-running it. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, first bullet.) | A bailed issue is never retried in a tight loop. |
| SL24 | A stop (the issue just run ended in a stop) makes the loop report and stop: it does not go back to Loop 1 and does not select another issue. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, second bullet.) | A stop ends the drain at that issue. |
| SL25 | Unlike a bail, a stop has no `--continue-on-bail` equivalent: a stop is the operator speaking, and the loop has no business overruling it. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, second bullet.) | An operator's stop is final for the invocation. |
| SL26 | Re-invoking the same command resumes the stopped issue from its committed ledger (the single-issue resume, no new machinery) and then carries on draining the scope. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, second bullet.) | A stopped drain continues with one command. |
| SL27 | An empty list, `solvable-issues.sh` exiting 3, is a stop condition: the drain is complete. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, third bullet.) | The loop ends when there is nothing left to take. |
| SL28 | The cap stops the loop when `${VERI_MAX_ISSUES:-0}` is non-zero and that many issues have been attempted; the loop says the cap stopped it and that re-invoking the same command continues. (`### Loop 4. Stop conditions`, fourth bullet.) | A capped drain says why it ended and how to go on. |
| SL29 | Loop 5 prints one line per issue attempted, in the order attempted, each starting `#<n> — ` and then one of: `<PR url>`, `bailed: <phase> (<system or process> failure)`, `stopped: <mode>` or `skipped: <why>`, separated in the file's template by `a vertical bar`. (`### Loop 5. Report`.) | One predictable line per issue, a stop carrying its mode. |
| SL30 | After the issue lines Loop 5 prints one line naming what stopped the loop: the bail, the stop and the stopped issue's mode, the empty list, or the cap. (`### Loop 5. Report`.) | The reader sees why the drain ended, not only what it did. |
| SL31 | If a run was left mid-issue, Loop 5 says that re-invoking the same command resumes it from its committed ledger, with no step repeated and none skipped. (`### Loop 5. Report`.) | The report tells the operator how to continue an interrupted issue. |
| SL32 | The loop adds no new failure mode: every outcome is one of rung 1's, and a conductor that finds itself inventing a state the loop can be in is doing rung 1's job over again and does not. (`### Loop guardrails`, first bullet.) | One failure taxonomy for every scale. |
| SL33 | The loop never merges and never marks a draft PR ready: a human lands the code. (`### Loop guardrails`, second bullet.) | Review and merge stay a human act. |
| SL34 | The loop never hand-picks an issue that `solvable-issues.sh` did not print and never re-orders the ones it did: the `ready-for-agent` label is the opt-in and `## Blocked by` is the ordering, and neither is the conductor's to override. (`### Loop guardrails`, third bullet.) | The human's labels and blockers decide what is taken and in what order. |
| SL35 | The loop recomputes and never caches: the stale-list bug is the one mistake available in it. (`### Loop guardrails`, fourth bullet.) | The one known way for this loop to go wrong is named. |
| SL36 | The loop does not touch a bailed issue's branch: it is pushed and diagnostic; the human reads it, or a later single-issue run resumes it from the ledger. (`### Loop guardrails`, fifth bullet.) | A bailed branch stays as evidence until a human or a resume uses it. |

## skills/tdd/STOPPING.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SP1 | Opening paragraph and sequence: a **stop** is not a bail. A bail ends an issue; a stop pauses one at an operator's word — `stop-requested.sh` printed a mode (`at-ledger`, `at-leaf` or `now`) at a checkpoint — and leaves the run resumable. The six numbered steps run in order. | A pause the operator asked for must not be taken for a failure, and the order is what keeps the tree and the record consistent. |
| SP2 | Step 1: before touching the tree, the conductor settles the leaves: it resets nothing while any `.tdd/<name>.pid` names a process still running `supervise.sh <name> …`, since a live leaf owns the working tree (#170). | A reset under a running leaf would destroy work the leaf is still writing. |
| SP3 | Step 1: for each such live leaf the conductor calls `$S/wait-leaf.sh <name>`, again on an exit 3 as everywhere else, until it returns 0, 1 or 2. | The wait ends only on an answer, so the tree is the conductor's by the time step 2 runs. |
| SP4 | Step 1: under mode `now` that wait ends at once, because `stop-run.sh` has already sent that supervisor its TERM and the supervisor recorded a 2 in the completion file. | Mode `now` has no leaf left to wait out, and the wait must not be mistaken for a hang. |
| SP5 | Step 2: the conductor first asks whether the tree is the run's to settle. If `git rev-parse --git-path rebase-merge` or `git rev-parse --git-path rebase-apply` names a directory that exists, or `MERGE_HEAD` resolves, an interrupted rebase or merge is in progress: it **resets nothing**, records `at=unsettled`, says in the comment what a human must finish (`git rebase --continue` or `--abort`, `git merge --abort`) and skips the rest of step 2. | Dropping that tree would throw away conflict resolutions no commit holds yet, and `git reset --hard HEAD` cannot put a half-finished rebase back. |
| SP6 | Step 2: otherwise, and only with `git reset --hard HEAD`, the conductor drops the tree; a non-empty `git status --porcelain` is the half-done step, dropped exactly as `<base directory>/BAIL.md` step 1 drops it and for the reason given there. | A half-done step no verifier saw is not kept, and a stop drops it the same way a bail does. |
| SP7 | Step 2: that reset is the only reach for the tree the section has; restoring a path out of some other tree is not what dropping a half-done step means. The conductor says in the narration what was dropped. | A narrower or wider reach than the reset would drop or keep the wrong files, and the operator reads what was lost. |
| SP8 | Step 3: the conductor pushes the branch with `$S/push-branch.sh`, so the run's commits and leaf logs live somewhere a resume can reach. Exit **0** is the normal path. | A resume on another machine needs the commits and logs on the remote. |
| SP9 | Step 3: on push-branch.sh exit **1** (the remote holds a branch this HEAD does not fast-forward) the conductor records it and does not fix it: it takes the remote's sha from the script's stderr, records `push=rejected sha=<remote-sha>` and says so in the comment. | The pause must record the divergence without acting on it. |
| SP10 | Step 3: a stop launches no leaves, so the reconcile leaf `<base directory>/RECONCILE.md` describes is not an option; a stop has no cause, so a rejected push is not a bail either. The run stays paused and resumable, and the resume reconciles. | Reconciling needs a leaf and a bail needs a cause; a stop has neither. |
| SP11 | Step 3: push-branch.sh exit **2** is recorded the same way as exit 1: the stop is not bailed, and the answer is named in the comment. | An unanswerable push is still not a reason to end a paused run. |
| SP12 | Step 4: the conductor marks the stop exactly once, with the boundary the run actually reached; `<at>` is read off the committed ledger. | One mark per pause keeps the record readable and each pause counted once. |
| SP13 | Step 4: `at=unplanned` when there is no ledger yet; `at=planned` when the ledger is committed and no step is green; `at=green` when the newest ledger commit ticked a step that still carries `⟂ refactor pending`; `at=step-done` when every ticked step has had that mark taken off again; `at=unsettled` when step 2 refused the tree. | A resume reads how far the work got from the mark alone. |
| SP14 | Step 4: `at=unsettled` wins over the other four values. | Where the tree stands is the first thing a resume has to deal with. |
| SP15 | Step 4: the mark is the command `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$(cat .tdd/issue-<n>.md \| $S/ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done)" $S/event.sh stop mode=<mode> at=<at>`. | The mark names the issue, the actor and the step the run paused at. |
| SP16 | Step 4: that one line is the whole record of the pause and is never a `bail` mark: a stop has no cause, so no system-versus-process label is derived and `classify-bail.sh` is not called. Both keys ride on the one line, `mode` for the checkpoint the operator's stop was taken at and `at` for how far the work got. | A reader asking "where did this run pause?" reads one mark, not two, and a stop is never counted as a bail. |
| SP17 | Step 5: the conductor renders `$S/metrics.sh <n>` after the mark, so it reads the mark, straight into a `### 📊 Metrics` block. | The stop appears in the numbers it produced. |
| SP18 | Step 5: it posts one `### ⏹️ Stopped — <mode>` comment by running `$S/comment.sh <n> stopped`, carrying that metrics block, the boundary reached (`at=<at>`), what step 2 dropped, the branch, the worktree path, any `push=rejected sha=<remote-sha>`, and the resume command `$S/run-conductor.sh <n>`; when `.tdd/restart-pending` names a live pid, the comment instead says the conductor is being restarted for context growth, and carries no operator wording and no resume command. | The operator sees what happened and the one command that resumes it. |
| SP19 | Step 5: the metrics go into the comment and nowhere else; **no metrics file is written or committed**, and the section stages and commits nothing. | Committing metrics marks an ending, as *Done* and BAIL.md do, and a stopped run will be resumed. |
| SP20 | Step 6: the conductor runs `$S/stop-requested.sh --clear` after the report, so the request does not outlive the stop it asked for and pause the resumed run at its first checkpoint, then ends the run. | A leftover request would stop the resume at once. |
| SP21 | Step 6: it opens **no** PR and readies none, leaves the worktree where it is, and leaves `.tdd/conductor.pid` in place. | The resume picks all three up, and the conductor exiting is what frees the issue (liveness is the whole rule, step 1.2). |
| SP22 | Step 1: a stop in mode `now` also ends the conductor's `detach-command.sh wait` on a detached command of its own (the baseline `fulltier`, `fasttier`): `stop-run.sh` has sent that command's whole process group TERM, then KILL after a bounded grace (`VERI_STOP_KILL_GRACE`, default 5 seconds), and the wait returns 2 at once, so that 2 is the stop being carried out and the run goes on to step 2, recorded as a stop and never as a bail or a preflight failure. | A `now` stop must end a run that is waiting on its baseline; reading that 2 as a red or dead baseline would bail a run the operator only paused. |
| SP23 | Step 1: when `stop-run.sh` could not signal a leaf or a detached command (a refused signal, not a process already gone) it exits 2 naming the pid and the run, having still written `.tdd/stop`; the process may then still be alive, so the conductor that reaches step 1 under `now` settles the leaves as step 1 says and does not treat the tree as free until each wait has returned. | A refused signal leaves a live process that owns the tree. |
| SP24 | Right after its opening paragraph, *Stopping* tells the conductor to run `$S/stop-settle.sh <n> <mode>` first, again on exit 3, with `--drop` only once it has judged a dirty tree the run's to settle, and on exit 1 or 2 to go on by the step its last line names; every step's prose stays. | One call settles a stop, and the prose stays the path for everything not clean (PRD 038.5, STS8). |
## skills/tdd/RECONCILE.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RC1 | `push-branch.sh` exit 1 means the remote holds a branch the run's HEAD does not fast-forward, usually the run's own pre-rebase history after a resume rebased onto `origin/main`; its stderr names the remote's sha. | The leaf is told which remote commit to reconcile with, and what exit 1 normally is. |
| RC2 | Whether those remote commits are the run's own or someone else's is a judgement, so a leaf makes it, launched through the supervisor like every other. | A judgement call goes to a leaf, and stuck detection lives only in the supervisor. |
| RC3 | Before the leaf is launched, the conductor copies the ledger `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` to `.tdd/reconcile-ledger.before`. | The after-check needs the ledger as it was. |
| RC4 | The leaf's prompt is built by `build-develop-prompt.sh reconcile`, given the issue spec, the step text `ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done` prints for the ledger, the test command, the branch and the remote sha, and written to `.tdd/develop-reconcile.prompt`. | The leaf sees the last done step and the exact remote sha its push must be reconciled against. |
| RC5 | Before the launch the conductor removes any `.tdd/develop-reconcile.exit` and `.tdd/develop-reconcile.pid` left by an earlier reconcile leaf. | A stale exit file cannot pass for this leaf's result. |
| RC6 | The leaf is launched through `detach-leaf.sh` running `supervise.sh develop-reconcile run-leaf.sh` on that prompt file, with `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, with `VERI_TEST_CMD` set to the test command, `VERI_ISSUE` to the issue number and `VERI_STEP` to the `current-or-last-done` step text, and with stdout and stderr written to `.tdd/develop-reconcile.out` and `.tdd/develop-reconcile.err`. | The leaf runs under stuck detection, and its environment names the issue, the step and the test command it is held to. |
| RC7 | The conductor waits for the leaf with `wait-leaf.sh develop-reconcile` in the foreground, and calls it again when it exits 3. | Exit 3 means the leaf is still running, not an answer. |
| RC8 | After the leaf exits 0, the conductor re-runs `push-branch.sh`, which must now exit 0. | The reconcile's purpose is a push that lands. |
| RC9 | After the leaf exits 0 the conductor also checks that the committed ledger `.tdd/issue-<n>.md` still carries every `[x]` tick and every ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark `.tdd/reconcile-ledger.before` had, by the stated `diff` of the done steps `ledger-steps.sh` reports for the before copy and for `git show HEAD:.tdd/issue-<n>.md`. | The leaf must not lose a done step or turn an unfinished refactor into a finished one. |
| RC10 | When the leaf exited 0, `push-branch.sh` exited 0 and the ledger check holds, the conductor continues where the push was called. | A successful reconcile resumes the run instead of ending it. |
| RC11 | The conductor bails with cause `reconcile-failed` when the leaf exits 1 or 2, when the second `push-branch.sh` exits other than 0, or when the ledger's ticks or marks changed. | A reconcile that did not land cleanly ends the run, named, rather than pushing anything further. |
| RC12 | `reconcile-failed` is a system failure: where the branch lives broke, not the methodology. | The bail is classified as plumbing, not as a rejected change. |
| RC23 | A `wait-leaf.sh develop-reconcile` exit 2 (stderr file `.tdd/develop-reconcile.err`) or a second `push-branch.sh` exit 2 (its stderr kept in `.tdd/push-branch.err`), with `adjudicate.sh left <n>` exit 0, launches `adjudicate-reconcile-failed` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `reconcile-failed`, `develop-reconcile` is not relaunched and `push-branch.sh` is not called again. (The paragraph after the ledger check.) | The site's two exit-2 sources, and RETRY reruns nothing here. |
| RC24 | A leaf exit 1, a second `push-branch.sh` exit 1 and a ledger whose ticks or marks changed are never adjudicated: each bails `reconcile-failed` as before. (The same paragraph.) | Only an exit 2 is eligible. |

## skills/tdd/WORKER.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| WK1 | Only a session born as a **worker** `<name>` reads `WORKER.md`: it is not resident in every conductor request the way `SKILL.md` is, and is read the way a scope run reads `SCOPE-LOOP.md` (stated in the opening paragraph). | The worker's half of the skill stays out of every conductor's context. |
| WK2 | The issues a worker works are the ones assigned to `<name>` in the work queue, `.tdd/workqueue` in the main working tree (opening paragraph). | A worker takes only its own issues, from the one queue file. |
| WK3 | `workqueue.sh` is the only thing that writes `.tdd/workqueue`: every state change in the worker loop is one of its calls, never an edit by hand (opening paragraph). | One writer keeps the queue's states consistent. |
| WK4 | The worker repeats the loop until `next` says there is nothing left (*The worker loop*, lead line). | The worker drains its queue and then stops. |
| WK5 | Step 1 is `workqueue.sh next <name>`: it prints the first `queued` issue of the worker's whose `## Blocked by` names no open issue; exit 3 with nothing printed means nothing is launchable, and the worker stops. | A blocked issue is not launched, and an empty result ends the loop. |
| WK6 | Step 2 is `workqueue.sh start <name> <issue>`, which moves the item `queued` → `running`; a refusal (exit 1) means the item is not the worker's or not `queued`, and the worker does not launch it. | A claim that was refused never becomes a run. |
| WK7 | Step 3: the worker launches `run-conductor.sh <issue>` detached and waits for it to finish. | The run is the conductor's, started detached and awaited. |
| WK8 | Step 4: the worker reads what the conductor left, one of two things: a draft PR or a bail. | The next queue move depends on which one it is. |
| WK9 | On a draft PR the worker merges it under the merge lock, then `workqueue.sh done <name> <issue> <merge-sha>` moves the item `running` → `merged`. | The queue records a merge only after the merge happened. |
| WK10 | On a bail the worker reads it; on the issue's second bail, `workqueue.sh park <name> <issue> <reason>` moves the item `running` → `parked`, recording why. | An issue that bails twice leaves the queue's runnable set with its reason kept. |
| WK11 | Step 5: the worker goes back to step 1. | The loop closes. |
| WK12 | The judgement calls, merging and reading a bail, stay the worker's, and no line in `.tdd/workqueue` is ever deleted (closing paragraph). | Deciding stays with the worker, and the queue keeps its whole history. |

## skills/tdd/RESUME.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RS1 | The committed ledger makes a run resumable: re-running `/tdd <n>` picks up where a previous run stopped (a bail, an interruption, or a human pause), without redoing finished steps. (Opening paragraph.) | A stopped run costs only the steps it had not finished. |
| RS2 | On a resume the issue's missing opt-in label is expected, since the claim removed it at the first launch; the conductor never stops or asks about it. (Second paragraph.) | Stopping on the missing label would stop every resume. |
| RS3 | On re-run, preflight step 3 calls `issue-worktree.sh` again; because it is idempotent it prints the same `.tdd/wt/<n>` path and creates nothing, so the `cd` puts the run back in the very worktree the previous run committed in: the branch is already checked out there, and nothing is re-created or switched. A branch only origin holds, whose issue carries this lane's `taken:<lane>` label, is resumed by recreating the worktree from origin's branch tip; another lane's claim is refused (exit 2). With no exact branch, the issue's one `tdd/<n>-*` branch is reused whatever slug is passed (a local one, or an origin one this lane holds); two or more such branches exit 2 naming each and the requested branch. (Third paragraph.) | The resume continues on the commits the previous run made. |
| RS4 | A resumed run's baseline runs on the branch head in that worktree, before any cycle of the resumed run starts, unless preflight step 3 skips it as already proven green: a matching green record on this machine, or a CI `push` run concluded `success` on exactly that sha. (Third paragraph.) | The baseline judges the tip the resume builds on. |
| RS5 | When that baseline is red, preflight step 3 runs the red suites on `origin/<default>`: in a throwaway checkout made with `git worktree add --detach <scratch> origin/<default>` (removed afterwards), it runs the test command restricted to the branch-red suites (`make test TESTS="<those suite paths>"` here) in the foreground, and writes `<default-red-file>`: one suite still red there per line, optionally followed by ` — ` and its first FAIL line, or the single line `none` when no suite is red there. (Third paragraph.) | Whether the red is the branch's own is measured on the default branch, not assumed. |
| RS6 | It then asks `$S/suite-red-resume.sh check .tdd/issue-<n>.md <branch-red-file> <default-red-file>`: exit 1 refuses as it always has, and exit 0 (every branch-red suite is green on the default branch, whatever the previous bail's cause) saves the branch-red list to `.tdd/resume-suite-red` and goes on. (Third paragraph.) | A run that bailed on suites only its branch broke can resume to repair them; any other red baseline still refuses. |
| RS7 | The resume check then finds an existing `.tdd/issue-<n>.md`, and when it does the four numbered sub-steps follow. (Third paragraph, last sentence.) | An existing ledger is what makes the run a resume. |
| RS8 | Sub-step 1: do not re-plan, since the ledger is the source of truth, except that the amended criteria are planned with `--planned` in sub-step 3. Re-write only the blind spec file (`.tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md`), since it is run scratch, not committed. | Finished steps are never planned again, and the uncommitted spec has to be rebuilt. |
| RS9 | Sub-step 1, ahead of sub-step 2's refactor clear and every later ledger commit: when the committed ledger holds step lines beyond its plan commit and there is no `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md`, run `$S/ledger-growth.sh record-existing .tdd/issue-<n>.md` once, before the resume's first ledger commit, and stage `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` in that same commit with the ledger. | Sub-step 2's refactor clear can be the resume's first ledger commit, so the record has to exist before it. |
| RS10 | Sub-step 2 opens by saying the run uses the reset preflight already made, then asks for a pending refactor. Whatever killed the previous run killed its leaf too, at whatever point the run had reached (a usage limit takes the conductor and every leaf together), so a resumed worktree may carry uncommitted work from a killed Red or Green leaf just as readily as from a killed refactor one. | Stray work is expected from any phase, not only from a refactor. |
| RS11 | Sub-step 2: no verifier has seen any of that work and none of it may reach the PR, so it is thrown away before the ask, not under one of its answers: preflight step 3's `resume-clean.sh` already captured `git status --porcelain` and ran `git reset --hard HEAD`, ahead of the resume rebase, and its stdout is `$stray`. | A resume with no pending step still discards the stray work, and the paths are captured before the reset. |
| RS12 | Sub-step 2: the conductor does not capture again here; the tree is clean, so a second capture would empty `$stray`. | The paths sub-step 4 has to name would be lost. |
| RS13 | Sub-step 2: `$stray` holds the paths that reset discarded, and sub-step 4 is what names them: a killed leaf's stray work is real work (the run that prompted this left a 235-line untracked test file behind) and it has to go loudly, not in silence. | Discarded work is reported, never lost silently. |
| RS14 | Sub-step 2: then `$S/next-pending-refactor.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` prints the first checked step whose refactor-pending mark is still there, a step the previous run left green but not tidied because it was killed between that step's green commit and its verify-refactor. Exit 3 means no step carries the mark: go to sub-step 3. Otherwise STEP is that description, and this step's refactor loop is re-entered where it stopped. | A green but untidied step is finished before any new step starts. |
| RS15 | Sub-step 2's code block computes, in order, `green="$(git log -1 --format=%H -- .tdd/issue-<n>.md)"`, `step_green=<step-green>`, `touched="$($S/refactor-touched.sh "$step_green")"`, `count="$(git log --format=%s "$green"..HEAD \| grep -c '^refactor:')"` and `left=$(( ${VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET:-5} - count ))`, with `left` floored at 0. | The re-entry is computed from the git history, never remembered. |
| RS16 | Sub-step 2 names two shas apart: `$green`, the cycle loop's `<green-sha>`, is the ledger's last commit past any re-pin step's, the span `$count` counts and verify-refactor's `<base-sha>`, the range it reviews; `$step_green`, `<step-green>`, is the step's own green tick and verify-refactor's `--step-green`, the commit whose files bound it (#4544), the newest commit whose change to the ledger touches a line other than a re-pin step's (`⟦diff-checked⟧ edit the case of the pin on …`), so a re-pin plan or tick committed while the step still carries the mark is passed over (#4510). It says no script prints `<step-green>` (`fused_step.green_tick` computes it inside `refactor-half.sh`) and to read it off `git log -p` of the ledger. The tick is atomic with the green commit and the clear that would follow never happened. So `$touched` (that commit's `git diff --name-only`, through the same script the cycle loop calls) is the same bounds the interrupted loop had, and `$count` the transforms it already committed. | The relaunched loop has the bounds and the history the interrupted one had. |
| RS17 | Sub-step 2: run the Refactor loop (the cycle loop's sub-section) with `$touched` as its bounds and `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET=$left` in the environment of every call that reads it: `refactor-budget.sh`, `build-develop-prompt.sh refactor` and `leaf-timeout.sh`. | Every reader of the budget sees the reduced number. |
| RS18 | Sub-step 2: one leaf is handed the budget as a number and spends it, so the relaunched leaf gets the run's budget reduced by `$count`, never the ambient one: on the ambient number the interrupted step pays for its tidying twice over, which is the cost the budget exists to bound. | An interruption never doubles a step's tidying budget. |
| RS19 | Sub-step 2: a `$left` of 0 is a budget already spent, and `refactor-budget.sh 0` then skips the leaf; the step is tidied as far as it was funded to be. | A spent budget launches no leaf. |
| RS20 | Sub-step 2: unless `refactor` is listed in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify`, run its verify-refactor after it, the check the interruption skipped, with `$green` as the cycle loop's `<green-sha>` and `$step_green` as its `--step-green`: `verify.sh --branch-base <base> --step-green "$step_green" --step "<STEP>" --fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>' refactor <spec-file> <test-path> "$green"`, launched as in the cycle loop. | The tidying the interruption left is still judged blind, bounded by the step's green commit's files and reviewing from the ledger's last commit, unless the run switched that verifier off. |
| RS21 | Sub-step 2: the verify-refactor's PASS clears the mark and commits the cleared ledger exactly as in the cycle loop; then the run comes back to sub-step 2 and asks it again. | Every pending step is drained before sub-step 3. |
| RS22 | Sub-step 3: `next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` gives the first unchecked step; the cycle loop continues from exactly there, so STEP is that step's description, bound here instead of at the top of the cycle loop. Already-checked steps (and their commits) are kept. | The resume starts at the first step not done. |
| RS23 | Sub-step 3: before that pick, plan each criterion that is unticked in the issue body when the resume starts and that no ledger step's `⟦criterion k⟧` serves (scope a human amended in) with `add-ledger-step.sh --planned --criterion k`, one call per criterion, committed as a `plan:` commit before the cycle loop with `git add .tdd/issue-<n>.md .tdd/issue-<n>-added.md`, so the insertion records travel with the ledger. | An amended issue's new criteria are planned before the cycle loop. |
| RS24 | Sub-step 3: those steps are planned steps, not `⟦added⟧` growth, and do not count against `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`. Every other insertion during the resume omits `--planned`, stays `⟦added⟧` and counts. | Scope a human added is not growth; growth during the resume still is. |
| RS25 | Sub-step 3: a conductor never sets or raises `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`, `REVISE_BUDGET`, `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET`, `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` or `VERI_NO_VERDICT_RETRIES` mid-run, in its environment or in a scratch helper, and a budget it cannot fit inside is a bail. | The budgets bound the run only if the run cannot move them. |
| RS26 | Sub-step 4: post a `### ▶️ Resuming` comment by running `$S/comment.sh <n> resuming --note-file <path>`, naming that step and, when `$stray` is non-empty, the paths it lists, said as what they are: the stray work sub-step 2's reset discarded, seen by no verifier and now gone. | The thread says where the run picked up and what it threw away. |
| RS27 | Sub-step 4: mark the pick-up in the log with `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$(cat .tdd/issue-<n>.md \| $S/ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done)" $S/event.sh resume`. | The run log shows the resume beside the comment it mirrors. |
| RS28 | Sub-step 4: then, when `.tdd/resume-suite-red` exists, run `$S/suite-red-resume.sh insert .tdd/issue-<n>.md .tdd/resume-suite-red`, commit the ledger with `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md`, and hand the inserted `⟦suite-repair⟧` step to the cycle loop first. When `insert` exits 1 (the growth cap filed the step as owed), bail with cause `suite-red`, naming the branch-red suites; when it exits 2 (no answer), bail with cause `no-verdict`. When `insert` inserted nothing because the red was only a stale grade of a unit with a pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` step, hand that `⟦skill-edit⟧` step to the cycle loop first. | The repair the resume was let in for runs first, a refused insertion ends the run with its cause named, and a stale grade is regraded by the step that owns the unit instead of a repair leaf that cannot fix it (#2884). |
| RS29 | Sub-step 4: then reuse the claim the previous run left on the branch, which every tick of the cycle loop edits and step 4 rewrites: `gh api 'repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls?head={owner}:{branch}' -q '.[0].html_url // empty'` gives `<pr-url>`, the PR step 2.3 opened, and `<pr-number>` is its last path segment. A resumed run skips step 2 outright, so the url is read off the branch, never remembered. | The resume edits the PR the branch already has. |
| RS30 | Sub-step 4: the resume edits that PR and opens no second one: a second PR-creating `gh api` call against a branch that already has an open PR is either a refusal that ends the resume where it started, or two pages claiming one issue, the failure #195 exists to prevent. | One issue, one PR. |
| RS31 | Sub-step 4: only when that lookup prints nothing is there no open PR to reuse, because the previous run was killed inside step 2.3's own window, between the plan commit and the PR-creating `gh api` call that follows it. Then, and only then, run that sub-step's push and PR-creating `gh api` call here; opening the first PR a branch has ever had is not opening a second. | A run killed before its PR existed still gets one. |
| RS32 | Sub-step 4: then run the cycle loop as normal. Go to the draft PR only when `next-pending-refactor.sh` and `next-unchecked.sh` have both exited 3: every step is checked, and every checked step's refactor is verified. | A resumed run never readies a PR over untidied or unfinished steps. |
| RS33 | Sub-step 4: an unchecked `⟦full-suite⟧` or `⟦human-owed⟧` line does not hold the run back from its draft PR, since no cycle ticks either and `next-unchecked.sh` skips both. | Lines the cycle loop never ticks cannot block the draft PR. |
| RS34 | Because each step was checked off atomically with its green commit, the ledger and the git history agree on exactly what is done, so resume never repeats or skips a step. (Closing paragraph.) | The ledger can be trusted as the record of what is done. |
| RS35 | Before preflight step 3 runs the branch-red suites on `origin/<default>`, the conductor runs each test file the red baseline names alone once, with the repo's own runner (`make test TESTS="<file>"` on veri). When all of them pass, it writes a `suite-flake` event naming the files, writes no `.tdd/resume-suite-red`, and goes on as on a green baseline. When any stays red, or the output names no file, the baseline takes today's path. | A load flake on resume is not a defect of the branch, and must not buy the run a repair step with nothing to repair. |
| RS36 | Before the first step leaf, the resume compares the newest `plan:` commit to the last verify-plan PASS and runs verify-plan only when the plan commit is newer; a REJECT then follows step 2c's revise-plan route. | Without the comparison a resume either never re-verifies a rewritten plan or re-pays verify-plan on every resume of an unchanged one. |
| RS37 | Sub-step 4: when `insert` exits 2 naming a reorder of pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` steps, the resume bails with cause `no-verdict` carrying that stderr line, and inserts no step. | The operator reads the needed reorder from the bail, not from a failed repair leaf. |
| RS38 | Sub-step 2, at "Run its **verify-refactor** after it", says that with `refactor` in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no verify-refactor launches and the cycle loop's *Refactor loop* step 5 **Switched off** branch is taken instead: the `verify-refactor-skipped reason=switched-off` event, `skipped` in `.tdd/verdict-refactor.exit`, the `SKIPPED (switched off)` comment, then `pin-recheck.sh`, the pending-mark clear and the cleared-ledger commit, before sub-step 2 is asked again. | A resume read the verifier as unconditional and spent the one the run had pinned off; the pin, not the environment, rules a resumed run (PRD 018.1). |
| RS229 | Sub-step 2 says `$stray` is the paths on the `stray=` lines of `$S/preflight.sh`'s output (its last lines on exit 0, the lines before its `stopped at` line on a stop), not a capture the conductor makes or holds. | The value moves from the conductor's memory to the script's output (PRD 027 D7), and a second capture would empty it. |
| RS230 | Just before sub-step 1, *Resume* tells the conductor to run `$S/resume-start.sh <n>` first, again on exit 3, to take `step`, `pr_url` and `verify_plan` from its last lines on exit 0, and on exit 1 or 2 to go on by the sub-step its last line names; every sub-step's prose stays. | One call resumes a run, and the prose stays the path for everything not clean (PRD 038.6, RSS6). |

## skills/tdd/REVISE.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RV1 | A `REJECT` (verify.sh exit 1) does not stop the run. The conductor gets a bounded attempt budget, `REVISE_BUDGET` revise attempts, default 2, to satisfy the blind verifier before bailing the step. (Opening paragraph.) | A rejected phase gets a bounded number of revisions, then ends the run. |
| RV2 | The rejection reason is the carry-forward signal: leaves never share context, so each revise leaf is told explicitly what to fix. (Opening paragraph.) | A fresh leaf can only fix what it is told. |
| RV3 | A revise of red, green or refactor is built from a prompt that asks for no `SUITE:` line, so its wait exits 0 straight into its verifier and nothing checks for the line. The `diff` revise does ask for one; its check is stated in DIFF-CHECKED.md. (Second paragraph.) | The conductor does not run `suite-reported.sh` after a leaf that was never asked for a `SUITE:` line. |
| RV4 | The phases sent to `build-develop-prompt.sh revise <phase>` are red, green, refactor, transform, diff, rebase and the interface phases expand, beside, cover-new, dual-stub, migrate, contract-a, contract-b. A rejected transform is revised as `revise transform`, never mapped to `refactor`. A rebase rejection (the Draft PR step's rebase verifier) is revised as `revise rebase`. (Third paragraph.) | Each rejected phase is redone by the template written for it. |
| RV5 | A revise of green whose leaf reports `WRONG_TEST: <why>` is exempt from the `SUITE:` line and is routed as a develop-green `WRONG_TEST:` report is: `$S/wrong-test-route.sh --spec .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md .tdd/revise-green-<k>.out '<target-test-id>'` runs before the attempt is counted. Its exit 0 writes no `revise` mark, spends no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt and launches the `red-contradicted` red; its exit 3 keeps the attempt's main outcome, a verify-green REJECT; its exit 2 is a bail, never a `report-missing` bail. (Fourth paragraph.) | A test the criterion contradicts is the red's to fix, and costs the step no revise attempt. |
| RV6 | A `repair <case>` answer from the route launches `red-repair` over the revise-green report instead. (Fourth paragraph.) | A contradicted case that can be repaired gets a repair red, not a new one. |
| RV7 | A revise of green answering `IMPOSSIBLE` with a `MISSING_TEST:` line is routed the same way (`$S/wrong-test-route.sh --spec .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md .tdd/revise-green-<k>.out '<target-test-id>'`), as is a verify-green or mutation-parity REJECT reason before any revise. On `missing <behaviour>` the conductor writes a `reroute` event, writes no `revise` mark, spends no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, launches the `red-missing` red with that file as its brief (`build-develop-prompt.sh red-missing`), sends the added case through verify-red (verify-pin when it reports `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES`), re-records the step's frozen tests with `tests-frozen.sh record --step`, then runs green again. (Fourth paragraph.) | A missing case is added by a red and verified, not forced out of a green leaf that cannot write it. |
| RV8 | The route runs with `VERI_STEP` set to the current step, and its exit 0 writes a `reroute` event with `VERI_STEP` before the launch. The event carries `kind=<kind>`. A `missing` route is free up to `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` per step, and its exhaustion (`missing-exhausted`, exit 1) bails with cause `missing-reroutes-exhausted`; a repeat red, repair, supersede or fixture reroute in the step takes the revise path and spends one `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, except a `WRONG_TEST` that names no case: it routes `repair` once more after the free reroute, and the next one exits 1 (`repair-exhausted`) and bails with cause `repair-reroutes-exhausted`; it never takes the revise path. (Fifth paragraph.) | A step that keeps rerouting cannot loop for free, and a wrong test nobody named is never sent to a revise that cannot touch it. |
| RV9 | For attempt `k = 1 … REVISE_BUDGET` the loop runs five steps in order: Same reason?, Mirror, Fresh developer leaf, Check the prediction first (red only), Re-verify (blind, same phase). (Loop list.) | Each attempt has the same shape, so a log reader can tell where one stopped. |
| RV10 | Mirror (step 2): first build the revise prompt with step 3's `build-develop-prompt.sh revise <phase>` line; on its exit 2 post no comment, write no `revise` mark, launch no leaf and bail as a system failure. Only on exit 0 post a `### ♻️ Revise <phase> — attempt <k>/<REVISE_BUDGET>` comment carrying the verifier's rejection reason verbatim, and mark the same attempt in the log with `event.sh revise budget=<REVISE_BUDGET>`, run with `VERI_ISSUE`, `VERI_ACTOR=conductor`, `VERI_STEP` from `ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done`, `VERI_PHASE=<phase>` and `VERI_ATTEMPT=<k>`. | The thread and the run log both show each attempt and what it was told, and never an attempt that could not start. |
| RV11 | Fresh developer leaf (step 3): the revise prompt is built from the `revise` template by `VERI_ISSUE=<n> build-develop-prompt.sh revise <phase>` with the spec, the current step, the test command, the reason file `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` (the file the verifier's stdout went to) and the fast command, into `.tdd/revise-<phase>-<k>.prompt`. | The leaf is told the phase being redone and where its objection is. |
| RV12 | Before the leaf launches, the conductor runs `rm -f` on `.tdd/revise-<phase>-<k>.exit`, `.tdd/revise-<phase>-<k>.pid`, `.tdd/red-prediction.md` and `.tdd/red-run-output.txt`. | A previous leaf's completion and prediction files are never read as this one's. |
| RV13 | The revise leaf is launched through the supervisor (`detach-leaf.sh` over `supervise.sh revise-<phase>-<k>` over `run-leaf.sh`, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`) with `VERI_TEST_CMD`, `VERI_ISSUE` and `VERI_STEP` set, and waited on in the foreground with `wait-leaf.sh revise-<phase>-<k>` (on exit 3 call it again). A supervisor exit 2 is a bail, a system failure, after the stop check in *Launching a leaf*. | Stuck detection lives in the supervisor, and a stop request is honoured before a killed leaf is read as stuck. |
| RV14 | After the wait the conductor runs `step-cost.sh` on the `next-unchecked.sh` step (the step's cost check), only when `<phase>` is red or green. A refactor-phase revise never runs it, because the refactor phase comes after the step's green commit and *The step's cost check* runs no check past that commit. | A revise that blows the step's cost is caught for the phases the check covers, and for no others. |
| RV15 | The reason travels as a file, not as prose the conductor pastes in; that is why the verify step redirected it. The template tells the leaf it is fresh, that the reason is the whole of what it has to work from, that the work is on disk, and that it must answer that objection only. | The leaf answers the verifier's words, not the conductor's paraphrase. |
| RV16 | The revise leaf's report ends with `OUTCOME: REWRITTEN` or `IMPOSSIBLE` and `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: FAILS`, `PASSES` or `N/A`. `N/A` is the value whenever the leaf touched no test, which is every revise of green or refactor. | The conductor reads two fixed lines, and a revise that touched no test says so. |
| RV17 | `IMPOSSIBLE` with no `MISSING_TEST:` line is a legitimate answer, not a crash: the leaf says no revision can meet the objection, so the run spends no further attempt, launches no re-verify, and bails at once with cause `revise-impossible`, carrying the leaf's stated reason. | A second leaf receives the same answer at a full leaf's cost, and `revise-exhausted` would send the reader to a test that is correct. A bail never ticks a step, so the leaf's account still never replaces a verifier's PASS. |
| RV18 | Check the prediction first (step 4, red only): when `<phase>` is red and the revise leaf's report says `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: FAILS`, the leaf rewrote the failing test and reported its own `PREDICTED_FAILURE:` and `PREDICTION:` lines, so before anything reaches a verifier the conductor runs the gate the Red section runs, `red-divergence.sh .tdd/red-prediction.md .tdd/red-run-output.txt < .tdd/revise-red-<k>.out > .tdd/verify-red.reason`, and reads its exit code. A revise of red whose report says `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` is still a pin: it runs no prediction gate and goes to the verify-pin re-verify. | A rewritten red test is held to its prediction exactly as the original was; a pin has no failure to predict, so a missing prediction cannot be its defect. |
| RV19 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 1 (the prediction file is missing or newer than the run's output): narrate "prediction not written before the run", not a divergence, write that line to `.tdd/verify-red.reason`, and spend attempt `k` without re-verifying, exactly as a `REJECT` does. | A prediction written after the run is not a prediction, but is not called a divergence either. |
| RV20 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 3 (matched): nothing was surprising; carry on to the re-verify. | A matched prediction goes to the blind verifier. |
| RV21 | `red-divergence.sh` exit 0 or 2 (diverged, or a prediction nobody can read): the rewritten test failed for a reason other than the one it was written to catch (exit 0), or its report carries no readable `PREDICTION:` line (exit 2), handled the same way. The test is the suspect and verify-red is blind by construction, so attempt `k` is spent without re-verifying: skip the re-verification entirely, keep the reason the script wrote to `.tdd/verify-red.reason` (on exit 2 write your own one-line reason there), mark it with `event.sh prediction-diverged`, and treat the attempt exactly as a `REJECT` does: if attempts remain loop to attempt `k+1` with that reason, otherwise fall through to the budget-exhausted bail. | A surprise about the test goes to the next revise attempt and the run log, not to a verifier that cannot see it. |
| RV22 | Beside the `prediction-diverged` mark the conductor preserves the attempt with `preserve-attempt.sh <n> <step> revise-red <k> prediction-diverged`, best-effort: if it fails, note it and carry on. | The diverged attempt's work survives for inspection, and its failure never stops the run. |
| RV23 | Every other phase's revise template writes no `PREDICTION:` line, so the prediction check is the red phase's alone. | The check runs only where the report carries what it reads. |
| RV24 | When `<phase>` is green, the conductor runs `tests-frozen.sh check --step <step>` before the re-verify. On exit 1 it writes a reason naming the changed file to `.tdd/verify-green.reason`, spends attempt `k` as a REJECT that spends `REVISE_BUDGET`, and launches no verifier for that attempt; on exit 0 the verifier is launched as before. | A revise of green that changes a frozen test is rejected without a verdict. |
| RV25 | On `tests-frozen.sh` exit 2 the check could not answer, so the conductor bails as a system failure, never a REJECT, and launches no verifier. | A check that could not run is the plumbing's failure, not the methodology's. |
| RV26 | Re-verify (step 5, blind, same phase): the conductor runs `rm -f` on `.tdd/verdict-<phase>.exit` and `.tdd/verdict-<phase>.pid`, launches `verdict-<phase>` through `detach-leaf.sh` and `supervise.sh` over `run-verdict.sh --branch-base <base> --step <step> --fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>' <phase> .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md <test-path> [target]`, with `--step-green "$step_green"` (the step's own green tick the *Refactor loop* computed) added before `<phase>` for `refactor` alone, which exits 2 without it (#4544), with stdout overwriting `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` and stderr to `.tdd/verify-<phase>.err`, and waits in the foreground with `wait-leaf.sh verdict-<phase>` (on exit 3 call it again). Overwriting the reason file means attempt `k+1` carries the newest objection. | Every attempt is judged by a fresh blind verdict and passes on only its newest objection. |
| RV27 | The `--fast-cmd` rides along whatever the phase. Green, refactor, transform, diff, break and the interface phases (expand, cover-new, migrate, contract-a, contract-b, beside, dual-stub) refuse to build without it (exit 2), and their prompts have the verifier run it. Red's prompt has the verifier run it once and REJECT a red that fails it for any reason other than the target test's expected failure. The pin, plan and rebase prompts name no fast command, so it changes nothing there. | One launch shape serves every phase, and a re-verified red is held to the same command as the first. |
| RV28 | Both `rm -f`s matter in this loop: every attempt re-verifies the same phase, so the previous attempt's completion files would otherwise be read as this one's answer. | A stale completion file would pass a verdict that was never reached. |
| RV29 | Re-verify PASS (exit 0): the step is recovered. Post `### 🔎 verify-<phase> (revised) — PASS` with the reason by running `$S/comment.sh <n> verify-revised`, and resume the cycle from where that phase's PASS would continue. | A recovered step rejoins the cycle where it left. |
| RV30 | When `<phase>` is red, before resuming run `tests-frozen.sh record --step <step> <test-path>`: a revised red's PASS records the step's test files to the step's own record, as the Red section's does. `tests-frozen.sh` exit 2 is a bail. | A revised red's tests are frozen for the green that follows. |
| RV31 | Re-verify REJECT (exit 1): if attempts remain, loop; otherwise fall through to the budget-exhausted branch. | A rejected attempt is a normal outcome of the loop. |
| RV32 | Re-verify malformed or killed-stuck (exit 2): bail, a system failure. | No verdict is the plumbing's failure, not a rejection. |
| RV33 | The `diff` phase: a REJECT on a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step runs the same loop with `<phase>` set to `diff`, with two differences: no `step-cost.sh` line and no prediction check, since the step has no failing test, and a re-verify that is the blind `diff` verifier rather than one with a test path. (Paragraph *The `diff` phase*.) | A step with no test is revised without the checks that need one. |
| RV34 | The `diff` revise leaf is built as `VERI_ISSUE=<n> build-develop-prompt.sh revise diff .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md <step> '<test-cmd>' .tdd/verify-diff.reason '<fast-cmd>' > .tdd/revise-diff-<k>.prompt` and launched as `revise-diff-<k>` like any revise leaf. (Paragraph *The `diff` phase*.) | The diff revise is told the same objection file and fast command as any other. |
| RV35 | The `diff` re-verify is `run-verdict.sh --step "<STEP>" --fast-cmd '<fast-cmd>' --branch-base <base> diff <spec-file> <step-base-sha>`, launched as `verdict-diff` with its stdout overwriting `.tdd/verify-diff.reason`. Each base is the same base sha the original verify-diff verdict ran against: `<base>` the run's recorded branch base, and the step base sha recorded before the step's develop-diff leaf ran, not one recorded before the revise leaf, so the revised work is judged against the diff the rejection was about. (Paragraph *The `diff` phase*.) | A revised diff is judged against the same diff that was rejected. |
| RV36 | A recovered PASS on the `⟦diff-checked⟧` step is ticked exactly as the `⟦diff-checked⟧` PASS bullet in DIFF-CHECKED.md describes. (Paragraph *The `diff` phase*.) | A revised diff finishes the step the way an unrevised one does. |
| RV37 | If all `REVISE_BUDGET` attempts are still `REJECT`, the step is not bailed on the spot. A criterion no blind verifier could be satisfied about in that many attempts is usually one carrying more than one behaviour, the condition `SPLIT:` exists for, so the conductor sends one last leaf and asks it which of the two is true of this step, rather than deciding itself. The reason file the last re-verify overwrote is the whole of what that leaf is told. (Exhausted budget.) | An unsatisfiable step gets one question about itself before the run ends. |
| RV38 | The exhausted leaf is built with `build-develop-prompt.sh exhausted .tdd/issue-<n>-spec.md <step> '<test-cmd>' .tdd/verify-<phase>.reason > .tdd/develop-exhausted.prompt`; the conductor runs `rm -f` on `.tdd/develop-exhausted.exit` and `.tdd/develop-exhausted.pid`, launches it as `develop-exhausted` through `detach-leaf.sh` and `supervise.sh` over `run-leaf.sh` (`--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, `VERI_TEST_CMD`, `VERI_ISSUE`, `VERI_STEP` set), and waits in the foreground with `wait-leaf.sh develop-exhausted` (on exit 3 call it again). | It is launched like every other leaf, through the supervisor. |
| RV39 | The exhausted leaf answers in prose around its report lines, so its answer is read with the script, never by eye: `exhausted-outcome.sh < .tdd/develop-exhausted.out > .tdd/exhausted-answer.txt; echo $?`. Exit 0 is the split, exit 1 the step needs the user and is parked, exit 3 neither answer; each is a branch of its own. | Which outcome it holds is an exit code, never a judgement made per run. |
| RV40 | Exit 0, split: `.tdd/exhausted-answer.txt` holds the criteria the leaf named, one per line, and they replace the step nothing could satisfy rather than queue ahead of it, because the criterion itself was what was wrong. | A criterion that cannot be satisfied is replaced, not retried. |
| RV41 | Exit 0 (split) bullet: the criteria replace the step by the replacement split `SKILL.md`'s *The step's cost check* states on its exit 0, the tree restored first because the step's rejected work is uncommitted and would otherwise become the first replacement step's inherited start; the bullet names that split rather than restating its commands. | The replacement steps start from the last commit, by the one statement of the split. |
| RV42 | Exit 0 (split) bullet: the bullet holds no code block of the split's own (no `git reset`, `git clean`, `event.sh`, `add-ledger-step.sh` or `git commit` line) and names the two values that differ from the cost check's split: the criteria come from `.tdd/exhausted-answer.txt`, and `step-split` carries `at=exhausted`. | The log and the ledger still show the plan grew, and why, without a second copy to drift. |
| RV45 | The `at=exhausted` on `step-split` tells this split apart from a Green leaf's volunteered one: both grow the plan through `add-ledger-step.sh` and both write `step-added` and `step-split`, so without it a log reader cannot say whether the plan grew because a leaf asked or because the budget ran out. | Those are different facts about the run. |
| RV46 | Then the conductor loops back to `next-unchecked.sh`, which now hands over the first replacement step. The ledger is committed before the run goes on because the committed ledger is what a resumed run reads: uncommitted, the replacement steps are lost and the step that could not be satisfied is handed out again. | A resumed run continues from the replacement steps. |
| RV47 | `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 bails with cause `step-too-big` (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`), exactly as a Green leaf's volunteered split does when it meets `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`: the criteria are filed under `## Owed steps` and the plan is unchanged. Exit 2 bails with cause `step-add-refused` (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`). | A refused growth ends the run with the plan intact and the cause named. |
| RV48 | Exit 1, the step needs the user: `.tdd/exhausted-answer.txt` holds the leaf's answer (the criterion is wrong, contradicts another, or needs a decision this run has no authority to make). Park the step: stop the run there, carrying that answer and the verifier's last rejection reason (`.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason`), and leave the plan as it is so the user's decision picks the run back up. | A decision the run cannot make is handed to the user with its evidence. |
| RV49 | On the park the conductor posts a `### ⏸️ Parked — <phase> (needs the user)` comment carrying `.tdd/exhausted-answer.txt` verbatim and that rejection reason, so what the user has to decide about is on the thread they already read rather than in a leaf log inside a worktree. | The decision is where the user reads. |
| RV50 | The park is a deliberate escalation, not a failure to split, so it carries a mark of its own, `event.sh step-escalated anchor=<step>` (with `VERI_ISSUE`, `VERI_ACTOR=conductor`, `VERI_STEP` set from `current-or-last-done`), rather than the split's or the bail's. It is the one outcome that stops the run without bailing it, and a log holding neither reads as a run that stopped saying nothing. | A parked run is told apart from a silent one. |
| RV51 | Exit 3, neither answer: the leaf volunteered no split and asked for no decision, so there is nothing new to act on. Bail the step with cause `revise-exhausted` as a process failure (the methodology could not satisfy the blind verifier within budget), recording the last rejection reason. | An exhausted budget with no new information ends the run, labelled as methodology. |
| RV52 | A supervisor exit 2 on the exhausted leaf is a bail like any other developer leaf's (`killed-stuck`, a system failure): nothing answered, so no outcome was reported. | No answer is the plumbing's failure. |
| RV53 | `REVISE_BUDGET` (verdict axis) is independent of the supervisor's `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` (liveness axis) and of `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET` (cost axis): the first governs a leaf that finished with a bad verdict, the second a leaf that never produced one, the third how many transforms one step's tidying is worth. | Different failures stay different decisions. |
| RV54 | A `REJECT` is veri working as designed, the blind verifier refusing to rubber-stamp, so the bounded revise is a correction, never an excuse to keep hammering past the budget. | The budget is a cap, not a target. |
| RV55 | Step 1 **Same reason?** runs before anything about attempt `k` is posted or logged: it copies `.tdd/verify-<phase>.reason` to `.tdd/verify-<phase>-<k>.reason`, because the re-verify below — and before it the red prediction check and the green frozen-tests check — overwrites that file. On attempt 1 the comparison is skipped, there being no previous attempt. On every later attempt it runs `$S/same-reject.sh .tdd/verify-<phase>-<k-1>.reason .tdd/verify-<phase>.reason`: exit 0 means attempt `k-1` was rejected for the same reason it was revising, and the loop goes to the `develop-exhausted` leaf without posting attempt `k`'s comment, writing its `revise` mark or launching it; exit 1 or exit 2 continues the loop. | A second rejection for the reason the attempt was already answering is a revise loop that has stopped learning, so it ends the loop instead of spending the budget. |
| RV56 | A red leaf relaunched by the revise loop is given a tree with no production change beyond `HEAD`: when `<phase>` is red the conductor first runs `$S/restore-production.sh <test-path>` (the step's test path from the Red report) before launching `revise-red-<k>`, keeping the red's own new or changed test; exit 2 bails as a system failure (`no-verdict`). | #2495: a red revise after a rejected green inherited the green's uncommitted edit, so its re-verification saw the target test pass. |
| RV57 | The `diff` phase paragraph states that a `diff` REJECT whose reason names a pytest, Go or JS case as renamed and changed launches no revise leaf and spends no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt: the conductor replaces the step with two steps through `add-ledger-step.sh instead-of` (amend under the old name, then rename with the body unchanged) and commits the plan, and `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 bails `step-too-big` (#2594). | No revise can satisfy that shape; a split is the only fix, and a revise attempt spent on it is spent for nothing. |
| RV58 | A red revise whose report says `PREDICTION: DIVERGED` leaves `.tdd/verify-red.reason` with the leaf's `FAIL_OUTPUT:` and `PREDICTION_NOTE:` values (step 4), so the next attempt and the bail comment carry the observed failure. | The reason is the whole of what the next leaf works from. |
| RV59 | A verify-pin REJECT of the case a `missing` reroute added is routed by PIN.md's REJECT branch, never straight to a revise of red: `Read` `<base directory>/PIN.md` and run `$S/pin-reject-route.sh --missing-reroute .tdd/verify-pin.reason .tdd/issue-<n>.md "$($S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md)" <target-test>` (the step's `TARGET_TEST`). Exit 0 retires the step (no `revise` mark, no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt, green not re-run), exit 1 (also a failing target test) enters the revise loop for red, exit 2 bails. (Fourth paragraph.) | #3261 step 9: a conductor revised red instead of retiring an unpinnable step. |
| RV60 | A REJECT from the verify-pin re-verify of a revised red whose report says `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES` runs the same `$S/pin-reject-route.sh` call, passing the `TARGET_TEST` of the step's red report (`.tdd/develop-red.out`, never the revise report) as the final `<target-test>` argument (with `--missing-reroute` on a step reached by a `missing` reroute) before attempt `k+1`; its exit 0 retires the step and ends the loop, exit 1 (also a failing target test) continues the loop, exit 2 bails. (Re-verify REJECT bullet.) | An unpinnable step is retired, not revised until the budget is gone. |
| RV61 | The `diff` revise leaf commits its change, and the conductor keeps that commit as made, with no `git reset --soft`, squash or re-commit, before the `diff` re-verify judges `<step-base-sha>..HEAD`. | A redo's commit is part of the diff the re-verify judges, so history stays as the leaf wrote it. |
| RV62 | REVISE.md routes the case a `missing` reroute adds the way SKILL.md states. When the step's own red reported `AGAINST_UNMODIFIED: PASSES`, verify-pin's `<target-test>` is what `pin-target.sh .tdd/develop-red.out …` prints (the step's own red report's `TARGET_TEST:`), after a red revise too, never the `red-missing` leaf's and never read from `.tdd/revise-red-<k>.out`. When the step's own red FAILS, the added case is verified by verify-red, and verify-pin runs only when it reports `PASSES`. | REVISE.md is read on the reroute path, so a stale rule there re-creates the bug SKILL.md fixed. A revise report carries no `TARGET_TEST:`. |
| RV63 | `REVISE.md` has a section for the `prep` answer of `wrong-test-route.sh`.
