It tells the conductor to discard the stuck step's uncommitted work the way the exhausted split does (`git reset --hard HEAD`, then `git clean -f -- <test-path>`); to insert one step before the stuck step with `add-ledger-step.sh … before "<stuck step>"`, the new step carrying the stuck step's `⟦criterion k⟧` tag and the mark `⟦diff-checked⟧`, its text naming every case `prep` printed and saying they are updated to hold both before and after the stuck step's change with no production code edited; to commit the ledger; and to write `event.sh reroute kind=prep` naming the cases. | Every one of six bails was resumed by hand with exactly this step (PRD 029 D2, D6, D7); the insertion is the `fixture` route's and the discard the exhausted split's. |
| RV64 | The same section says the inserted step runs as any `⟦diff-checked⟧` step does, and that once it is ticked the stuck step starts again from Red; and that a `prep` answer spends no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt and writes no `revise` mark for the stuck step. | The blind `diff` verifier, not the route, judges the edit, so a reported case that is a real regression is not waved through (#3618); the budget is for a rejected product, and this is a missing step. |
| RV65 | The same section says the inserted step counts toward `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`: `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 on the insertion bails with cause `prep-owed`, naming the cases, exit 2 bails `step-add-refused`, and no bail event is written when the insertion succeeds. | The growth budget is the existing bound on a run that keeps adding steps, and a refusal must end in a classified bail. |
| RV68 | Step 5 (**Re-verify**) says that for a phase listed in `.tdd/issue-<n>-skip-verify` no re-verify launches and `comment.sh <n> verify-revised <phase>` posts `SKIPPED (switched off)`. | A skipped re-verify is never posted as a PASS; one sentence serves every skippable phase (PRD 018.2 to 018.5): the first issue to land writes it, the others check it. |
| RV69 | Step 5 (**Re-verify**) records a switched-off re-verify's skip with the full `VERI_ISSUE=<n> VERI_ACTOR=conductor VERI_STEP="$(cat .tdd/issue-<n>.md \| $S/ledger-steps.sh current-or-last-done)" $S/event.sh <event> reason=switched-off` command, and names the concrete event of each phase it can skip (`verify-diff-skipped`, `verify-green-skipped`, `verify-pin-skipped`, `verify-red-skipped`, `verify-refactor-skipped`). | A skip event the conductor cannot copy as a command is not recorded the way `metrics.sh` and the journal read it. |
| RV66 | The exhausted section says that on the plain read's exit 3 the conductor runs `$S/exhausted-outcome.sh --step-ahead '<target-test-id>' < .tdd/develop-exhausted.out`; on its exit 0 it acts on the printed line as *The `prep` answer* paragraph says (discard, insert one `⟦diff-checked⟧ ⟦added⟧` step before the stuck step, commit, the `prep-owed` and `step-add-refused` bails), writing the `reroute` event with `kind=prep at=exhausted`; on its exit 3 it bails `revise-exhausted` as today; on its exit 2 it bails `revise-exhausted` with the stderr line in the bail comment. | One insertion procedure for both paths (PRD 029 C3), and an answer naming nothing real is no answer (C4). |
| RV67 | The same section says that once the inserted step is ticked, the stuck step starts again from Red and its revise loop starts again at attempt 1. | Carrying the spent budget would send the first rejection after the restart straight back to the exhausted leaf; `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` bounds the repeats (C5). |
| RV70 | At the exhausted split and at *The `prep` answer*'s insertion (which the exhausted section's step-ahead `prep` follows), an `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2 keeps its stderr in `.tdd/add-ledger-step.err` and, with budget left, launches `adjudicate-step-add-refused` through `supervise.sh` before any `bail` event (`Read` `<base directory>/ADJUDICATE.md`); every outcome, `retry` included, then bails `step-add-refused` and `add-ledger-step.sh` is not called again. (The exhausted split and *The `prep` answer*, each at its `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 2.) | Every site of the cause. |
| RV111 | When `<phase>` is red, after the revised red's PASS and its `tests-frozen.sh record`, the conductor runs `$S/red-setup.sh record <n> < .tdd/revise-red-<k>.out` once, on the revise leaf's report, and on exit 0, 2 or 3 alike goes on, the Red not redone. | The revise leaf rewrote the step's case, so its setup count is the one that counts; `.tdd/develop-red.out` describes a case verify-red rejected (PRD 032, TY4, C14). |
| RV112 | The revise loop's **PASS (exit 0)** branch says that on a cycle step the cycle resumes through that stage's `seam-settle.sh`, then `$S/run-step.sh <n> --from` the next stage: `green-half` after a revised red, `refactor-half` after a revised green, `step-close` after a revised refactor. (The **PASS (exit 0)** branch.) | Since `run-step.sh` runs the cycle, a recovered PASS has no runner call left on its path unless the branch names one, and a bare re-call reruns the revised stage (PRD 038.2). |
| RV113 | `REVISE.md` opens with one call line: at a REJECT of red, green, refactor or diff `run-step.sh` already ran `revise.sh`; by hand (`--hand revise`, or a stop it names) go on by the sub-step its last line names. Every sub-step stays. (The paragraph under the `## Revising on a REJECT` heading.) | The prose stays the fallback (PRD 038.9 RVS15, S8). |
| RV114 | REVISE.md's verify-pin REJECT branch for a revised red passes `pin-reject-route.sh` the step's `TARGET_TEST` from `.tdd/develop-red.out`, the case the revise leaf rewrote, never a `TARGET_TEST` of the revise report. | The revise report has no `TARGET_TEST:` line, so the conductor has no target test to pass and the run bails as a system failure. |

## skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SC1 | The catalogue lists the scripts in `$S`, which is `<base directory>/scripts`, moved out of SKILL.md; `$S` is defined in SKILL.md. | A reader of the catalogue knows where the scripts live and where `$S` comes from. |
| SC2 | `$S/supervise.sh <name> <cmd> [args...]` is the leaf launcher: it spawns `<cmd>` under a heartbeat and hard-timeout watchdog, owns the `.tdd/<name>.log` progress log, and returns the captured output on stdout, with a one-line status on stderr. Exit 0 is success, 1 a leaf failure, 2 killed-stuck (hung past the heartbeat window or over the hard timeout, after its stuck-retry budget). Tunables: `VERI_HEARTBEAT` (silence window in seconds, default 180), `VERI_TIMEOUT` (hard cap in seconds, default 900), `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` (extra attempts after a killed-stuck, default 1), which is the liveness axis, independent of the conductor's verdict-axis `REVISE_BUDGET` and cost-axis `VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET`. Four more choose the model each leaf runs on, resolved through `$S/leaf-model.sh` and passed to `claude --model`: `VERI_MODEL` (every leaf), `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER` (developer leaves only: `develop-*`, `revise-*`), `VERI_MODEL_REVISE` (only the developer leaves that redo rejected work, `revise-*` and `develop-exhausted`, beating `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER` for them) and `VERI_MODEL_VERIFIER` (verifier leaves only: `verify-*`, `verify-pin` included). A role variable beats `VERI_MODEL`; with all four unset the shipped defaults are sonnet for first-try developer leaves and verifiers and opus for the redo leaves and the conductor. The value goes through verbatim, so an alias (`sonnet`, `opus[1m]`) or a full id (`claude-sonnet-5`) both work. Because leaves stream, the default heartbeat is a real silence window (a working leaf keeps it fresh, a hung one trips it), so the defaults are used and the heartbeat is not pinned to the timeout. Every leaf is launched through it, never through `run-leaf.sh` or `claude` directly. It records its own final code in `.tdd/<name>.exit` on every path, killed-stuck included, which is what makes a detached launch waitable; a killed-stuck leaf is relaunched, so one call can take more than one `VERI_TIMEOUT` of wall time, which is why the wait loops rather than naming one long timeout. | One launcher owns stuck detection, the log, the completion file and the model choice, and each knob answers one question. |
| SC3 | `$S/detach-leaf.sh <cmd> [args...]` is the detaching half of a detached launch: it runs `<cmd>` as the leader of a new session and so of a new process group, then returns at once. Every leaf launch goes through it and none uses `nohup`, for the reason *Launching a leaf* gives. Redirections go on the call as usual and the launched process inherits them. | A leaf outlives the call that started it and can be killed as a group, with no `nohup`. |
| SC4 | `$S/wait-leaf.sh <name> [max-seconds]` is the foreground half of a detached launch: it blocks polling for that leaf's completion file and exits with the code recorded in it as soon as it lands (0, 1 or 2, the launcher's own contract), or exit 3, nothing to report yet, once `max-seconds` pass with no completion file. The default is 540 s, inside Claude Code's 10-minute cap on one foreground Bash call. On a 3 it is called again, since the leaf is still running; a 3 with no leaf running means no result was ever recorded, never success. A 2 also means the supervisor died without recording a status: no completion file, and the pid in `.tdd/<name>.pid` no longer runs a supervisor for that name; it takes the killed-stuck branch like any other 2. | A wait fits one Bash call, a missing result is never read as success, and a dead supervisor is handled like a stuck leaf. |
| SC5 | `$S/run-leaf.sh <prompt-file\|-> [claude args...]` is the inner leaf invocation, always run through `supervise.sh`, never on its own. It runs `claude -p --output-format stream-json` so the leaf streams events as it works (the supervisor's heartbeat), then appends the clean final result. `VERI_LEAF_TOOLS`, when unset, passes `--tools Bash,Read,Edit,Write,Grep,Glob` (`DEFAULT_TOOLS`) to `claude`; when set non-empty, passes `--tools <value>`; when set empty, passes `--tools ""` (`grade-prose.sh` sets it empty, so its grader has no tools); a `--tools` in the caller's own arguments wins over all three. It always sets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1` in `claude`'s environment, and sets `BASH_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` to the leaf's effective timeout, the one `supervise.sh` enforces (`VERI_TIMEOUT`, else the run's `.tdd/issue-<n>-timeout`, else 900 s), unless the caller already set them. | A leaf's own Bash calls never outlive the timeout the supervisor enforces, and a stuck leaf is detectable from its stream. |
| SC6 | `$S/extract-result.sh` pulls the final result text out of a leaf's stream-json; `run-leaf.sh` uses it, and it passes plain text through unchanged. | The result is read one way, and plain output is not mangled. |
| SC7 | `$S/build-develop-prompt.sh <red\|green\|refactor\|cover\|transform\|diff\|structural\|predicate\|revise\|exhausted\|reconcile\|over-budget\|rebase\|replaces-report\|red-contradicted\|red-repair\|red-missing\|red-supersede\|expand\|beside\|cover-new\|dual-stub\|migrate\|contract-a\|contract-b> …` assembles a developer leaf's prompt from a versioned template, with these shapes: `red <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd>`; `green <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd> <test-path> <target> <fast-cmd> [<owed-cases>]`, whose optional 8th argument is the named cases still owed for deletion; `refactor <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd> <touched-files>`; `revise <phase> <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd> <reason-file>`, which reads `VERI_ISSUE`, the run's issue number, for the `check-earlier` command in the prompt and exits 2 with one stderr line when it is not a number; `red-repair <case> <report-file> <superseded>…`, the Red leaf that repairs one case the step's own Red wrote, edited in place, never creating a suite; `red-missing <spec-file> <step-text> <test-cmd> <reason-file>`, the Red leaf that adds the one case pinning the `MISSING_TEST:` behaviour the reason file names and edits no other case. It prints the prompt on stdout; exit 2 with a diagnostic and no stdout on an unknown phase or a missing template, spec or reason file, never a partial prompt. It is the mirror of `build-verify-prompt.sh` and its opposite in emphasis: the verifier builder is defined by what it cannot carry (no rationale parameter, so blindness is structural), this one by what it must carry, the same instructions on every run of a phase with only the variables changing. Every developer prompt is built with it; a phase's instructions live in its template, not in prose improvised per cycle. | A developer leaf's instructions are the same on every run of a phase, and a bad call yields no prompt instead of a partial one. |
| SC8 | `$S/build-verify-prompt.sh [--step '<STEP>'] [--fast-cmd '<CMD>'] [--step-green <sha>] <red\|green\|refactor\|pin\|plan\|rebase\|transform\|diff> <spec-file> <test-path> [target]` assembles the blind verifier prompt. `green`, `refactor`, `break`, `diff`, `transform` and every interface phase refuse to build without `--fast-cmd` (exit 2); `refactor` refuses to build without `--step-green`, the step's green tick, and every other phase refuses one (exit 2). `verify.sh` uses it; it is rarely called directly. | The verifier's prompt is built in one place, from blind inputs only; a verifier that runs tests is never built without the fast-tier command it runs (#4444), and verify-refactor never without the green tick that bounds its files (#4544). |
| SC9 | `$S/verify.sh [--step '<STEP>'] [--fast-cmd '<CMD>'] [--step-green <sha>] --branch-base <sha> <red\|green\|refactor\|pin\|plan> <spec-file> <test-path> [target] \| --branch-base <sha> diff <spec-file> <step-base-sha> \| --branch-base <sha> rebase <spec-file> <onto-sha> <pre-rebase-sha>` is the verify verb: build the blind prompt, launch the verifier leaf through the supervisor, parse the verdict. `--step-green` is required for `refactor` and refused for every other phase (exit 2, from the prompt builder). It prints the verifier's reason on stdout; exit 0 is PASS, 1 REJECT, 2 malformed or killed-stuck. The `pin` phase is the already-satisfied check: the verifier confirms the test passes now, fails under a behaviour-removing mutation it applies and then reverts itself, and is behaviour-level, held to the same trivial, over-coupled and too-complex bar as `red`. The `rebase` phase launches no leaf when a tracked file holds an uncommitted change or `git status` fails (exit 2, the dirty paths or the failed `git status` named on stderr; an untracked file does not count), nor when a commit of `<onto-sha>..HEAD` adds a conflict marker line (exit 1, each commit and file named on stdout). | A verdict is a launched, supervised, parsed answer, a pin is proved by a mutation, and a rebase verifier judges only the committed HEAD the push ships. |
| SC10 | `$S/refactor-owed.sh append\|applied\|not-done\|render\|list <file> …` handles the structural leftovers a step's refactor leaf reports. `append <file> '<STEP>' [<bounds>]` reads the report on stdin. `applied <file> '<transform>' <sha> '<subject>'` records a candidate as applied, `<subject>` being the applying commit's subject, `"$(git log -1 --format=%s)"`, which `render` prints beside the sha so the row stays readable after a rebase rewrites it. `not-done <file> '<candidate>' <reason>` records a candidate the pass did not apply, which `render` shows under `### Not done`. `render <file>` prints the `## Refactor owed` section. `list <file>` prints each structural transform once. Exit 0 done, 1 a write failed, 2 a missing argument or an unknown verb, 3 `render` or `list` found nothing. | Structural leftovers are recorded, rendered and listed one way, and a rebase does not orphan a row. |
| SC11 | `$S/mutation-parity.sh <pairs-table>` proves each row of a `⟦replaces-case⟧` pairs table by applying its mutation. Exit 0 all proven, exit 1 a REJECT, exit 2 a bail. A row whose mutation no kept case catches prints `MISSING_TEST: <old>: no kept case fails when its mutation is applied` on stderr; saved to a reason file, that line routes through `wrong-test-route.sh` as `missing`. | A case may be replaced only when a kept case fails under the same mutation, and the gap routes to a Red that closes it. |
| SC12 | `$S/run-verdict.sh [--step "<STEP>"] [--fast-cmd '<cmd>'] [--step-green <sha>] --branch-base <sha> <red\|green\|refactor\|pin\|plan> <spec-file> <test-path> [target]` is the verdict launcher: `verify.sh`'s own call, argument for argument, reached as a command rather than as a shell string. `supervise.sh verdict-<phase>` runs it; it passes every argument through untouched and `exec`s the verify verb, so the step title travels in argv and its exit code is `verify.sh`'s, `refactor` without `--step-green` included (exit 2). | The step title reaches the verifier as data and the exit code is the verify verb's, unchanged. |
| SC13 | `$S/parse-verdict.sh` extracts `PASS` or `REJECT` plus the reason from a leaf's output; `verify.sh` uses it. | The verdict is read from output one way. |
| SC14 | `$S/tick-issue-criterion.sh <n> "<substring>"` ticks a criterion checkbox in the issue body, the human-facing mirror. | The issue mirrors the committed ledger's progress. |
| SC15 | `$S/next-unchecked.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` prints the first unchecked ledger step's description; exit 3 when none remain, the loop-done and nothing-to-resume signal. | The loop knows its next step and when it is done. |
| SC16 | `VERI_STEP=<step> $S/wrong-test-route.sh --spec <spec-file> <green-report> <red-test-id>` routes a green report's `WRONG_TEST:`, or a report carrying `MISSING_TEST:` (a verify-green REJECT reason, a mutation-parity stderr saved to a file, a revise-green report answering `IMPOSSIBLE`). Exit 0 prints one of: `fixture <path> <name>` (a helper, fixture or shared setup function defined in the test file `<path>` that is not a test case); `missing <behaviour>` (a behaviour no test pins; the report file is the `red-missing` brief); `red <case>` (an existing case other than the step's own that a `CONTRADICTS:` line names; a case named only in the `WRONG_TEST:` text is evidence, so a report that cites one routes to `repair`; a wrong-test report is exempt from the `SUITE:` line as `green-wrong-test` (`WRONG_TEST:` with `SUPERSEDED:`) or `green-wrong-test-contradicts` (`WRONG_TEST:` with `CONTRADICTS:`), a revise of green as `revise-wrong-test` or `revise-wrong-test-contradicts`); `repair <case> <superseded>…` (a case the step's own Red wrote, per its red record or as `<red-test-id>`, with the older cases listed on `SUPERSEDED:` lines); `supersede <case> <case>…` (two or more older cases named on `SUPERSEDED:` lines, which one `red-supersede` Red updates together). Exit 3 prints `revise`; exit 2 is bad arguments, or a bare case name (one with no `::`) that is defined in more than one test file or in none. The `red`, `repair` and `supersede` cases it prints carry the test file that defines them, as `<file>::<case>`. `missing <behaviour>` is answered on exit 0 until the step's `"kind": "missing"` `reroute` events in `.tdd/events.jsonl` (with `VERI_STEP`) reach `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` (default 3); then `missing-exhausted <behaviour>` is printed on exit 1. The other four routes (`red`, `repair`, `supersede`, `fixture`) stay free only for the step's first reroute of any kind: a later one answers `revise` (exit 3) and spends one `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt. A `fixture` route is taken only when the helper is defined in the step's own test file, the one before `::` in a `<file>::<case>` id; an id with no `::` (the Go and JS names) falls back to searching every test file. | A missing test is rerouted to the leaf that adds it until its own budget is spent; the other wrong-test routes are free once per step, and every later one is paid for from the verdict budget. |
| SC17 | `$S/pin-fast-tier.sh '<fast-cmd>'` runs the fast tier over the tree a pin would commit, from the worktree; the fast command is its one argument. On failure it writes the command's output to `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`. Exit 0 the command passed, 1 it failed (output in the reason file), 2 no command given. | A pin commits only a tree the fast tier passes on, and a failure leaves its reason where the revise leaf reads it. |
| SC18 | `$S/tick-ledger.sh [--refactor-pending \| --phase <phase> \| --phase-owed <phase> \| --unpinnable <reason-file> \| --already-holds <reason-file>] [--case <id>] .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<substring>"` checks a step off in the committed ledger, in place, staged and committed together with that step's green code. Ticking a `⟦diff-checked⟧` step also re-records, via `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-earlier`, the earlier frozen cases its diff changed, and exit 2 can then follow the write of the ledger (the re-record failed). Exit 3 if no unchecked step matched the substring: the file is left untouched, so a green commit that ticked nothing says so instead of reading as done. `--refactor-pending` appends ` ⟂ refactor pending` to that same line in the same write, so the green commit records the step as green but not yet tidied; the green PASS ticks with that flag, and the pin path ticks without it, since a pinned step runs no refactor loop and a mark left there would never be cleared. A `⟦replaces-case⟧` step's deletion green after a pin PASS is a Green: it ticks with the flag and runs Refactor. `--phase <phase>` instead appends ` ⟂ <phase> done` to the first open step listing `⟦phases: …⟧` that matches and leaves its checkbox `[ ]`; once every listed phase is marked the step reads as done to `ledger-steps.sh` and `next-unchecked.sh` skips it. Exit 2, ledger byte-identical, for a phase the step does not list or one that is not its next unmarked phase, for a step listing `⟦phases: …⟧` ticked without `--phase`, and for `--phase` given with `--refactor-pending`. `--phase-owed <phase>` appends ` ⟂ <phase> owed` instead, under the same refusals; an owed phase ends the step, so it and every phase after it are never handed out again (see the exhausted-contract paragraph in `<base directory>/PHASED.md`). `--unpinnable <reason-file>` retires the matched step instead of ticking it as green: when the reason file (a verify-pin REJECT, `.tdd/verify-pin.reason`) says the mutation `stays green` or has a before-state-green clause (a clause naming the before-state and green or pass, with no `fail`, `red`, `not`, `n't` or `after` in it), it ticks the step `[x]` with ` ⟂ retired unpinnable` appended, in the same write, and appends one `unpinnable-step` event (`step=`, `reason=` from its `REASON:` line) to `.tdd/events.jsonl`, never a `revise` event, and only when the case named by `--case <id>` passes (run through the repo's one-test-file command (the `veri: one test file:` line of `CLAUDE.md`, as `detect-test-command.sh --tier "one test file"` prints it) given the case id's file path, the part before `::`, or as `make test TESTS="<id>"` when the repo declares none). Exit 0 retired; exit 1 the reason is any other rejection, no `--case` is named, or the named case ran and fails, ledger and events untouched and stderr naming the step and the cause; exit 2 no answer (bad arguments, an unreadable ledger, or the event not written) or the named case never ran (under the one-test-file command: its file path does not exist, or its output says no tests were collected or ran; stderr names the case), ledger and events untouched; exit 3 as above when no unchecked step matched. `$S/ledger-steps.sh next-phase < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` prints the first phased step's next unmarked phase, exit 3 when none is left. The cycle loop runs a phased step through both, per `<base directory>/PHASED.md`. | The ledger records green, tidied, phase-done and phase-owed in one committed file, a tick that matched nothing is never silent, and a failed re-record after a `⟦diff-checked⟧` tick is never read as a clean tick. |
| SC19 | `$S/reorder-ledger-steps.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md "<step>" before\|after "<anchor>"` is the one sanctioned way to change the order of unchecked steps; it rewrites the ledger only and never commits, so the operator commits the ledger on its own in a `plan:` commit; running it is an operator's decision, never the conductor's own. | Step order changes only by a visible, committed operator act, and the script never makes that commit. |
| SC20 | `$S/next-pending-refactor.sh < .tdd/issue-<n>.md` prints the first ticked step whose refactor is unverified: the description of the first `- [x]` step carrying the ` ⟂ refactor pending` mark, with the mark stripped, so what comes back is the same text `next-unchecked.sh` hands the loop and the tick wrappers match on. Exit 0 when it printed one; exit 3 with empty stdout when no step carries the mark, every green step having been tidied, so a resume has no refactor loop to re-enter. | A resume can tell green from finished and re-enters only a refactor that is owed. |
| SC21 | `$S/ledger-growth.sh <plan-ledger-file> <ledger-file> \| plan-sha \| record-existing <ledger-file> \| record-rewrite <pre-rewrite-ledger-file> <ledger-file> \| reopen <ledger-file> <needle> <change>` is called by the conductor at each ledger commit and compares the ledger with the plan commit's. The plan commit, `<plan-sha>`, is the newest `plan: scope` or `plan: rewrite` commit, so after a verify-plan REJECT the rewrite is the baseline; `git log --format='%H %s' \| $S/ledger-growth.sh plan-sha` prints its sha, exit 2 when the log holds none. Each step line the plan did not hold must match, by equal step text, a record `add-ledger-step.sh` wrote (a line of the tracked `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the ledger, committed with it so it survives a resume in a fresh worktree, or a `step-added` event of actor `add-ledger-step` in `events.jsonl`), and must carry `⟦added⟧` unless it is a `⟦suite-repair⟧`, `⟦ci-repair⟧` or `--planned` insertion. The `⟦added⟧` lines never exceed ⌈`VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` (default 50) × P ÷ 100⌉, P the steps in `<plan-ledger-file>`. Ticks, ` ⟂ refactor pending` and phase marks on planned lines are not growth. Exit 0 when every line passes; exit 1, naming each failing line on stderr, when one lacks its record or `⟦added⟧` or the cap is passed; exit 2 on a missing argument, an unreadable ledger or plan file, a malformed `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`, or a set `VERI_STEP_GROWTH`, which is retired (stderr names `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`). `record-existing` is the one-off migration for a ledger that grew before the records existed: it writes `.tdd/issue-<n>-added.md` beside the ledger, one `- [ ] <step>` line, as `add-ledger-step.sh` writes it, for each step line the plan commit did not hold; `⟦suite-repair⟧`, `⟦ci-repair⟧` and `⟦added⟧` lines keep their marks. | SC37 | `$S/solvable-issues.sh [--repo <owner/name>] [--not-running]` is the selection layer: it prints the numbers of the issues veri may take now (open, labelled `ready-for-agent`, every issue under their `## Blocked by` heading closed, and not claimed by an open PR saying `Closes #<n>`, a still-draft PR on the issue's own run branch `tdd/<n>-…` claiming nothing, ADR-0036, while that PR once readied, not a draft, claims it), one per line, in four bands: the `bug`s labelled `priority` first, then the other issues labelled `priority`, then the `bug`s, then the rest, each band ascending by number. A bug labelled `low-risk` counts as the rest, and `priority` outranks `low-risk`. An issue also inherits the band of every open issue that names it under `## Blocked by`, transitively, and takes the better of its own and the inherited band: blocking a `priority` bug puts it in the first band, blocking another `priority` issue in the second; blocking a plain `bug` lifts nothing. Each printed issue whose band was inherited gets one stderr line `#<blocker> inherits <band> from #<dependent>`. The band only reorders that set, never enlarges it: the four conditions run first, so a `priority` issue, or a blocker of one, that is blocked, claimed or not opted in is still not printed. `VERI_PRIORITY_LABEL` names the `priority` label (default `priority`; set-but-empty is exit 2, like `VERI_LABEL`) and `bug` is GitHub's own. Set but empty, or any other value, is exit 2, with a stderr line naming `VERI_GH_DRIVER` and the value, checked at call time. Operations with no GraphQL route (issue edit and create, `gh_rest_list`, `gh_json` with no gh args) use REST under every value, and `VERI_GH_JSON` takes precedence over it. | SC38 | `$S/wait-ci.sh <pr-url> [slice-seconds] [total-seconds] [head-sha]` waits one bounded slice for a PR's checks, then is called again: *4. Draft PR*'s CI wait. It reads the check runs and commit statuses of the PR's head; given `<head-sha>`, a PR still reporting another head reads as pending; a `<head-sha>` that is not a full 40-hex sha exits 2 with one stderr line. `<slice-seconds>` (default and cap 540) bounds this call; `<total-seconds>` (cap 600) bounds the whole wait, and when it is not given it is twice the mean of the last 5 completed CI runs, capped at 600; 540 when fewer than 5 exist or `gh` cannot answer; the slice length when reading them fails otherwise. The deadline does not run only while every pending check is `queued` (none `in_progress`), or the PR reports another head: it is recorded in `.tdd/ci-deadline-<sha>`, which later calls inherit, by the first call where that is not so (a check in progress, no check yet, or only a commit status pending). The first queued sighting is recorded in `.tdd/ci-queued-<sha>`. A draft PR records no deadline. Exit 0 every check completed `SUCCESS`, `NEUTRAL` or `SKIPPED`, not all `SKIPPED`. Exit 1 a completed check concluded anything else, or a commit status is `failure` or `error`; the failed check names go on stdout, comma-separated. Exit 2 no answer: bad arguments, a read of the checks that fails and is still failing when the deadline passes (retried with backoff until it; a read refused with a rate-limit message is waited out until GitHub's reset when that falls before the deadline and, for every read (the PR's head, check runs or commit status), before the slice end (this call's start plus `<slice-seconds>`); past the slice end that read sleeps only the time left to it and the call exits 3; the call exits 2 at once, naming the rate limit and its reset time, when the reset falls at or past the deadline; a 4xx other than 403 or 429 exits 2 at once), a PR with no checks (or only skipped ones) that conflicts with its base branch (stderr says `conflicts with its base branch`), a check still pending past the recorded deadline, an unreadable deadline file, or checks that never started, still queued more than `VERI_CI_QUEUE_WAIT` seconds after the first call that saw them queued. Exit 3 still pending, no check yet, every one `SKIPPED`, or a mismatched head, when the slice runs out. With `VERI_CI_ON_PR=0`, a PR whose head has no check runs and no commit statuses exits 0, since there is nothing to wait for; checks that do exist are still waited on and read as above. A `VERI_CI_ON_PR`, or a `veri: ci on pr:` line, other than `0` or `1` is an exit 2. Tunables: `VERI_POLL` poll interval (default 10 s), `VERI_CI_RETRY_BACKOFF` (`1` by default doubles the retry sleep from `VERI_POLL`, at most 60 s; `0` keeps it flat), `VERI_CI_QUEUE_WAIT` (default 3600 s), `VERI_CI_ON_PR` (unset means checks are expected, unless the target repository's `CLAUDE.md` `veri: ci on pr: 0|1` line or the detection of workflows says none will come; the variable wins over the line). | CI is waited on in bounded slices, a deadline that starts only when work starts, and pending, skipped and mismatched are never read as green. | SC610 | The catalogue lists `$S/reroute.sh <n> <report>`: `wrong-test-route.sh`'s `red`, `repair`, `supersede` and `missing` answers through the rerouted red leaf, verify-red and the frozen-test re-record, its stops (`prep`, `later`, `fixture`, `supersede-diff`, a pin report, the two exhaustions), `route=revise` as exit 1, and its exit codes 0, 1, 2 and 3. (The `reroute.sh` entry, after `revise.sh`'s.) | A reader of the catalogue finds the one-call reroute and where it stops (PRD 038.10). |
| SC611 | The catalogue lists `$S/run-pr.sh <n>`: the Draft PR step's five stages in series, its `title` and `body` seams and the stacked stop, that it writes neither the title nor the body and launches no leaf, and its exit codes 0, 1, 2 and 3. | A reader of the catalogue finds the one-call Draft PR step and where it stops (PRD 038.11). |
| SC612 | The catalogue lists `$S/go-red-step.sh <n>`: the probe wording it reads from the open `⟦go-red⟧` step, `mutation-probe.sh` on the named case with the `veri: one test file:` command into `.tdd/issue-<n>-mutations.md`, the restore check, `step-tick.sh <n>`, and its exit codes 0, 1 (only the probe seeing no red, or the probe's `<old>` text absent from `<file>` before any probe) and 2 (no step, no probe, no `veri: one test file:` command, a probe 2, the file not restored, any `step-tick.sh` exit but 0), never through the Red leaf; the `step-tick.sh`, `run-step.sh` and `tick-ledger.sh` entries say how each handles a `⟦go-red⟧` step, the last that a record of another probe on the same file does not tick it. (The `go-red-step.sh` entry, after `step-tick.sh`'s.) | A reader of the catalogue finds the go-red stage and where its exits lead (#4443). |
| QU6b | `SCRIPTS.md`'s `dispatch.sh` entry names the `queueing` line as advice printed while dispatch keeps watching, never a closing line. (`skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`, the `dispatch.sh` entry.) | Docs drift blocks. |
| RW7 | `SCRIPTS.md`, where it describes `timed-test-run.sh`, names the `test-stretch` event. Its `test-ceiling.sh` entry names the `idle` lines, and its `dispatch.sh` entry names the `stretched` line as advice printed while dispatch keeps watching, never a closing line. (`skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`.) | Docs drift blocks. |
| LO13 | `SCRIPTS.md`'s `test-ceiling.sh` entry says its exit 0 removes `VERI_TEST_LOAD_FILE`. Its `dispatch.sh` entry names the `t-lowered` line as advice printed while dispatch keeps watching, never a closing line. (`skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`, those two entries.) | Docs drift blocks. |
| FN11 | `SCRIPTS.md`'s `dispatch.sh` and `role-state.sh` entries each name the test budget entry it writes and `VERI_CONDUCTOR_TEST_WORKERS`, `VERI_TEST_BUDGET_DIR` and `VERI_TEST_BUDGET_TTL`. (`skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`, those two entries.) | Docs drift blocks (PRD 036.5, #4453). |
| SC613 | The catalogue lists `$S/skill-edit-half.sh <n> [--change "<change>"]` (a ⟦skill-edit⟧ step's clean path in one call, `skill-edit-step.sh` detached, then `step-tick.sh`), with its stops and exit codes 0, 1, 2 and 3. | A reader of the catalogue finds the one-call skill-edit stage (PRD 038.4). |
| SC614 | The `run-step.sh` entry says that on `skill-edit-half.sh`'s exit 2, and no other stage's, the line before the `seam=skill-edit-half code=2` line is `out=.tdd/skill-edit-<n>.out refusal=yes|no`, `yes` when that file holds a line starting with one of `skill-edit-step.sh`'s refusals and `no` otherwise, a missing file included. (The `run-step.sh` entry, its `⟦skill-edit⟧` clause.) | The conductor tells a refusal bail from a no-verdict bail without reading the out file blind, and no other stage's 2 points it at a skill-edit file (SKH18). |

(`skills/tdd/SCRIPTS.md`, the `reject-breakdown.sh` entry.) | Docs drift blocks. |
| SC623 | The `rebase-onto-default.sh` entry states `--resolved <onto-sha>`: it fetches and rebases nothing, re-records the run's branch base as `<onto-sha>`, exit 0 with stdout the sha the record held before (nothing when there was none), writes no stage line, and exits 2 with the record untouched on a missing argument, a non-hex one, one HEAD does not contain, or a record it cannot write. | DRAFT-PR.md's conflict path and its reset path both call it (#4553). |
| SC626 | The catalogue has an entry for `$S/near-duplicates.sh <commit>` saying it prints one line per pair, `<file>:<first>-<last> <file>:<first>-<last>`, the added block first, for each run of 3 or more consecutive lines added between `<commit>^` and `HEAD` that repeats, ignoring whitespace and identifier names, elsewhere in the non-test files `<commit>` touched; that a line holding no letter and no digit does not count toward the 3; and that it exits 0 with pairs printed, 3 with none, 2 on a missing or unresolvable `<commit>`. (The entry for `near-duplicates.sh`.) | A caller reads the output format and the exit codes from the catalogue, and branches on 3 as "no pair", never as a failure. |
| SC627 | The `build-develop-prompt.sh` entry gives the `refactor` phase an optional last argument `<pairs-file>`, after `<touched-files>`, and says its lines fill the prompt's near-duplicates section, which reads `none` when the argument is absent or the file empty, and that an unreadable pairs file exits 2 with no stdout. (The `build-develop-prompt.sh` entry, its `refactor` phase.) | A caller learns from the catalogue how to hand the refactor leaf its pairs, and that a missing pairs file is no pair while an unreadable one is no answer. |
| SC628 | The `refactor-half.sh` entry lists, among its calls, `near-duplicates.sh <step-green>` writing `.tdd/develop-refactor.pairs`, passed as `build-develop-prompt.sh refactor`'s last argument, and says its exit 3 (no pair) still launches the leaf while its exit 2 stops `refactor-half.sh` with exit 2 before any leaf is launched. (The `refactor-half.sh` entry, its list of calls.) | The script's calls and stops are read from its entry, and a detector that could not answer is never read as no duplication. |
| SC624 | The pin-recheck entry says that a pin whose old text is gone from its recorded file but occurs exactly once in another file of the same directory is replayed in that file, and that one whose old text is in no file of the directory, or in more than one, prints `not checked: <file> (<reason>)`, exits 1 and leaves no `.tdd/pin-recheck-ok` receipt; and that one whose old text is also absent from its file at `<base-sha>`, and which no commit of `<base-sha>..HEAD` added to or removed from that file, prints `stale: <record file> <file>` instead, with no effect on the exit code, `<base-sha>` read only when the old text is absent at `HEAD` and no single file of the directory holds it. | A pin a refactor moved to another file was printed as `needs a look` and counted as checked, disarming it for the run's lifetime (a foreign run). A pin an earlier issue broke charged every later step that touched its file, whose revise leaf could only answer IMPOSSIBLE (#4644); text a step's green commit added and its refactor removed is that step's own breakage, not stale. |

## skills/tdd/SKILL-EDIT.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SE1 | The conductor first reads the issue body into `.tdd/issue-<n>-body`, with `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/<n> -q .body > .tdd/issue-<n>-body`. (Paragraph 1.) | The runner reads the approved requirements diff from a file, and the file must exist before it runs. |
| SE2 | Then it runs `$S/skill-edit-step.sh` on the issue body, `.tdd/issue-<n>-body`, the step's unit, and then every pin file the step names, in that order. (Paragraph 2.) | The runner takes the diff, the one unit the step edits, and the pin files it must keep green. |
| SE3 | On exit 0 or 3, the conductor ticks the step and commits the ticked ledger. (Paragraph 3.) | Both mean the unit is signed and committed; the ledger records the step done. |
| SE4 | On exit 1, it bails with `cause=revise-exhausted`, as an exhausted revise. (Paragraph 4.) | The author's budget ran out against the grader: a process failure, classified like any exhausted revise. |
| SE5 | On exit 2 (a bad argument, budget or heading, or no answer), it bails at once and never relaunches the runner or spends `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` on it. When the runner printed a refusal message, the bail is a process failure with cause `skill-edit-refused`, naming the refusal; a bare exit 2 with no refusal message bails as a system failure with `cause=no-verdict`. (Paragraph 5.) | A refusal such as a held requirement-row id is bad input, not a plumbing failure; labelling it `system`/`no-verdict` sent an operator to resume instead of fixing the issue (#3471 run). |
| SE6 | A killed or stuck author leaf is still retried under `VERI_STUCK_RETRIES` by the `supervise.sh` launch inside the runner, before that exit 2 reaches the conductor. (Paragraph 6.) | Liveness is handled where the leaf is launched, so the conductor's exit-2 bail is not a missing retry. |
| SE7 | A resumed run redoes an unfinished skill-edit step from its start, with its budget reset. (Paragraph 7.) | A step takes minutes, and a partial-step resume costs more than a redo (K9). |
| SE8 | For a skill-edit step whose ledger line carries `⟦added⟧`, the conductor runs `$S/skill-edit-step.sh` with `--added` and the step's text, before the issue body and the unit. (Paragraph 8.) | An added step brings no approved diff of its own, so the runner is told it is one. |
| SE9 | The refusal holds only for the rest: an added step bringing rows new to an already-passing unit's list is committed with its regrade and no author (exit 3, ticked); one whose text names a prose change runs with `--change`; and any other whose grade passes before any author ran, or that has nothing left to commit, gets exit 2 and is bailed, never ticked. (Paragraph 9.) | An added step that changes nothing must not be ticked as if it had done its work. |
| SE10 | The step's own text never reaches the author or the grader. (Paragraph 10.) | The grader is blind, and the author works from the requirements and the FAIL reasons only (K5). |
| SE11 | When a skill-edit step's text names a change to the prose, a sentence to delete or reword, the conductor runs `$S/skill-edit-step.sh` with `--change` and that change, so an author runs even on a unit that already grades PASS. (Paragraph 11.) | A prose change the list does not forbid would otherwise end at K4 with nothing edited. |
| SE12 | A skill-edit step's unit may be a heading unit, `<file>#<heading>` (a section of a file that a `REQUIREMENTS.md` claims with a `## <file>#<heading>` heading). The issue's `## Requirements` then names it under `### <file>#<heading>`, never under the whole-file `### <file>`, and the conductor passes that `<file>#<heading>` as the step's unit. (Paragraph 12.) | Rows about a claimed section filed under the whole-file unit are graded against a view that no longer shows the section and FAIL (PRD 024.7b MV6, decision O2); a step must name the unit its rows are filed under. |
| SE13 | `SKILL-EDIT.md` opens with one call line: run `$S/run-step.sh <n>` (on a step whose text names a prose change, `$S/skill-edit-half.sh <n> --change "<change>"` in its place), again on exit 3, and on 1 or 2 go on by the paragraph its last line names; the line after it reads `skill-edit-half by hand ends $S/step-tick.sh <n>`. Every paragraph stays. | The prose stays the fallback (SKH14, SKH37, SKH47, SKH48). |
| SE14 | The tick paragraph names the ledger commit's subject for a ⟦skill-edit⟧ tick: `chore: skill-edit — <step summary> (#<n>)`. | Runs have committed five different subjects and no skill file names one (SKH28, S20). |
| SE15 | Paragraph 5 says `$S/run-step.sh` prints `out=.tdd/skill-edit-<n>.out refusal=yes|no` on the line before its `seam=skill-edit-half code=2` line, and that `refusal=yes` means the runner printed a refusal message, held in that file. (Paragraph 5.) | The bail's cause follows from that one line, not from re-reading the runner's output (SKH18). |

## skills/tdd/PREFLIGHT-FAILURES.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PF20 | A red baseline on the rebased tip resets the branch to `<pre-rebase-sha>` before the run bails. | A bailed resume must leave the branch on the commit it resumed from, not on a tip no suite proved green. |
| PF21 | If any preflight check fails, the baseline is poisoned: the run reports which check failed and stops. This is a degenerate bail — no commits, nothing to revert or push — and a red, dirty or unauthenticated baseline never gets a cycle. | Preflight exists so no leaf is spent on a run that cannot succeed. |
| PF30 | `leaf-launchable.sh`'s exit 2 is described as the case `README.md`'s *Launching the conductor* describes (#187), naming that README section rather than a section above. | *Launching the conductor* moved from SKILL.md to README.md (#1785); the pointer must follow it. |
| PF32 | A preflight refusal calls `$S/preflight-refused.sh <n> <check> <cause>`, passing the refusing check's own stderr line as `<cause>` (the `issue-worktree.sh` refusal's `already on origin` message is the covering case), so the `preflight-refused` event and the one issue comment name the failing check and why it refused, visible to `status.sh` and to a reader of the issue, not only in the conductor's log. | A refused run otherwise leaves no trace outside the conductor's own output, and a comment naming only the check sends the operator digging through the log (#3763). |
| PF36 | An exit 2 of `resume-clean.sh` (an uncommitted `.tdd/` append it could not re-apply, kept in `kept-tdd-appends.diff`, or a failed git command) bails as a system failure with cause `no-verdict`. (Step 3, the handed-back run.) | The discard failed, so no answer was obtained; it is not the user's doing. |
| CY117 | When the resume's baseline is red only in suites that are red on the branch head but green on the default branch, preflight does not refuse, whatever the previous bail's cause (`revise-exhausted` included). The conductor inserts one `⟦suite-repair⟧` step naming those suites before the first unchecked step, commits the ledger and runs that step first, except that when those suites are red only because a unit's `GRADED` signature is stale and that unit has a pending `⟦skill-edit⟧` step, it inserts no repair step and that step runs first. A suite also red on the default branch still refuses as above. | Otherwise a bail is terminal: every resume stops at preflight before the repair it needs (#2206); and a repair step placed ahead of the `⟦skill-edit⟧` step that would regrade the unit bails the run `revise-impossible` (#2884). |
| PF40 | An exit 2 from `leaf-launchable.sh` stops the run, and its stderr diagnostic is reported verbatim. veri never unsets `CLAUDECODE` or any other launch guard itself. (Step 2.) | A suite, worktree, spec and public `Resuming` comment are wasted if no leaf can launch (#187). |
| PF41 | A failing `gh api user` (the REST route) or a `claude auth status` that is not `"loggedIn": true` is a preflight failure: report it and stop. (Step 2.) | An expired session makes every `claude -p` leaf fail at launch, which has no bail classification. |
| PF42 | A red baseline stops the run as a preflight failure, whatever state the caller's checkout is in; a red output naming no test target, or any target still red alone, stops the run. (Step 3.) | A cycle must not start on a red baseline, but a load flake is no defect of the baseline, and refusing it can deadlock the fix for that very flake. |
| PF43 | A red full tier stops the run; a red output naming no test target, or any target still red alone, stops the run. (Step 3.) | A load flake in the full tier is no defect of the baseline (#2912), but a red one is. |
| PF44 | A leaf-timeout exit 2 stops the run with its diagnostic reported verbatim. (Step 7.) | Every Bash call is a fresh shell, so an export does not survive. Prose that said otherwise was fixed in #954. |
| PF45 | A `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT` that is set-but-empty or not a whole number ≥ 0, or a `VERI_STEP_GROWTH` set to any value (it is retired), is an exit-2 preflight stop, reporting the value verbatim and naming `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`. (Step 5.) | A bad value caught here costs nothing. Caught later it surfaces mid-cycle as a refused plan insertion. |
| PF46 | An exit 2 from `step-budget.sh` is a preflight stop, reporting its stderr diagnostic verbatim. (Step 5.) | A budget that cannot be read is a poisoned baseline. |
| PF47 | An exit 2 from `leaf-model.sh` stops the run with the diagnostic reported. (Step 6.) | Each of `VERI_MODEL_DEVELOPER`, `VERI_MODEL_REVISE` and `VERI_MODEL_VERIFIER` is read only for its own name, so skipping a name lets its bad value through. |
| PF48 | Any model variable value containing whitespace other than an empty or whitespace-only one — internal, or a leading or trailing space (`sonnet 5`, `" sonnet"`, `"sonnet "`) — is exit 2: a preflight stop reporting the diagnostic verbatim. (Step 6.) | A weaker rule would stop runs on blank variables (#1485); a value with whitespace cannot be a model name. |
| PF49 | Exit 2 of the resume rebase bails as a system failure (`no-verdict`). (Step 3.) | The three exits are different outcomes and must not be collapsed. |
| PF50 | If the caller's checkout is dirty, the run is a preflight refusal: the conductor calls `$S/preflight-refused.sh <n> 1 <cause>`, `<cause>` naming the blocking paths, and stops without a bail, so the issue gets no `bailed` label and stays queued. (Check 1.) | The conductor still reads the issue and the default branch from the caller's checkout, and a dirty one means something else is mid-work in it. Found before the claim, it is no fault of the issue's run, and a `bailed` label needed a hand unlabel (#4505). |
| PF51 | Exit 2 of `refactor-budget.sh 0` (`VERI_REFACTOR_BUDGET` not a whole number ≥ 0) stops the run, reporting its stderr verbatim. (Step 5.) | Catching it here is what lets the refactor loop treat exit 2 as impossible. |
| PF52 | Exit 2 of `refactor-budget.sh --issue 0` is the same stop as PF51. (Step 5.) | The structural pass's budget gets the same early check. |
| PF53 | `step-budget.sh`'s exit 2 means `VERI_STEP_BUDGET` or `VERI_RUN_BUDGET` is not a non-negative decimal number: a preflight stop before any leaf is launched. (Step 5.) | Spells out which two money budgets PF11's call checks, and what a pass prints. |
| PF54 | `worktree-setup.sh` exit 1 (`<cmd>` exited non-zero) **bails** with cause `setup-failed`, a system failure, naming the command; exit 2 (no answer: bad arguments or an unreadable file) is a preflight-class stop. (Step 3.) | A nested package's dependencies are otherwise never installed in a run worktree, so a run's suite passes while the merge gate fails on a missing module (gs-marketplace); a worktree re-created for an existing branch gets its ledger back but not its dependencies, so no ledger or bail condition may skip it. |
| PF55 | A `VERI_MISSING_REROUTE_BUDGET` failing that check (false) is an exit-2 preflight stop that reports the value verbatim. (Step 5.) | Same early check as PF45. |
| PF56 | `tests-frozen.sh rerecord-rebased` exit 1 bails `needs-user`, a process failure, its bail comment quoting each `<case> (<owning step>)` line and saying the branch changed a frozen earlier case for one of two reasons, named both: the default branch also changed it, or the branch's own work (a refactor, say) changed it with the default branch untouched; or telling the two apart. Exit 2 bails `no-verdict`, a system failure (see `<base directory>/BAIL.md`). (Step 3.) | A rebase moves upstream's edits under earlier steps' records; refreshing only the cases the branch left alone lets upstream move without ever erasing a finding about the branch. The refusal has two causes, and a message naming one sends the reader to look for a conflict that is not there. |
| PF57 | `detach-command.sh wait` answers 1 when it finished red with its code on stdout (a red suite: stop and report) and 2 when it died recording nothing, except that a 2 while `$S/stop-requested.sh` reports a pending `now` is the stop being carried out, a stop and not a failure (see PF61). (Step 3, the baseline.) | The conductor branches on these four answers, and the same 2 must not bail a run the operator only paused. |
| PF58 | Any `VERI_SKIP_VERIFY` word that is not a phase this release can skip, or is `plan`, exits 2 before the baseline, naming it. (Step 5.) | A bad list must stop the run before it spends anything. |
| PF61 | A `detach-command.sh wait` that answers 2 during a requested stop (`$S/stop-requested.sh` exits 0 and prints `now`) is not a preflight failure and not a poisoned baseline: the conductor goes to *Stopping* (`Read` and run `<base directory>/STOPPING.md`), records a `stop` event and no `bail`, and posts no preflight refusal; a wait that exits 2 while no `now` stop is pending is still read as the failure it reports. | `--mode now` makes `wait` return 2 at once, and reading it as a dead baseline would bail a paused run. |
| PF73 | PREFLIGHT-FAILURES.md has an entry for `requirements-preflight.sh`'s exit 2 (a Requirements row that clashes with a held ID and needs `<ID><reword>`, or a skill-edit unit with no `### <path>` heading), naming its cause, the `preflight-refused` event and issue comment it gets like every other preflight refusal, and the fix: correct the issue's `## Requirements`. | A refusal with no entry loses its event and comment, and the human sees no remedy (PF59). |
| PF67 | `### Step 2` says a stop at `2 ready-check.sh` (a failing refusing row, exit 1, or no answer, exit 2) is a preflight refusal: the conductor calls `$S/preflight-refused.sh <n> ready-check.sh <cause>`, `<cause>` being the first refusing row's `fail` line, or on exit 2 `ready-check.sh`'s own stderr line; the fix is the one each `fail` row names. | A refusal with no entry loses its event and comment (PF59, PF73); `preflight.py` writes no event itself. |
| PF74 | PREFLIGHT-FAILURES.md's entry for `requirements-preflight.sh`'s exit 2 says a `## Requirements` row whose Requirement and Why cells the unit's section already holds under another ID is not a clash, so a resumed run whose branch renumbered a row is not refused for the body's old ID; only a held ID with different text, whose text the unit does not hold, is. | #4472's resume was refused for CM310 after its branch had landed the row as CM312; the entry named only the clash, so the human saw no hint the row was already done. |

## skills/tdd/ADJUDICATE.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| AJ1 | The file lists the eligible causes (in 019.1: `ci-timeout`) and, for each one, the route tokens it allows (in 019.1: none). It states that `killed-stuck`, `no-verdict`, `killed-external` and preflight refusals are never adjudicated. | The eligibility list has one home. |
| AJ2 | The file states the procedure: check `VERI_ADJUDICATE_BUDGET`, build the prompt with `build-adjudicate-prompt.sh`, launch the `adjudicate` leaf through `supervise.sh`, parse with `parse-adjudication.sh`, record `adjudicated`, then act on `retry`, `route` or `bail`. A parse exit 2 is a bail with the original cause and class. | The conductor follows one rule at every site. |
| AJ3 | The file states that the adjudicator writes no file and no ledger line, never relabels the 2 as a 1, and that a bail after adjudication keeps its original classification. It cites ADR-0035. | Guards stay rigid. |
| AJ4 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `ci-conflict`: `rebase` ``. (Eligible causes.) | The cause and its one route have one home. |
| AJ5 | *Procedure* step 3 removes `.tdd/adjudicate-<cause>.exit` and `.tdd/adjudicate-<cause>.pid` before the leaf is launched, as *Launching a leaf* in `SKILL.md` does. (Procedure, step 3.) | A second adjudication of one cause by one conductor otherwise ends its wait on the first one's completion file. |
| AJ6 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `pr-ready-failed`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | The cause has one home. |
| AJ7 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `report-missing`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause. |
| AJ8 | *Procedure* step 4's exit-`0` bullet says the step that returned 2 is run once more only where the cause's own site states a retry (`ci-timeout`, `ci-conflict`, `pr-ready-failed`); for any other cause exit `0` is a bail with the original cause and class, the `adjudicated` event still saying `outcome=retry`. (Procedure, step 4.) | `parse-adjudication.sh` accepts `retry` for every cause, so the generic bullet would rerun a command known to return the same 2 and spend budget doing it (spec D1, deferred). |
| AJ9 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `step-add-refused`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause. |
| AJ10 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `replaces-report-failed`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause. |
| AJ11 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `unreplaced-cases`: `replaces-report` ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause and its route. |
| AJ22 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `reconcile-failed`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause. |
| AJ23 | The *Eligible causes* list holds the bullet `` - `criterion-unreadable`: none ``. (Eligible causes.) | One home for the cause. |

## skills/tdd/PUBLISHED.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| PB1 | `PUBLISHED.md` lists the published files: the journal, the metrics file, and `report-owed.json` marked optional (it may be absent or old). For each it states its path and how the path is resolved, which script writes it, and when. | A reader needs one place, and today the journal's path is only in a spec. |
| PB2 | It states the journal's path as `$VERI_HOME/runs/<owner>-<repo>/<issue>.jsonl` with `VERI_HOME` defaulting to `~/.veri`, and that a run with no issue or no `origin` remote writes none. | A reader globbed one directory too shallow for lack of this sentence. |
| PB3 | It states the journal's line shape: one JSON object per line, `ts` and `awake` numbers, every other value a string, the common keys (`issue`, `actor`, `phase`, `step`, `event`, `attempt`, `skill_commit`) in that order, and that the events and their keys are those `JOURNAL.md` lists. | The page must be enough to write a parser. |
| PB4 | It states the rules: events and keys are only ever added, never removed or renamed; no line carries a schema version; a reader ignores an event or key it does not know and survives a line that is not valid JSON. | Append-only is a promise on both sides: veri adds, the reader tolerates. |
| PB5 | It names what is private and may change without notice: `report-cache.json`, `.tdd/status-last`, `conductor.pid`, the worktree's `.tdd/events.jsonl` path, and every other file under `.tdd/`. | A reader that knows what not to read does not break when it changes. |
| PB6 | It says nothing of any particular reader: it names no particular reader program, no reader's path and no rule for one reader alone. | Veri never knows which program reads it (PRD 014). |
| PB9 | It says the journal holds only the lines whose writer gave them an issue, a line with an empty `issue` going only to the worktree's `.tdd/events.jsonl`, and lists the events that today never reach the journal, each with the script that writes it: `comment`, `diff-leaf-deviation`, `grading`, `pin-retired`, `prose-owed-waived`, `rebase-refresh-retry`, `suite-flake`, and `step-added` as `add-ledger-step.sh` writes it. | A reader that waits in the journal for an event its writer never sends there waits forever; the page must not promise every event `JOURNAL.md` lists. |
| PB10 | `PUBLISHED.md` states that each key of the journal has a format stated in `JOURNAL.md`; that a list of values may gain values, as the journal may gain events and keys; and that a reader treats a value it does not know as it treats a key it does not know. | Append-only applies to values once a list is closed, and the reader must be told to tolerate a new one. |
| PB11 | `PUBLISHED.md` states, for `report-owed.json`: its path, `.tdd/report-owed.json` in the main working tree; that only `report.sh --store-owed`, run by `/vreport`, writes it, replacing the whole file at once; that a missing file means no answer is stored yet; that an entry is current only while its `updatedAt` equals the PRD issue's `updatedAt` on GitHub, and stale otherwise; and that its `computedAt` says when it was computed. | Optional means absent or old, and the reader must be able to tell which. |
| PB12 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states: its path, `<issue>.metrics.json` beside the issue's journal; that `event.sh` rewrites the whole file at once, and only after an `exit`, `step-done`, `bail`, `done` or `stage-end` event of that issue, so it may be one phase behind the journal; that it covers every run of the issue; that a missing file means no such event has been written yet; and that a failure to write it never stops a run. | A reader must know how fresh the file is and that its absence is not an error. |
| PB13 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states each top-level key (`run`, `phases`, `steps`, `conductor`, `total`) and each key of an entry under it, with its type and whether it may be `null`; that every figure is a count or a sum and none is a rate; that `null` means not known and is never zero; and that keys are only ever added and the file carries no version key. | The page must be enough to write a parser, and a reader that sums must tell nothing from not known. |
| PB16 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states that each phase entry holds `verified_steps`, `first_try_pass` and `revise_cycles`, each a number, `0` when none: the steps with a PASS or REJECT verdict of that phase, those whose first such verdict was PASS, and the `revise` events of that phase; and that a journal written before `revise` was recorded shows `revise_cycles` `0`, the event's absence, not a run with no revise. | A reader divides these counts itself, and must not read an old log's zero as a perfect developer. |
| PB17 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states the top-level key `leaves`: keyed by leaf kind, the leaf's name less a trailing `-<digits>`, each entry holding the keys of a phase entry less `verified_steps`, `first_try_pass` and `revise_cycles`; that a line older than the leaf's name being recorded counts under the leaf its `launch` pairs it with, and one pairing with none under `unnamed`; and that its attempts, wall seconds and cost sum to the phase entries' own. | A reader must know what a leaf kind is and that `leaves` and `phases` are two views of one total. |
| PB18 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states the top-level key `runs`, a list in order, and each entry's keys with type and nullability: `index`, `ended`, `cause`, `wall_seconds`, `awake_seconds`, `sessions`, `leaves`, `cost_usd`; and states in one sentence that a run ends at a `done`, `bail` or `stop` line and the next line opens the next run, so a restart with no ending line continues the run. | A reader must know what one run is before comparing them. |
| PB20 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states the top-level key `stages`, keyed by stage name, each entry's keys `runs`, `ok`, `failed`, `no_answer`, `nothing`, `unended` and `wall_seconds` as numbers; that `ok`, `failed`, `no_answer` and `nothing` count `stage-end` lines with `status` 0, 1, 2 and 3; that `unended` counts a start with no end, as a killed stage; and that a stage absent from `stages` was not recorded, never that it ran zero times. | A reader must tell a stage that never ran from one veri did not mark, and a killed stage from a passed one. |
| PB41 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states that each phase entry and each step entry holds `rejects`, an object whose keys `caught`, `flipped`, `overturned`, `unresolved`, `pending` and `untracked` are each a number, never `null`, summing to the entry's `reject`. | A reader that checks or sums the counts must know they are complete and never missing. |
| PB42 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` names the six outcomes of a REJECT, one sentence each: caught, a later PASS of the same step and phase judged a different tree; flipped, it judged the same tree; overturned, a `revise-impossible` bail of that step came first; unresolved, no later PASS and a bail after it; pending, no later PASS and no bail; untracked, the PASS or the REJECT carries no tree, as every line written before the tree was recorded. | A reader may rely only on what the page states, and a flip or an old line must not read as a catch. |
| PB43 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states that each step entry carries the step's latest text and `former`, the list of its earlier texts in order, empty when it was never reworded, and that a rewording made outside the ledger rewrite (a hand edit) shows as a new step. | A reader matching steps across files must know which text is current and when identity is lost. |
| PB44 | In its section on the metrics file, `PUBLISHED.md` states `conductor.by_phase`, keyed by leaf kind, stage, `between` and `untimed`, each entry's `turns`, `output_tokens` and `cost_usd` with types and nullability; that a turn is counted under the innermost leaf or stage window holding its time; that its `turns` sum to `conductor.turns`, which counts each session's turns, the more of those its closing record counts and those the log shows, or the shown ones for a session with no closing record; that `by_phase` is `null` when no conductor log was read; and that its costs are estimates, not billed amounts. | A reader must not take an estimate for a bill, nor `null` for zero. |

## skills/tdd/TIDY-FIRST.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| TFS1 | The file says that when `$S/tidy-first.sh < .tdd/develop-green.out` exits 0 the conductor launches no verify-green, discards the step's uncommitted work as the `prep` answer does (`git reset --hard HEAD`, then `git clean -f -- <test-path>`), inserts the printed step with `$S/add-ledger-step.sh .tdd/issue-<n>.md before "<stuck step>"` carrying the stuck step's `⟦criterion k⟧`, commits the ledger, and writes `event.sh tidy-first anchor=<stuck step> refactoring=<refactoring>` beside the `step-added` event; exit 3 goes on as the green report would without it. | The insertion is 029.2's and 029.3's, unchanged (PRD 032, TI3). |
| TFS2 | The file says the inserted step runs as any `⟦diff-checked⟧` step does (`develop-diff`, then the blind `diff` verifier), and once it is ticked the stuck step starts again from Red, its revise loop at attempt 1. | `verify-diff` runs the suites and rejects a step adding a test (PRD 032, TI4, C7). |
| TFS3 | The file says a `TIDY_FIRST:` answer spends no `REVISE_BUDGET` attempt and writes no `revise` mark for the stuck step. | The budget is for a rejected product (PRD 032, TI5). |
| TFS4 | The file says the inserted step carries `⟦added⟧` and counts toward `VERI_STEP_GROWTH_PERCENT`: `add-ledger-step.sh` exit 1 bails with cause `tidy-first-owed`, naming the refactoring, and exit 2 bails `step-add-refused`. | Plan growth is bounded on its existing axis (PRD 032, TI6, C8). |
| TFS5 | The file says `tidy-first.sh` exit 1 is a verify-green REJECT whose reason is its stderr line, spending `REVISE_BUDGET`, with no verifier launched, and its exit 2 (no answer could be read) bails as a system failure with cause `no-verdict`. | The leaf answered wrongly, so the methodology spoke; a 2 is the plumbing, never a verdict (PRD 032, TI7). |

## skills/tdd/SMELLS.md

| ID | Requirement | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SM12 | The file lists smells, each with a name, a threshold stated as a number wherever a diff can show one (function length in lines, nesting depth, parameter count), so that two readers of the same diff agree whether it is met, and one or more named refactorings that answer it. | One standard for the developer and the verifier, which today is "up to standard" (PRD 032, D2; SM1). |
| SM13 | The file states, each as a rule of its own: search for an existing helper before adding one; do not reverse a refactor made earlier in the same run; in test code, expected values stay literal and are never computed by the code under test. | The transform guards and test-code rules a refactor is held to (PRD 032, SM2). |
| SM14 | The file is at most 60 lines. | It is included in every refactor and structural prompt (PRD 032, SM10). |

(`tests/py/README.md`, *The pytest leg*, *The idle window*.) | A per-case timeout cannot fire once no case runs, and legs hung for up to 4 h 48 min after their cases ran, holding their callers, their worker share and, for a full run, the machine's full-run lock (2026-11-17). |
